# Parity ledger

Maps each dcrd package to its dcroxide crate and tracks parity status against
the pinned upstream **master `3360ae4d`** (version `2.2.0-pre`, wire protocol
12, JSON-RPC 8.3.0). The target is dcrd v2.2, which upstream has not yet
released: until it is tagged the pin follows master, moving at upstream
milestones and on the owner's word. `3360ae4d` moved up from `382ff4dd`, which
moved up from `6f6cf21b`, which moved up from `b9634e01`, which moved up from
`036b7090`, which moved up from `29f17894`, which in turn moved up from
`452c1a6c`, the dcrd 2.2 campaign target, and originally from the
`release-v2.1.5` tag. README carried `29f17894` until the `b9634e01` move, two
pins behind, so the two files named different specifications. The interop
harness (`dcroxide-testutil` `DCRD_PARITY_COMMIT`) and CI's `DCRD_COMMIT` also
stayed at `29f17894` through the `036b7090` and `b9634e01` moves, so the
harness refused a dcrd built at the actual pin. Both now name `3360ae4d`, and
`dcroxide-testutil`'s `the_dcrd_pin_matches_ci_and_the_oracle` fails if either
drifts from `tools/oracle/go.mod` or `tools/fullblockgen/go.mod`: from a
header comment, from any dcrd module pseudo-version their `require` lines name
(those decide what the tools link; until 2026-09-25 only the oracle's comment
was read, and until the `6f6cf21b` move nothing read
`tools/fullblockgen/go.mod`, which sat at `036b7090` through the `b9634e01`
move), or if a `replace` redirects a dcrd module.

**Oracle pin.** Most frozen vectors were generated by exporters running inside
dcrd's own packages: most at `release-v2.1.5`, some at `452c1a6c` or a later
pin, as each replay's header says. Most of those exporters were scratch code
that is not in the tree; ADR-0001's 2026-10-26 addendum on frozen batteries
sets when one is rebuilt. The vectors are taken to hold at each later pin
unless a move's delta reaches them, and the first such case was found when the
pin moved to `036b7090`: `a38c0195` edits
`blockchain/fullblocktests/generate.go`, changing the expected rejection of two
instances from `ErrImmatureSpend` to `ErrMissingTxOut`, so
`fullblock_vectors.txt` was regenerated at `036b7090` by `tools/fullblockgen`,
and again at `6f6cf21b`, where three test commits change four expectations and
rename one block (below). That battery seeds its block timestamps from the
wall clock, so a regeneration rewrites every block hash in the file and the
replay reads the recorded `now` row to compensate — a full-file diff there is
expected and carries no information; the row counts and the expectations are
what to review. At `6f6cf21b` `processheaders_vectors.txt` was regenerated
too and `ticketspend_vectors.txt` added, both from scratch exporters at that
commit, and rows elsewhere have been re-expected by hand where a delta reached
them. Some replays record how (netsync's, seed_tor's and srvadmit's do); the
RPC replays record only `rpchandlers4`'s native case 35, not the nine rows
`940d6e4`, `e06812e` and `e362edf` edited, and the config and flags replays not
the 68 rows `e06812e` and `e362edf` edited (ADR-0001's 2026-10-26 addendum on
frozen batteries and ADR-0007's of the same date). Up to `6f6cf21b` the rest
were not regenerated: several are expensive to reproduce. A battery whose
exporter was scratch code is now regenerated, not re-expected by hand, once a
move changes the package it was dumped in, its exporter first rebuilt as a kept
tool that reproduces the frozen file at the commit it was dumped at (ADR-0001's
2026-10-26 addendum on frozen batteries, which reads the rule as counting from
the `3360ae4d` move and adds two triggers of its own).

At `382ff4dd` eight files were regenerated or added by generators kept in the
tree. Each runs inside a dcrd checkout's own package as an overlay and writes
nothing into the checkout (its `run.sh` or header says how), and each writes
its committed files back byte for byte when run again at `382ff4dd`.
`mixpool_vectors.txt` is regenerated by `tools/mixpoolgen` under a clock
overlay of `mixing/mixpool`; with the script the file held before, it
reproduces that file byte for byte at `6f6cf21b`. `txstake_vectors.txt` and
`review_agenda_vectors.txt` are regenerated by `tools/mempoolvectors` in
`internal/mempool`; a variant with the earlier scripts, not kept, reproduces
byte for byte at `6f6cf21b` the earlier copies of all three files the tool
writes, and the tool gives back `txpool_vectors.txt` unchanged.
`utxoview_vectors.txt` is regenerated by `tools/utxoviewgen` in
`internal/blockchain`, which computes every row but the base view and the two
blocks it reads from the file; a copy adapted to three older call signatures,
not kept, reproduces the previous file byte for byte at `6f6cf21b`.
`processblock_vectors.txt`, `invalidate_vectors.txt` and `reorg_vectors.txt`
are regenerated by `tools/chainopsgen` in `internal/blockchain`, which reads
each file's seeds, blocks, operations and universe keys and writes every other
field from dcrd. It gives every input the fraud proof of the output it spends,
which dcrd checks on every block it attaches since `22fd2431`; the files had
carried 500000000, 0 and 1 on every input that spends an output, and, given
that constant proof instead, it reproduces them byte for byte at `6f6cf21b`.
`disapproval_vectors.txt` is new, from `tools/disapprovalgen`, which plays
dcrd's disapproval scenarios in `internal/blockchain`; the scenarios exist only
from `2b896ffb` on. The lesson is that "the checkout did not move" was never
the right test — run `tools/pinbump` and check whether the delta touches an
exporter's own source.

At `3360ae4d` no vector file changed and no kept generator was run again: the
delta changes only `internal/rpcserver`, which none of them runs in, and
`tools/profilergen`, whose package main links it, writes only what
`profileServer.Start` returns, which the delta leaves alone. The scratch
exporter behind the 17 RPC vector files dumped at `release-v2.1.5` did run in
`internal/rpcserver`; under ADR-0001's 2026-10-26 addendum on frozen batteries
those files are due to be regenerated by a rebuilt, kept exporter, which is
not yet built.

`tools/pinbump` lists changed upstream `_test.go` files in a section of
their own, resolved to crates like the source files, and reports a moved
file under both its old and its new package. A vector the port copied from
a listed test file is ported or regenerated in the same bump. Until
2026-09-23 it dropped every test file, so the `29f17894..b9634e01` reviews
never listed `addrmgr/network_test.go`,
`blockchain/stake/internal/tickettreap/immutable_test.go`,
`blockchain/stake/staketx_test.go` or `peer/peer_test.go`. The source half
of each change (`cc5e1d63`, `036b7090`, `5dd4ca59`, `589dd4c5`) was listed
and is assessed below; `staketx_test.go`'s zero-commitment revocation
vector (`9a2e88b9…`) is covered by the differential generator rather than
copied, and the other three test files have not been checked for vectors
this port should carry.

A bump also checks the Go release dcrd's `contrib/docker/Dockerfile` builds
with (golang 1.26.2 at `b9634e01`, unchanged at `6f6cf21b`, `382ff4dd` and
`3360ae4d`). That release, to the patch, is the port's reference Go for every
behaviour dcrd takes from Go's standard library (ADR-0001's 2026-10-26
addendum on the reference Go), and CI's `GO_VERSION` names it, so a bump that
moves the Dockerfile's release moves that line in the same commit. The
Go-JSON error texts in the `dcrjson` row follow that toolchain, not the
module's `go` line: from Go 1.27, `GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2` is on by default and
serves `encoding/json`'s v1 API from the v2 implementation, which words the
scanner's syntax errors differently. When the release image moves to 1.27 or
later, the batteries whose texts it changes are regenerated under it, each by
an exporter first rebuilt as a kept tool that reproduces its frozen file
(ADR-0001's 2026-10-26 addendum on frozen batteries; none is rebuilt yet for
`dcrjson_vectors.txt`, `rpchandlers13_vectors.txt` or
`seed_tor_vectors.txt`), and the port either follows the new texts or records
the divergence. The float digits are not the exposure: the 90 tie rows and
both trigger values of
`crates/dcroxide-dcrjson/tests/review_go_text_parity.rs` come out the same
under go1.26.2 and Go 1.27.1 (ADR-0007's 2026-10-26 addendum).

A bump that changes `config.go` also re-extracts `tools/helpgen`'s config
struct and `loadConfig` defaults literal from the new target and regenerates
the help vectors (`help_vector.txt`, `help_vector_140.txt`): both are verbatim
copies of the pin's `config.go`, so the vectors are only as current as the
copy. `tools/pinbump` does not name helpgen, and it lists `config.go` among
the unmapped paths under `.`, since no mapping row resolves dcrd's top-level
files. The `6f6cf21b` move was one (`3fe61c02`); the `382ff4dd` and `3360ae4d`
moves were not, since `config.go` is the same at all three pins.

**`382ff4dd..3360ae4d` (1 commit).** The target is now dcrd v2.2, which
upstream has not yet released, so the pin follows master until v2.2 is
tagged, moving at upstream milestones and on the owner's word. `3360ae4d` was
master's head on 2026-10-25, with no `release-v2.2` branch yet. `tools/pinbump`
resolves the delta to `dcroxide-rpc`, `dcroxide-dcrjson` and
`dcroxide-rpctypes`, through one source file,
`internal/rpcserver/rpcserver.go`, and lists
`internal/rpcserver/rpcserverhandlers_test.go` among the changed test files.

- **No change needed:** `3360ae4d` has `handleSendRawTransaction` read as
  `false` an `AllowHighFees` that an explicit JSON `null` leaves nil
  (`rpcserver.go:4403-4406`), where `382ff4dd` dereferenced it and panicked,
  and adds a test of the case. The port has always read it as `false`, which
  PARITY.md recorded as a deliberate divergence from dcrd; that entry is
  retired, and `sendrawtransaction_null_allowhighfees_reads_as_false`
  (`crates/dcroxide-rpc/tests/review_rpc_handler_parity.rs`) now pins a match.

Vectors. The oracle and `tools/fullblockgen` link `3360ae4d`. No module was
tagged in the delta and no linked module's directory changed, so the split
stays as at `382ff4dd`: every pseudo-pinned module moves to the new commit,
nine in the oracle and seven in `fullblockgen`, and each one's files, as the
module proxy serves them, are byte-identical at both commits. No vector file
changes (the *Oracle pin* paragraph above). Every dcrd file outside
`internal/rpcserver` is byte-identical at both pins.

Citations. The undated citations of `rpcserver.go` lines past the change, 62
of them in the code, its tests, QUIRKS.md and this file, move four lines on to
point at `3360ae4d`; each cited line reads the same at both pins. Citations
dated by a commit beside them or by a pin-move section, and those into files
the delta did not change, are left as they are.

**`6f6cf21b..382ff4dd` (39 commits).** `tools/pinbump` resolves the delta to
`dcroxide-blockchain` (five source files), `dcroxide-mempool`,
`dcroxide-mining` (a doc comment), `dcroxide-mixing`, `dcroxide-netsync`,
`dcroxide-stake` (module files only) and `dcroxide-wire` (copyright lines
only). It lists changed test files under the blockchain, mempool, mixing,
stake and wire crates, and leaves `go.mod` and `server.go` unmapped under `.`
and the release notes under `docs/release-notes`. `go.mod` moves dcrd's own
`wire` requirement to v1.8.0, and `server.go`'s two hunks wire the mempool's
two new lookups, `WinningTicketsByHash` and `MisbehavingMixSpend`, which are
ported with it. All 39 are ported or accounted for. Thirty-eight of them are
also in dcrd v2.1.7, cut from the release branch the same day, so the pin
carries every behaviour change of that release; the thirty-ninth adds its
release notes.

Mempool:

- **Ported:** `a9b79d1b` refuses a vote that claims a height more than five
  above the tip (`ErrFutureVote`) and moves the height policy, with a new
  old-vote text, to right after the stake validation height gate, ahead of
  the revocation gate and every later check (`mempool.go:1104-1134`,
  `:1492-1502`). `6816d9de` then refuses a vote on a block the chain does
  not know (`ErrVoteBlockUnknown`) or whose claimed height is not the
  block's (`ErrVoteBlockHeight`, `mempool.go:1136-1164`). `023c42ff`
  refuses, after the scripts are checked, a recent vote whose ticket is not
  one of the voted block's winners (`ErrIneligibleTicketVote`,
  `:1166-1203`, `:1849-1861`), from `LotteryDataForBlock`. The pool's chain
  seam now distinguishes an unknown block and answers a block's winners. A
  vote on a block known only by its header fails that lookup with a plain
  error, as in dcrd. Pinned by the unit tests of the three checks,
  `tests/vote_header_only.rs` in the node crate, and the regenerated
  batteries.
- **Ported:** `6444f057` moves the vote metadata into a tracker, and
  `d54099ad` prunes it once the voted block is more than ten blocks below
  the height the stake pruning runs at (`mempool.go:385-396`, called from
  `:2270`). Vote transactions are still pruned by their acceptance height,
  so a late-accepted vote can outlive its metadata, and a second vote by the
  same ticket on the same block can then enter up to the five-vote cap, as
  in dcrd. Pinned by the unit tests of the tracker, among them dcrd's
  `TestVoteTrackerPruneOldVotesBoundary` from `cd33677e`, and one showing
  that a stake pruning whose agenda query fails prunes no metadata either.
- **Ported:** `3c8a1734` refuses a non-mix transaction that spends an
  output the mixing observer has struck twice (`ErrMixingMisbehavior`,
  `mempool.go:1555-1561`), through a second method on the mixpool probe,
  `MixpoolProbe::misbehaving_mix_spend`. Pinned by `tests/mixpool_gate.rs`
  (for regular spends, votes and ticket purchases alike),
  `tests/review_rule_error_text.rs` and the kind-name unit test. The node's
  probe answers through the mixpool's `Pool::misbehaving_tx`, whose answers
  the mixpool vectors pin; the delegation itself is not driven by a test,
  since raising strikes in the daemon's mixpool takes a whole mix session.
- **No change needed:** `84961241` keeps the whole chain error in
  `wrapChainRuleError`. The port's chain errors are typed, so the
  description and kind are unchanged.
- **Test only, vectors regenerated:** `b443b0b3`, `5a6f22f0`, `93544c30`,
  `f367cdff`, `7388fcd6` and `cd33677e`. `txstake_vectors.txt` (394 rows,
  eight sections) and `review_agenda_vectors.txt` (61 rows) were regenerated
  by `tools/mempoolvectors`, an exporter inside dcrd's `internal/mempool` at
  `382ff4dd`, with block-add, best-hash, winner and per-step metadata rows
  and an `oldvotes` policy row. The treasury section, the auto-revocations
  section and `txpool_vectors.txt` (90 rows) come out byte for byte as
  before; a variant of the tool with the earlier vote and subsidy-split
  scripts, not kept, reproduced the earlier files byte for byte at
  `6f6cf21b`.
- **No change needed:** `e8f47dc6` modernizes a Go test helper.

Mixing:

- **Ported:** `6b65e0d1` drops the 64-pair-request limit on a key exchange's
  seen list (`checkAcceptKE`, `mixpool.go:1669`), which only the wire now
  bounds, at 512.
- **Ported:** `ffb42adf` refuses a second, distinct key exchange by one
  identity in a session (`mixpool.go:1703-1722`), with dcrd's plain error
  for a session that records a message `RemoveMessage` dropped.
- **Ported:** `38811b0b` caps an identity at 896 messages over its pair
  request's life, checked on the direct path, before a key exchange is
  orphaned, and when orphans are reconsidered (`mixpool.go:46-51`, `:1333`,
  `:1641`, `:1724`).
- **Ported:** `3ce5cc41` and `874f6772` refuse a key exchange more than 20
  minutes past its epoch, or one whose epoch is not a whole minute
  (`mixpool.go:1685-1692`). `mixpool_vectors.txt` was regenerated by
  `tools/mixpoolgen` with re-signed inputs at whole-minute epochs inside
  dcrd's window: 1,107 rows where it had 1,027, with rows for epochs too
  late, far past and off the minute, a second key exchange in a session, a
  session recording a removed message and 65 seen pair requests. The
  nanosecond edge of the late gate, which a capture at a whole second cannot
  show, is pinned by `key_exchanges_late_for_their_epoch_are_refused`
  (`tests/mixpool_acceptance_rules.rs`).
- **Ported, not observable:** `70485d9e` finds an epoch's active peers in
  one pass over the pool.
- **Not ported:** `1d78ba45`, `6aa6b468`, `83beff12` and `55892be8` change
  the wallet-side `mixclient`, and so do the client halves of `874f6772` and
  `6b65e0d1`.
- **No change needed:** `46fc30ad` prepares `mixing/v0.7.5`.

UTXO view safeguards:

- **Ported:** `9112e4e6` leaves a cache entry already marked spent as it is
  when a commit spends it (`utxocache.go:340-355`). Pinned by
  `TestSpendEntry`'s and `TestCommit`'s rows for it
  (`tests/utxo_cache_commit.rs`).
- **Ported, not observable:** `248a9d40` tests an output's spendability
  before building a ticket submission output's minimal outputs
  (`utxoviewpoint.go:92-162`), with the single-output helper folded into the
  range add as in dcrd.
- **Ported:** `96a55a74` looks the grandfathered stake spends up by height,
  adds each one's output to the view before the input fetch on every
  connect (`check_connect_block` and the view's block connect alike), and
  marks it spent again once a block's trees are disconnected
  (`utxoviewpoint.go:613-631`, `:974-1004`), so the startup catch-up, a
  re-attach of an already validated block and a detach across mainnet
  blocks 1106817 to 1107195 and testnet3 block 1980161 work. Pinned through
  a lookup the tests supply (`utxoview::tests`): no network the vectors use
  has such a height, so the add in `check_connect_block` and the chain's
  call sites are not reached with one by any test.
- **Ported:** `2115b56b` decides once per fetch which in-block spends may
  bypass the cache (`utxoviewpoint.go:1106-1198`): an output a later
  transaction of the regular tree spends is eligible only when the view
  holds none of its creating transaction's regular outputs, and one set on
  the view, filled by the fetch, drives the connect, the disconnect and
  `checkTransactionsAndConnect`. Pinned by the regenerated
  `utxoview_vectors.txt`, `tests/reincluded_zero_conf_spend.rs` (dcrd's s4
  shape), `tests/disapproval_round_trips.rs` and `utxoview::tests`.
- **Ported:** `e60cedc5` turns a view input that is missing into a panic with
  dcrd's text, in both trees (`utxoviewpoint.go:274-280`, `:342-348`), where
  the port returned `ErrUtxoBackendCorruption` in place of dcrd's
  `AssertError`, and the commit's zero-confirmation check panics with dcrd's
  text (`utxocache.go:594-601`). The daemon's `Critical failure`
  classification keeps its `AssertError` exception for
  `checkTreasurybaseUniqueHeight`'s. A block connected without input checks,
  an ancestor of the assumed-valid block or any block of a bulk import
  (`addblock`), that spends an output the chain does not have now stops the
  process, as in dcrd. Pinned by `utxoview::tests` and
  `tests/utxo_cache_commit.rs`.
- **Ported:** `22fd2431` panics when the view holds an input already spent
  (`utxoviewpoint.go:281-284`, `:349-352`), asserts every attached block's
  view before it is connected (`utxoviewpoint.go:633-917`,
  `chain.go:1236-1238`), with the grandfathered same-block stake spends its
  one exception, and panics at commit when the cache holds an unspent entry
  for a zero-confirmation spend (`utxocache.go:602-606`). The assertions run
  unconditionally, as in dcrd; their cost was not measured. A block
  connected without input checks, an ancestor of the assumed-valid block or
  any block of a bulk import, whose inputs' fraud proofs name other outputs,
  now stops the process, as in dcrd. Pinned by `utxoview::tests`,
  `tests/attach_view_assertion.rs` and every replay that attaches blocks,
  dcrd's full block battery among them.
- **Test only, ported:** `6b5bc9d0` adds `TestCommit`'s two panicking
  zero-confirmation rows and the row for the zero-confirmation spends a
  commit drops without touching the cache (`tests/utxo_cache_commit.rs`).
- **Test only, ported:** `81a671bd`, `2b896ffb` and `016d46af` are replayed
  from the chains `tools/disapprovalgen` dumps by running dcrd's scenarios
  inside its own package at `382ff4dd`, in both flush modes
  (`tests/disapproval_vectors.rs`). The port's chain has hidden test hooks for
  it: one turns the cache flushes off, as `disableFlush` does on dcrd's test
  cache, and the other logs where the cache flushed, to compare with the
  flushes the generator records from dcrd's cache. dcrd's switch also skips
  the shutdown flush, which goes through `MaybeFlush` (`utxocache.go:1073`);
  the port's does not, since its shutdown flush writes the block index in the
  same transaction, and no scenario shuts the chain down.

The rest:

- **Ported:** `17dd9f45` sets the sync height to the new sync peer's claim,
  but no lower than the best header, each time the initial header sync
  selects one (`manager.go:679-682`). A peer claiming `math.MaxInt32` had
  held the header sync until restart. `netsync_vectors.txt` is unchanged.
- **No change needed:** `efa17f60` and `7e3dd854` prepare `wire/v1.8.0` and
  `blockchain/stake/v5.0.3`, changing copyright lines and module files. At
  `382ff4dd` both directories are identical to the tags, which the oracle now
  links. `382ff4dd` adds the v2.1.7 release notes. Every entry in them is at
  or before `6f6cf21b` or in this delta except four commits made on the
  release branch alone: its version bump, and the three that move its root
  `go.mod` to the backported `wire`, `blockchain/stake` and `mixing` releases,
  whose source `382ff4dd` builds from its own tree.

Vectors. The oracle and `tools/fullblockgen` link `382ff4dd`. `wire` and
`blockchain/stake/v5` move from pseudo-versions to their new tags, and every
other pseudo-pinned module moves to the new commit on the same base, so the
oracle pins nine modules by pseudo-version and `fullblockgen` seven, where
they pinned eleven and eight. `fullblock_vectors.txt` is not regenerated:
`blockchain/fullblocktests` and the code it links are unchanged.
Regenerated, each by a generator kept in the tree that writes the committed
file back byte for byte at `382ff4dd` (the *Oracle pin* paragraph above
says how each reproduces the previous file at `6f6cf21b`):
`mixpool_vectors.txt` by `tools/mixpoolgen`; `txstake_vectors.txt` and
`review_agenda_vectors.txt` by `tools/mempoolvectors`;
`utxoview_vectors.txt` by `tools/utxoviewgen`, where three rows change from
state 7 to 3, as `2115b56b`'s eligibility rule determines (derived in the
replay's header); and `processblock_vectors.txt`, `invalidate_vectors.txt`
and `reorg_vectors.txt` by `tools/chainopsgen`, which gives every input the
fraud proof of the output it spends, since `22fd2431`'s assertion panics on
the constant proof the files had carried, and changes no verdict, status,
count or presence flag. New: `disapproval_vectors.txt` (170 blocks, in both
flush modes), from `tools/disapprovalgen`. Every other vector file is
unchanged and replays as before. Every dcrd package outside the delta is
byte-identical at both pins.

Deviations the port keeps at `382ff4dd`:

- The port keeps its cap on the key exchanges one identity holds at once,
  raised from 32 to 64 (`MAX_KES_PER_IDENTITY`), and its one-per-type rule
  for un-orphaned mix messages, now beside dcrd's 896-message limit and one
  key exchange per session. dcrd's limit is checked first wherever it
  applies, so the answers match there (the *Mixpool key exchanges per
  identity* and *Un-orphaned mix messages* rows below).
- The session walk in `accept_ke` answers for the hash smallest in internal
  byte order (the reverse of the displayed hex) where dcrd's map order
  decides which of two errors it reports; no peer can make a session hold
  both.
- The pool's `header_by_hash` answers an `Option`, since dcrd's real lookup
  fails only with `ErrUnknownBlock`, so the port has no counterpart of
  `checkVoteBlock`'s branch for other errors, which the chain cannot reach.
- The chain's test hook that turns the cache flushes off leaves the shutdown
  flush on (above).
- The UTXO cache does not mark an output fresh over a missing slot when a
  disconnect in the view marked it spent, where dcrd's `addEntry` does (the
  *UTXO cache entry for an output added again after a disconnect* row
  below).
- The UTXO view's and cache's new panics carry dcrd's texts but do not reach
  the log, where dcrd's `panicf` writes them at the critical level first
  (the *Panic policy* entry below).
- The debug lines the delta adds or makes reachable in the mixing pool are
  not reproduced, since the port's mixing pool logs nothing: the one for an
  orphan held back at the per-identity message limit when orphans are
  reconsidered (`mixpool.go:1642`), and the observer's for a transaction
  that spends a struck output (`mixing/mixpool/observer.go:487-488`), which
  `3c8a1734` makes reachable from the mempool.  The vote metadata pruning's
  (`mempool.go:391-392`) is logged, with the rest of the mempool's lines
  (*The mempool's and the template generator's lines below info* under
  Accepted).

**`b9634e01..6f6cf21b` (31 commits).** `tools/pinbump` resolves the delta to
`dcroxide-blockchain` (13 source files, among them the new
`internal/blockchain/agendas.go`), `dcroxide-wire`, `dcroxide-txscript` and
`dcroxide-mixing`. It lists changed test files under the blockchain and wire
crates, and `internal/rpcserver/rpcserverhandlers_test.go` under the three RPC
crates, and leaves `config.go` and `doc.go` unmapped under `.`. All 31 are
ported or accounted for. Most of the consensus half is two upstream series:
sixteen commits that separate agendas from deployments, and three that
validate a block's header before its data.

The agenda series:

- **Not observable:** `bf449ef6` takes the chain lock in two more exported
  methods (`MaxTreasuryExpenditure`, `treasury.go:874-875`, and
  `FetchUtxoView`, now for writes, `utxoviewpoint.go:961-962`) and adds lock
  notes to comments; its message says there was no race. The daemon
  serializes every chain call through one `Mutex<Chain>`, so there is no
  read/write split to correct.
- **Ported:** `cd07eeef` moves the agenda queries into the new `agendas.go`
  and changes one behaviour: `isTreasuryAgendaActive` returns false for a nil
  parent as well as for height 0, ahead of the agenda lookup
  (`agendas.go:882-890`). The port's `is_treasury_agenda_active` returned the
  forced state for `None`; it now matches (`agendas.rs:1115`), and
  `calc_sequence_lock` passes `None` for a genesis node where it passed
  `Some(-1)` (`sequencelock.rs:97`). `TestDeploymentParamsValidation`, which
  moved with the code, is ported for the first time
  (`tests/agenda_tables.rs` `deployment_params_validation`, 18 cases).
- **Ported:** `9dc1c4f5` routes every single-choice agenda query through one
  `isAgendaActive` (`agendas.go:717-745`): look the agenda up (an unknown ID
  is an error), answer false for the genesis block's nil parent even when the
  agenda is forced, then try the positional query (`dbb1cbc6`), then tally.
  The port's `is_agenda_active` takes the same steps in the same order
  (`agendas.rs:1042`), with a wrapper for each of dcrd's twelve agenda
  queries. The genesis rule changes the port's public functions, which
  returned the forced state for `None`; no dcrd caller passes a nil parent.
- **Ported:** `82d927b3` selects the stake difficulty algorithm through
  `isSDiffAlgoAgendaActive` and makes `calcNextRequiredStakeDifficulty` and
  `estimateNextStakeDifficulty` fallible (`difficulty.go:860-871`,
  `:1335-1347`); `connectBlock`, `checkBlockHeaderContext` and the startup
  load (`chainio.go:1749-1752`) propagate the error. The port's selectors
  return `Result` (`agendas.rs:1340`, `:1363`) and lose their "no deployment
  selects V2" special case, and the header context check, the connect path
  and `load_chain_state` propagate the error, the last as the new
  `ChainDbError::Rule` (`process.rs:1690-1691`). The new-tickets notification
  already reused the connect path's value, as upstream now does. No shipped
  network can raise the error.
- **Ported:** `e0f917df` makes `maxBlockSize` fallible and has it take
  `MaximumBlockSizes[1]` only when the agenda is active and a second size
  exists (`chain.go:1615-1638`). The port indexed `[1]` without that guard,
  so an active agenda with one listed size would have panicked;
  `max_block_size` now has the guard and returns `Result`
  (`agendas.rs:1218-1229`), and `check_block_context` and
  `Chain::max_block_size` propagate the error.
- **Test only:** `43b67ce6` adds `TestMaxBlockSizeChoice`, ported as
  `tests/agenda_tables.rs` `max_block_size_choice` (7 cases).
- **Ported:** `05c31a8e` replaces the threshold state tuple's choice pointer
  with a choice ID, empty for none (`thresholdstate.go:68-87`).
  `ThresholdStateTuple` now carries `choice_id: &'static str` and is `Copy`
  (`thresholdstate.rs:66`), and a forced tuple carries the forced choice's
  ID. RPC output does not change, since `getvoteinfo` and `getblockchaininfo`
  read only the state, and the replays print an empty ID as the `-` their
  files already hold. The matching edits to `rpcserverhandlers_test.go` are
  mechanical (`Choice: nil` becomes `ChoiceID: ""`) and change no expected
  output.
- **Ported:** `476d2da3` separates agendas from deployments: an agenda holds
  an optional forced state and an optional deployment, and `makeAgendas`
  builds one per deployment (`agendas.go:401`, `:449`). An unknown ID is now
  `ErrUnknownAgendaID`, "agenda ID %s does not exist" (`error.go:653-654`),
  from the agenda queries, `NextThresholdState` and `StateLastChangedHeight`.
  A forced agenda answers its forced tuple from the first and height 1 from
  the second, and an agenda with no deployment fails both with
  `ErrUnknownDeploymentID`, "agenda ID %s does not have associated deployment
  information" (`thresholdstate.go:513-577`). `GetVoteCounts` still scans the
  deployments and keeps `ErrUnknownDeploymentID`. Ported as
  `ConsensusAgenda`, `lookup_agenda` and `agenda_state` (`agendas.rs:652`,
  `:905`, `thresholdstate.rs:492`), with `Chain::next_threshold_state` and
  `Chain::state_last_changed_height` in upstream's order (`process.rs:7219`,
  `:7450`). Every unknown-agenda site in `validate.rs`, `process.rs` and
  `sequencelock.rs` now carries dcrd's kind and text, where it had carried
  the port's own text under `ErrUnknownDeploymentID`; none is reachable with
  shipped parameters. `ErrUnknownAgendaID` is a context error, so it is not
  blamed on a peer (`dcroxide-node` `sync.rs`
  `a_deployment_context_error_is_not_blamed_on_the_peer`).
  `query_vectors.txt` rows 1304 and 1307 (`ntse id`, `slce id`) were
  re-expected by hand to the new kind, with the derivation in
  `tests/query_vectors.rs`' header, and the replay now checks their text too.
- **Ported:** `ea2689db` adds the thirteen required agenda IDs
  (`agendas.go:35-49`; `lnsupport` is not one) and gives each one a network
  has no deployment for a forced default without a deployment:
  `(Defined, "")` on mainnet and `(Active, "")` everywhere else
  (`agendas.go:572-589`), which retires two special cases. Of the port's networks only
  `maxblocksize` lacks a deployment, on mainnet and testnet3, so
  `Chain::next_threshold_state` for it now answers `(Defined, "")` and
  `(Active, "")` there, and `state_last_changed_height` 1, where both failed
  with `ErrUnknownDeploymentID`. Block sizes do not change: mainnet keeps
  393216, and testnet3 keeps 1310720 because it lists one size.
  `is_blake3_pow_agenda_forced_active` now reads the agenda's forced state,
  defaults included (`agendas.go:1002-1011`, `agendas.rs:1203`).
- **Test only:** `88e6a8df` adds `TestMakeAgendasDefaultRequiredAgendas`,
  ported as `tests/agenda_tables.rs` `make_agendas_default_required_agendas`.
- **Ported:** `9a12399a` gives each agenda an activation anchor: the first
  LockedIn to Active boundary a tally computes, cached for an agenda with no
  historical entry (`thresholdstate.go:377-415`), after which `agendaState`
  answers active for the anchor's descendants without the threshold cache
  (`:441-460`). The port keeps the anchors per chain in the block index
  store, keyed by agenda ID (`blockindex.rs:362`), behind new
  `VoteChainView` hooks that `NodeBranchView` implements over the store's
  ancestor walk (`chainview_nodes.rs:493-565`), which a query from the node
  remembered beside the anchor, or from a child of it, skips (below), and
  the tally caches at the same point (`thresholdstate.rs:451-469`). For
  full-context answers this is only memoization; it matters because the
  positional query treats a discovered anchor as definitive. Any path that
  computes the boundary discovers it: the agenda queries' tally,
  `NextThresholdState` and `StateLastChangedHeight`.
- **Ported:** `dbb1cbc6` adds the historical activation tables, the parent
  of each agenda's activation block on mainnet (13 entries, `lnsupport`
  included) and testnet3 (10) (`agendas.go:66-188`). `makeAgendas` attaches
  them and validates them, with the new `ErrHistoricalForcedChoice` for a
  forced agenda that has an entry (`error.go:722-724`), so `New` fails on a
  bad table (`chain.go:2148-2154`). `isAgendaActivePositional`
  (`agendas.go:620-679`) answers from the block's position and its
  ancestors' headers when it can: the genesis block, a forced state, a
  parent below the anchor height, a cached anchor, or an ancestor at the
  anchor height carrying the anchor's hash, which it then caches; and
  `isAgendaActive` tries it before tallying. The port stores the tables in
  internal byte order and pins them against `Hash::from_str` of dcrd's
  literals (`agendas.rs:105`, `:264`; `tests/agenda_tables.rs`
  `historical_agenda_tables_match_dcrd`), runs `make_agendas` with its full
  validation when a chain is built (`process.rs:812-817`, `:1126`), and adds
  `is_agenda_active_positional`, `is_agenda_active_positional_by_id` and
  `Chain::is_agenda_active_positional_by_id` (`agendas.rs:949`, `:1017`,
  `process.rs:7264`). On the real chains the answers equal the tally's. What
  changes is that a mainnet or testnet3 query below an anchor no longer
  counts votes, so `tests/review_blake3_anchor.rs`, whose synthetic mainnet
  chain votes BLAKE3 in at 28287, drops blake3pow from its view's table, the
  counterpart of upstream's `removeHistoricalConsensusChange`
  (`common_test.go:573-586`).
- **Test only:** `5cf38f6a` adds `TestIsAgendaActivePositional`, 17 ordered
  cases over a branched chain, ported as `tests/agenda_positional.rs`
  `is_agenda_active_positional_cases`.
- **Test only:** `20bc7518` adds `TestHistoricalAgendaInvariants` (each entry
  sits one block before a rule change interval), ported as
  `tests/agenda_tables.rs` `historical_agenda_invariants`, which also checks
  that each ID has an unforced deployment.
- **No change needed:** `26ea49aa` removes the unused exported
  `CheckProofOfStake` wrapper. The port carries the unexported
  `checkProofOfStake` (`validate.go:655`, `validate.rs:418`); only its doc
  reference changed.
- **Ported:** `a0cdd433` removes `ErrStakeFees`, which nothing raised.
  `RuleErrorKind::StakeFees` is gone too, so with the two kinds above the
  port carries dcrd's 161.

Block processing:

- **Ported:** `024899e4` validates and indexes a previously unknown header in
  full before it looks at the block's data. `maybeAcceptBlockHeader` loses
  its `checkHeaderSanity` flag and always sanity checks an unknown header
  (`process.go:159-221`), and `checkBlockSanity` becomes the header checks
  followed by the new `checkBlockDataSanity` (`validate.go:881-987`). In
  `ProcessBlock` (`process.go:445-547`), header sanity, `ErrMissingParent`,
  `ErrInvalidAncestorBlock` and the header's positional errors now all come
  before any data error, so an orphan with bad data is reported as an
  orphan, and a valid new header stays in the index when its data is
  rejected, which shows in the best header, `getchaintips` and a later
  `ProcessBlockHeader`. A header already in the index is not sanity checked
  again, so `ErrTimeTooNew` after the clock steps back no longer rejects its
  block. `maybeAcceptBlockData` no longer marks a failed positional data
  check itself; `ProcessBlock` does, under the rule `ddd3b6d2` adds. Ported
  in `maybe_accept_block_header`, `check_block_data_sanity`,
  `check_block_sanity` and `process_block` (`process.rs:3679`,
  `validate.rs:694`, `:889`, `process.rs:5207`).
- **Ported:** `ddd3b6d2` checks a block's data against its header first.
  `checkMerkleRoots` now takes the merkle variant to check
  (`validate.go:1879-1952`). The new `checkBlockDataPreconditions`
  (`:1954-2049`) checks the wire size limit, asks the positional query
  whether the header commitments agenda is active after the parent, and
  then either checks the variant a definitive answer selects and reports the
  commitment proven, or accepts either variant and reports it unproven. The
  answer is definitive on simnet (forced), on mainnet and testnet3 (the
  historical tables), and on regnet once the agenda is forced or a tally has
  found its activation; where both variants are tried and both fail, the
  error text is the DCP0005 variant's. `checkMerkleRootsContext`
  (`:2051-2078`) checks the variant the full context selects, and
  `checkBlockContext` now runs it right after the header context checks
  (`:2125-2143`). `ProcessBlock` runs the preconditions right after header
  acceptance and never marks on them; data sanity and data positional
  failures mark the block only when the commitment is proven and the error
  is a rule error (`process.go:488-515`, `:537-547`). `maybeAcceptBlocks`
  and `reorganizeChainInternal` no longer mark a contextual
  `ErrBadMerkleRoot` (`process.go:367-374`, `chain.go:1208-1214`), and
  `CheckConnectBlockTemplate` runs the preconditions after its parent rule
  and before sanity (`validate.go:4678-4682`). So the wire `ErrBlockTooBig`
  and `ErrBadMerkleRoot` now come before every data sanity, data positional
  and contextual error, and data that does not match its header, known or
  new, no longer marks it, so the real data is accepted afterwards
  (upstream `process_test.go:745-759`). Ported as `MerkleRootVariant`,
  `check_merkle_roots`, `check_block_data_preconditions`,
  `check_merkle_roots_context` and `Chain::check_block_data_preconditions`
  (`validate.rs:2287`, `:2307`, `:2463`, `:2543`, `process.rs:4865`), with
  the marking in `mark_block_failed_if_data_commit_proven` and
  `mark_block_failed_on_context_violation` (`process.rs:4932`, `:6714`).
  The contextual exemption is pinned at both sites by
  `tests/review_merkle_variant_stall.rs`
  `a_contextual_bad_merkle_root_marks_no_block`; what the reorganization
  does once an attempt has failed at such a block differs from dcrd
  (*Reorganization past a block whose contextual merkle check fails*
  under Deliberate divergences).
  Porting it surfaced a gap: dcrd's `PopulateTicketInfo` marks the node
  modified (`blockindex.go:955-960`, called at `process.go:297-298`), so a
  header's stored index row is rewritten with the block's votes even when a
  later data check rejects the block. `maybe_accept_block_data` populated
  without marking, which no test saw while every such rejection also marked
  the block failed; it now marks (`process.rs:5008`), pinned by
  `tests/review_process_reorder.rs`
  `rejected_block_data_still_rewrites_the_headers_votes`.
- **Ported, not observable:** `b56e78f5` adds `recentMerkleChecks`, a
  25-entry LRU of block hashes whose data commitment has been proven
  (`chain.go:52-55`, `:205-209`). The preconditions put a definitive pass
  into it and `checkBlockContext` a contextual one; `checkBlockContext`
  consults it before hashing the transaction trees; `InvalidateBlock` and
  both `ReconsiderBlock` loops delete the block (`validate.go:2032`,
  `:2134-2143`, `process.go:709`, `:837`, `:889`). It only spares hashing
  the trees again. The port generalizes its recent-context-checks set into
  `LruHashSet`, which holds both caches (`process.rs:625`, `:643`), and
  `check_block_context` stays a pure function that takes the cache as a
  `&mut impl MerkleCheckCache`; the vector replays pass `NoMerkleCheckCache`
  (`validate.rs:2563-2584`).

The test commits:

- **Test only, vectors regenerated:** `2bd17b22` points `brt7`'s revocation
  at its ticket's change output, which the input index check must reject
  with `ErrInvalidRevokeInput` (`generate.go:2866-2879`;
  `validate.go:3218-3224`, port `validate.rs:3324-3333`), and renames the
  accepted block that follows to `brt8` (`generate.go:2885`), which the
  `bdt` fork now builds on. `tests/review_rule_marking.rs` moves to `brt8`
  with it.
- **No change needed:** `17c51410` rewords `bmf37`'s comment
  (`generate.go:2115-2116`); its expectation, `ErrRegTxCreateStakeOut`, is
  unchanged.
- **Test only, vectors regenerated:** `70ba63ea` adds `bti1`, accepted, which
  pays an output to a bare `OP_TRUE`, and `bti2`, whose ticket spends that
  output and is rejected with `ErrTicketInputScript`
  (`generate.go:2971-2997`; `validate.go:2695-2701`, port
  `validate.rs:2728-2739`).
- **Test only, vectors regenerated:** `90accde2` has `bti1` also pay a
  version 1 P2SH output and adds `bti3`, whose ticket spends it and is
  rejected with `ErrTicketInputScript` by the script version check
  (`generate.go:3004-3012`; `validate.go:2688-2694`, port
  `validate.rs:2716-2727`).
- **Test only, ported:** `855dc8be` adds a `TestAutoRevocations` block whose
  version 2 revocation references its ticket's change output and is rejected
  with `ErrInvalidRevokeInput` (`validate_test.go:1946`). `1995f4fc` adds
  `TestImmatureTicketSpend` (`:1741`), the vote and revocation maturity
  checks on either side of maturity with and without automatic revocations,
  and `b6c67575` adds `TestInvalidTicketInput` (`:1854`), votes and
  revocations whose ticket input is a stake output that is not a ticket.
  Upstream calls `CheckTransactionInputs` directly for the last two, because
  the block path's ticket redeemer checks run first and cannot reach these
  rules; the mempool can. The port has no `chaingen`, so all three are
  replayed from chains and verdicts a scratch exporter dumped inside dcrd's
  `internal/blockchain` at `6f6cf21b` (`tests/ticketspend_vectors.rs`,
  `data/ticketspend_vectors.txt`): the replay rebuilds each chain through
  `Chain::process_block`, checks every input's utxo entry and the tip header
  against dcrd's, and compares kind, fee and text through
  `check_transaction_inputs`, the mempool's entry point.

The rest:

- **Ported:** `3dde63a8` makes `ReadOutPoint` refuse a negative tree once it
  has read the tree byte, and `WriteOutPoint` refuse one before writing,
  with the new `ErrNegativeTxTree` ("ReadOutPoint: negative transaction
  tree: -128"; `wire/msgtx.go:1174-1218`, `wire/error.go:114-116`). See the
  `wire` row for the port and the one divergence it keeps.
- **Test only, ported:** `6f6cf21b` adds `TestOutPointSerialize`
  (`wire/msgtx_test.go:702-837`), ported as `msgtx.rs`
  `read_out_point_matches_dcrd` and `write_out_point_matches_dcrd`. Its
  three short-write rows rely on dcrd's `fixedWriter`, which has no
  counterpart because a `Vec` cannot fail a write short; they are checks of
  the full encoding at the same byte boundaries.
- **Ported, help text only:** `3fe61c02` corrects the `--profile` help from
  "between 1024 and 65536" to "65535" (`config.go:123`, `doc.go:38`). dcrd's
  `validateProfileAddr` (`profiler.go:30-44`) always refused 65536, and so
  did the port, so only the text moves: the `flags.rs` description,
  `tools/helpgen`'s re-extracted config struct, and both help vectors,
  regenerated with one line changed in each. Nothing in this repository
  copies `doc.go`'s usage text.
- **Ported, comment only:** `b02ffa7b` explains why `OP_2MUL` and `OP_2DIV`
  run as NOPs yet stay out of the discouraged set
  (`txscript/opcode.go:740-744`); the paragraph is on `opcode_nop`
  (`opcodes.rs:225-230`), and no opcode's behaviour changes.
- **Not ported:** `c658232f` removes a shadowed `roots` declaration from
  `mixclient`, which is wallet-side and not ported (the `mixing` row).

Vectors. `fullblock_vectors.txt` was regenerated at `6f6cf21b`
(`now 1790925179`): 573 instances became 577, with four expectation changes
and one rename. `brt7` now rejects with `ErrInvalidRevokeInput`, the accepted
block it used to name is `brt8`, and `bti1` (accept), `bti2` and `bti3`
(`ErrTicketInputScript`) are new, so accept goes from 204 to 205 and reject
from 350 to 353, with the other row kinds unchanged. `tools/fullblockgen` had
stayed at `036b7090` through the `b9634e01` move, because `fullblocktests`
did not change then and nothing read its `go.mod`. It now links `6f6cf21b`,
which also moved `chaincfg`, `blake256` and `crypto/rand` from tags to
pseudo-versions (their differences never reach regnet), and
`the_dcrd_pin_matches_ci_and_the_oracle` checks its `go.mod` as well as the
oracle's. The battery's first block carries its generation time, so a
regeneration broke `dcroxide-node`'s `review_bgtemplate_clock.rs` for about a
day; it now mines over regnet genesis instead of a battery prefix. The help
vectors were regenerated by `tools/helpgen`. `processheaders_vectors.txt` was
regenerated without the flag column `024899e4` removed: six rows (36, 38, 39,
40, 42 and 46) had passed `false`, five of their headers were reground to
pass sanity, and the file was replayed through dcrd's `maybeAcceptBlockHeader`
at `6f6cf21b`, keeping every verdict, status byte, assumed-valid height and
checkpoint height and the `[43, 1, 4]` counts. The same harness reproduces the
previous file byte for byte at `b9634e01`, and the derivation is in the
replay's header. Re-expected by hand, each with its derivation in the
replay's header: `query_vectors.txt` rows 1304 and 1307 (above), and fifteen
rows of steps 84-87 of `netsync_vectors.txt` (see the `internal/netsync`
row). New: `ticketspend_vectors.txt`. None of the generators behind these
four files is committed. Every other vector file is unchanged and replays as
before. The agenda-dependent sets (agenda, threshold, difficulty, stake
version, positional, contextual, sequence lock) were checked against the new
rules, and the block processing sets (blocksanity, blockcontext,
processblock, template, connectblock, reorg, invalidate) against the reorder;
in `blockcontext_vectors.txt` every `cbc` block but the two that already
expect `ErrBadMerkleRoot` carries a valid DCP0005 root, so moving the merkle
check changes no verdict, as the replay's header records. Every dcrd package
outside the delta is byte-identical at both pins.

Deviations the port keeps at `6f6cf21b`, beyond the wire one:

- The port's chain holds no parameters, so it keeps no agenda map: each
  query builds its one agenda with `lookup_agenda`, which shares every
  per-agenda step with `make_agendas`
  (`tests/agenda_tables.rs` `lookup_agrees_with_make_agendas`).
  `make_agendas` runs for its validation when a chain is built and its map
  is dropped, so the per-query lookup relies on that check for the rules
  that span all the parameters (duplicate IDs, overlapping masks, choice
  shapes).
- `Chain::new` cannot fail, so it panics with dcrd's error text when the
  agendas do not validate, as dcrd's test constructor `newFakeChain` does.
  `Chain::open` and its variants return the error as `ChainDbError::Rule`,
  as `New` returns it.
- Activation anchors live in the block index store, keyed by agenda ID,
  where dcrd hangs one on each agenda; lifetime and first-found sharing are
  the same. Each anchor also carries the node most recently shown to
  descend from it (`ActiveAnchor`, `blockindex.rs:297`), which dcrd does not
  have: a query from that node or from a child of it is answered without
  dcrd's `IsAncestorOf` walk (`blockindex.go:372-374`, called at
  `thresholdstate.go:454` and `agendas.go:652`), and every other query
  walks as before (`active_anchor_cached`, `chainview_nodes.rs:501`). A tip
  is remembered only once it has been answered yes, by a walk or because
  it is the remembered node or a child of it, and a replaced anchor starts
  from the tip whose walk found it. The answers are dcrd's: a child's
  branch is its parent's and one block higher, which the positional
  header checks hold every header to, and under debug assertions a
  remembered answer is walked for and compared. Pinned by
  five unit tests in `chainview_nodes.rs`, one of them compiled only in
  debug builds; one of the seven changes the `internal/blockchain` row
  measures together. Views other than `NodeBranchView` keep no-op anchor
  hooks and tally instead, which gives the same answers; only the positional
  query's validity flag can differ there, for an anchor a tally found. The
  historical tables reach every query through
  `VoteChainView::historical_agendas`, whose default is the network's table
  and which only test views override, as the counterpart of
  `removeHistoricalConsensusChange`; a vector view without `ancestor_hash`
  cannot resolve the descendant-of-anchor rule and tallies above the anchor
  height instead.
- `makeAgendas` ranges over Go maps, so when several errors apply which one
  it reports is not fixed; the port reports in parameter and table order.
- `estimate_next_stake_difficulty` and
  `Chain::calc_next_required_difficulty_by_hash` keep their `String` errors,
  now with the agenda error's text.
- `bf449ef6`'s lock changes have no counterpart (above).
- dcrd's `checkBlockDataPreconditions` is split in two: a pure function in
  `validate.rs` that takes the positional query as a closure and runs it
  after the size check, as dcrd does, and
  `Chain::check_block_data_preconditions`, which supplies the query and puts
  the proven hash in the cache. The marking gates test
  `RuleErrorKind::is_rule_violation()` where dcrd tests
  `errors.As(err, &RuleError)`, the convention the port already follows,
  because storage failures travel as rule error kinds it excludes.
- `LruHashSet` is a 25-entry `VecDeque` searched linearly rather than dcrd's
  map-backed `lru.Set`, with the same recency rules.
- The block index flush dcrd makes after accepting block data
  (`process.go:549-554`) is still not reproduced: `connect_block` flushes
  moments later, and a data-stored bit lost to a crash is healed by the
  store's duplicate-block check (`process_block`'s comment).
- `ticketspend_vectors` replays chains an exporter dumped rather than
  building them with `chaingen`, fetches each input with
  `Chain::fetch_utxo_entry` where upstream calls `FetchUtxoView`, compares
  error text as well as kind, and stops `TestAutoRevocations` after `b5`,
  the extent of `855dc8be`'s change.

Found and fixed during the bump, though not part of the delta: dcrd formats
`maxAtoms` and `dcrutil.MaxAmount`, untyped floating-point constants, with
`%v`, so its range messages say `2.1e+15` where the port printed
2100000000000000 (`blockchain/standalone/tx.go:33-34`,
`dcrutil/const.go:13-16`, `internal/blockchain/validate.go:3344-3348`,
`:3523-3527`, `:3727-3731`, `blockchain/stake/staketx.go:1411-1415`). The
standalone sanity checks, `check_treasury_spend_inputs`,
`check_transaction_inputs` and `create_revocation_from_ticket` now print Go's
text (`dcroxide-standalone` `tx.rs:32`, `dcroxide-stake` `MAX_AMOUNT_TEXT`,
`lib.rs:191`), and it reaches `submitblock`'s rejection text for the battery's
`bcb9` and `bcb10`. Pinned by `tests/review_error_texts.rs`,
`treasury_tx_vectors.rs` `check_transaction_sanity_amount_range_texts` and
the stake oracle differential `create_revocation_amount_range_text`. And
`ErrInvalidTemplateParent` now names the tip's parent, with dcrd's second
form for a tip without one (`validate.go:4665-4675`, `process.rs:5902-5917`;
`review_error_texts.rs` `template_parent_texts_name_the_tips_parent`).

**`036b7090..b9634e01` (3 commits).** `tools/pinbump` resolves the delta to
`dcroxide-blockchain` and `dcroxide-stake`, with no unmapped packages. All
three are ported or accounted for.

- **Ported:** `ead8ba7a` moves the block size and merkle root checks out of
  the `BFFastAdd` exemption so they run on the assumevalid fast-add path too,
  on the grounds that the hashing is now cheap and dropping them "prevents
  certain classes of potential harassment during initial sync". The port had
  faithfully reproduced the exemption, so until this it accepted blocks during
  assumevalid sync that current dcrd rejects. Both checks now sit above the
  `fast_add` gate in `check_block_context`, as upstream places them.
- **Ported:** `5dd4ca59` corrects an off-by-one in the revocation creation
  helper, which rejected zero-valued commitments that are valid by consensus
  while its own error message said the minimum was 0. The port carried the
  identical `<= 0`, message included. What kept it invisible was the
  differential generator: `build_ticket` drew amounts as `rng.below(..) + 1`,
  so the boundary the bug lived on was never generated. It now emits a zero
  for a later output, and reverting the fix fails that test at round 1. Only a
  later output, because the reward split divides by the total contribution and
  an all-zero ticket -- which cannot exist on chain -- divides by zero in both
  implementations, `uint256::div2` panicking there on purpose.
- **No change needed:** `b9634e01` fixes two Go `:=` shadowing bugs. The port
  already assigns rather than shadows at the `chainio.go` site
  (`new_rules_start_time` in `Chain::open_with_config`, `process.rs`), and
  dcrd's `upgrade.go` database migration path is not ported at all, so
  neither half reaches this tree.

**`29f17894..036b7090` (19 commits).** Two are consensus-affecting and
unported, four more need assessment, and the remaining thirteen are not
observable.

- **Ported in this sync:** `a38c0195` and `9b7ab54a` add dcrd's explicit
  rejection of stake transactions that spend an output created in the block
  they are mined in, together with the historical mainnet and testnet3
  exceptions that keep those chains validating. Shipped as part of dcrd v2.1.6,
  a mandatory security release. Reproduced in `validate.rs`
  (`SBSS_VIOLATIONS`, `is_sbss_spend_violation`, the check in
  `check_transaction_inputs` and the view seeding in `check_connect_block`) and
  pinned by dcrd's own battery: the regenerated `fullblock_vectors.txt`
  carries `bmf25` and `bmf35` at `ErrMissingTxOut`, which only passes if the
  check fires ahead of the maturity check exactly as upstream's does. Since
  `96a55a74`, on master after `6f6cf21b`, the predicate is `is_sbss_violation`
  over a lookup by height (`sbss_violation_hash`), and the seeding is
  `UtxoView::add_sbss_violations`, run before the input fetch by
  `check_connect_block` and by the view's block connect, and the view's block
  disconnect spends it again (`remove_sbss_violations_with`).
- **Assessed by walking `tools/pinbump`'s crate list:** `cc5e1d63` treats
  ORCHIDv2 (`2001:20::/28`) as unroutable and the port did not — now ported,
  pinned by `orchid_v2_addresses_are_unroutable`. `3d1c81e1` and `8f6ff12a`
  are `mixclient` changes, and `mixclient` is not ported (deferred with
  `keyagreement.go`, per the mixing row below). `589dd4c5` gives dcrd its own
  per-peer queued-output byte cap (40 MiB, disconnect on exceed), which turns
  the *Per-peer outbound queue* row below from a divergence in kind into one in
  value and action; reconciling the two is a deliberate choice about honest
  peers on slow links, not a porting gap, and is still open.
- **Ported late, after being misfiled here as not observable:** `01208035`
  makes an in-flight utxo add cover only the referenced output
  (`AddTxOut(originTx, originIdx)`) rather than every output of the origin
  transaction. This ledger once claimed the port "already adds singly"; it
  never did. `queue_regular_input_utxos` called `add_tx_outs_with_hash` for
  the whole origin, and the difference decides acceptance: a disapproved
  parent's disconnect leaves its outputs spent in the view, and re-adding a
  re-mined transaction's unreferenced siblings marked them unspent again, so a
  forward reference to one of them passed where dcrd fails it with
  `ErrMissingTxOut`. The same review found `resolve_queued` keeping an entry
  that a later in-flight add had filled after the outpoint was queued, where
  dcrd's `UtxoCache.FetchEntries` overwrites every queued outpoint with the
  backend result, so a forward reference whose origin a later transaction also
  spends was accepted as well. Both now follow dcrd, pinned by
  `tests/inflight_utxo.rs` (each case fails against the old code).
- **Not observable:** `efc7e3d9` and `ae4c5818`
  stop a subsidy cache inserting duplicate intervals under concurrency, which
  cannot arise here because the port's cache is a `BTreeMap` with no parallel
  sorted-interval vector to grow; `085eb08c`
  is secp256k1 byte clearing over a layer the port delegates to `k256`; and
  `9ae5864c`, `ffecb8e0`, `025e7c6e`, `495124df`, `7080c11c`, `885ea18c`,
  `e201209e` and `500ce093` are build, docs, module-version and test-only.
- **Mis-bucketed on the first pass:** `036b7090` tightens a `getByIndex`
  bounds guard from `>` to `>=`, recorded here as not observable because it
  only changes which panic fires.  The port had the same off-by-one, and the
  same blind spot in the test that should have caught it -- asserting only
  that an out-of-range index panicked, which the walk running off the end
  satisfied.  Both are fixed; the test now asserts the panic came from the
  bounds check.

`QK-NNNN` references in the notes below are entries in
[QUIRKS.md](QUIRKS.md), which catalogues the deliberate bug-for-bug
reproductions and the tests pinning them.

Status legend:

- **—** not started
- **WIP** implementation in progress
- **vectors** dcrd test vectors ported or regenerated via `tools/oracle`
- **semantics** an interface reimplemented over a different engine, pinned by
  observable behaviour rather than by dcrd's own bytes
- **diff** live differential coverage against the dcrd oracle
- **parity** vectors + differential + fuzz sign-off complete

| dcrd package | dcroxide crate | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `crypto/blake256` | `dcroxide-crypto` | vectors + diff | Vendored from dcr-rs `fd32c1a`; KATs regenerated via oracle; live differential test + incremental-hashing fuzz target |
| `crypto/ripemd160` | `dcroxide-crypto` | vectors | RustCrypto `ripemd` one-shot digest (dcrd's package is itself the old x/crypto impl); standard Bosselaers reference vectors pinned. Consumed by `txscript` OP_RIPEMD160/OP_HASH160 |
| `crypto/rand` | `dcroxide-crypto` | semantics | `prng.go`'s byte layer is ported as `dcroxide_crypto::rand::Prng` (`maxCipherRead` :20, `nonce.inc` :26-40, `NewPRNG` :55-63 including the zero-value-cipher mix, `PRNG.seed` :67-81, `PRNG.Read` :85-105 including the mid-buffer split loop), behind a non-default `rand` feature so the no_std job keeps building this crate and the six of its other seven crates that reach it on `x86_64-unknown-none`. `uniform.go`'s reductions are ported too — `PRNG.Uint64` (:54-58), `PRNG.Uint64N` (:102-148, Lemire multiply-shift with dcrd's `lo < n` fast path and `-n % n` threshold, and its power-of-two mask branch, which also takes a zero bound) and `PRNG.Shuffle` (:214-230) — as are the package globals: `lockingPRNG` (`prng.go:111-114`), the `init` that seeds it (`:116-122`), and the `default.go` entry points the peer module's draws go through, `Uint64` (:37-42), `Shuffle` (:136-141) and `ShuffleSlice` (:144-148), plus `IntN` (:109-114) for the mining-address pick and `Duration` (:126-131) for the seeder's backdating, the rebroadcast timer and the inventory trickle period (`peer/peer.go:1628-1631`). The globals are ported because dcrd's peer module reaches randomness through nothing else — `rand.Uint64()` for the ping and version nonces (`peer/peer.go:1813`, `:2186`) and `rand.ShuffleSlice` for both addr relays (`:842`, `:873`) — with no per-peer generator anywhere in that file. Go runs `init` before `main`; Rust has no equivalent, so the seeding is lazy behind a `OnceLock` and `dcroxide_crypto::rand::init()` is the explicit hook the daemon calls at startup to put dcrd's one fatal kernel read back there. Porting the global is also what makes the `rand` feature link `std`: a `OnceLock<Mutex<Prng>>` has no `core` form. `semantics` rather than `vectors` because dcrd ships no tests for this package at all — `crypto/rand` carries only `bench_test.go`, with no `Test` functions — so there is nothing to port; it is pinned by behavioural tests plus a cross-language known-answer vector for the zero-value cipher dcrd's first seeding mixes through. dcrd reaches one `rand.PRNG` two ways: most packages through the package global — `addrmgr`, `peer` and `internal/rpcserver` among them — and `internal/connmgr` through an instance of its own. So does this port: `dcroxide_addrmgr::SystemRng` and `dcroxide_connmgr::SystemCsprng` are instances, and the peer environment, the websocket session id, the template generator's address pick and extra nonces, the CPU miner's extra-nonce offset, the seeder's address backdating, the RPC ping nonce with the auth HMAC key beside it, the rebroadcast timer's jitter, the inventory trickle period, and the additional data hedging each P-521 RPC handshake signature all draw from the process-wide generator.  The RPC auth HMAC key is drawn as dcrd draws it, by one `rand.Read` of thirty-two bytes (`internal/rpcserver/rpcserver.go:6238-6239`) rather than through the `rand_u64` config seam, so the generator's lock is taken once where it had been taken four times with room for another thread to interleave.  It is not injected, for the same reason dcrd does not inject it: the stored MACs only ever compare against MACs made under the same key in the same process, so nothing observes it.  The ping nonce keeps its seam, because the handler vectors run through it. Not ported, with the reason for each: `maxCipherDuration` (unobservable, and a clock read on every draw); `uniform.go`'s `Uint32N` and the `is32bit` delegation to it (:61-97, :99, :103-104 — Go's own comment says the 32-bit arithmetic exists "to preserve the exact output sequence observed on 64-bit machines" (:69-71), so the 64-bit `Uint64N` ported here yields the same values on every target); `Shuffle`'s `(n, swap)` callback form (Go needs a swap closure because it cannot name a generic slice-element swap; `<[T]>::swap` is that closure, so `Shuffle` and `ShuffleSlice` collapse into one `shuffle_slice`); the `default.go` entry points no consumer reaches — `Reader`, `Read`, `Uint32`, `Uint32N`, `Uint64N` and the remaining `Int*`/`UintN`/`BigInt`/`Float64` wrappers, each two lines over `Prng` when a consumer appears; and `prng_arc4random.go` (a Go-toolchain fallback). The remaining `getrandom` calls are enumerated site by site, each with the reason it is allowed to stay, by `crates/dcroxide-node/tests/entropy_policy.rs`.  Four file entries remain: `socks.rs` reads once per outbound dial under `--torisolation`, and three are one-shot (the RPC credential write in `bin/dcroxide.rs`, `bin/gencerts.rs` a tool, `rpcrun.rs` at boot), and the event-paced one is already correct, returning the error rather than aborting, because its surrounding exchange is fallible for a dozen other reasons anyway. The fifth, `rpctls.rs`'s read once per RPC TLS handshake a client steers to P-521, moved with the P-521 key exchange into `dcroxide-tlscrypto`, the in-tree TLS provider (ADR-0015), whose `tests/entropy_policy.rs` holds that crate to one kernel read, `rand::kernel_fill`. What that does and does not close, stated carefully because four attempts to summarise this set as closed were each wrong: every draw in this crate that an event could pace *and that could abort* now comes from a generator seeded once — the process one for the seams dcrd reaches through its package functions, an instance for the address and connection managers, which dcrd also gives their own. One event-paced kernel read remains in this crate and is staying. `socks.rs` draws credentials per outbound dial under `--torisolation`, paced by the node's connect schedule, and upstream's go-socks draws there from Go's standard-library `crypto/rand` rather than dcrd's package generator — so converting it would be the divergence, and it already returns its error rather than aborting. The ephemeral scalar of each P-521 key exchange, paced by whoever connects to the RPC port, is drawn from the kernel too, now by `dcroxide-tlscrypto`, for the same two reasons — Go's `crypto/tls` draws that key from its standard-library `crypto/rand`, and a failed read fails the one handshake — and a third: the scalar is the session's whole secret, and the process generator keeps its ChaCha20 key for up to 4 MiB of draws, so a later read of the process's memory would recover every scalar drawn since it was last keyed, where the kernel's generator erases its key as it goes. The P-521 handshake signature stays on the process generator, since RFC 6979 hedges its draw; it is made by `dcroxide-tlscrypto`'s ECDSA signer now, which draws that hedge through the crate's `rand::ProcessRng`, its one use of the process generator, and the crate's own `tests/entropy_policy.rs` accounts for both of its sources. The scope throughout is this crate's own source; dependencies draw their own entropy, rustls and ring per TLS handshake among them, and `dcroxide-tlscrypto` per handshake that negotiates P-521 or is signed with a P-521 key. Those counts are asserted by `entropy_policy.rs` rather than only stated here, because restating them in prose across four conversions left one or another of them stale every time. What the conversions cost is worth recording too: the peer environment was estimated at a field on `NodePeerEnv` plus a shape change across eight construction sites and four test doubles, and it turned out to be none of them — dcrd's peer module holds no generator, so porting `globalRand` left `NodePeerEnv` zero-sized and nothing else moved. The two an unauthenticated caller could pace are both gone — the peer environment and the websocket session id draw from the process-wide generator, as dcrd's peer and rpcserver packages do, so a handshake nonce, an over-full addr shuffle and a session id cost no kernel read at all. None is the mempool defect any more; that class is closed (see the divergences table below) |
| `chaincfg/chainhash` | `dcroxide-chainhash` | vectors + diff | `hash_test.go` vectors ported (incl. short-string zero-pad quirk); parse/display differential + fuzz target. Not ported: Go-specific plumbing (`SetBytes` pointer API, marshalers) |
| `dcrec/secp256k1` + `ecdsa` (type 0) | `dcroxide-dcrec` | vectors + diff | dcrd's exact DER + pubkey acceptance (all 25 error kinds, incl. hybrid keys) over libsecp256k1 per ADR-0006; TestSignatureParsing/TestParsePubKey/TestSignatureSerialize ported, and TestSignAndVerify, TestVerifyFailures, TestSignAndRecoverCompact and TestRecoverCompactErrors (`tests/ecdsa_vectors.rs`; the recovery failures by verdict alone until `recover_compact` reports dcrd's kinds). The signature soak's witness vectors (`tests/sigdiff_witnesses.rs`) hold verify and recovery to dcrd's answers at the parity pin for the branches only a solved-for input reaches (X.x = r + N, or neither r nor r + N, with r below P - N; X.x = r + N - P; a sum at infinity; a doubling; a recovery through the overflow bit), each derived again on k256, with a recovery rejection held by its verdict alone as above. Differential: parse verdict, kind, values and error text. Pubkey errors print dcrd's `Error()` descriptions with their length, format byte, oddness and coordinates; stdaddr's `failed to parse public key: %v` returns them to RPC clients. RFC6979 sign byte-equality covers hashes >= N too. libsecp256k1 reduces the hash before deriving its nonce and dcrd's `NonceRFC6979` does not, so those hashes are signed on k256 through a port of dcrd's `signRFC6979`. Verify verdicts include high-S. `ecdsa::Error` still displays kind names: no daemon surface prints a DER parse error, because the engine pushes false and verifymessage discards it; 2 property fuzz targets, and 3 (`dcrec_diff_ecdsa`, `dcrec_diff_recover`, `dcrec_diff_pubkey`) that read each input as a case of one of the signature soak's classes and compare signing, verification, DER and key parsing and recovery with dcrd through the oracle. Since ported for the netsync/RPC phases: compact-sig recovery (`RecoverCompact`, behind the `recovery` feature, driving the `verifymessage` handler).  Not ported: `SignCompact` (only wallets sign compact messages); `PrivKeyFromBytes` mod-N reduction (the port rejects out-of-range keys instead — not an observable surface) |
| `dcrec/secp256k1/schnorr` (type 2) | `dcroxide-dcrec` | vectors + diff | EC-Schnorr-DCRv0 on k256 per ADR-0006; dcrd's `NonceRFC6979` ported exactly (raw-key HMAC variant, extra-data/version/iteration semantics) and pinned by dcrd's own nonce vectors; TestSchnorrSignAndVerify (RFC6979 and explicit-nonce rows) and TestVerifyErrors ported. The signature soak's witness vectors (`tests/sigdiff_witnesses.rs`) hold verify to dcrd's answers for r = R.x at or above N, an R.x of r + N or r - N, and a doubling sum, each derived again on k256. Differential: sign byte-equality; verify verdicts, including the rejection paths signing never reaches (R at infinity via s = -e*d, zero s, a committed odd R, arbitrary parsed bytes); parse verdict and kind; pubkey parse error text. Verification's R = s*G + e*Q runs as one variable-time GLV/wNAF pass (`mul_by_generator_and_mul_add_vartime`). Every input is public, as with dcrd's `NonConst` operations, and the key is handed to k256 uncompressed. Signing keeps the constant-time `GENERATOR * k`. `schnorr::Error` displays kind names. Its one observable surface, TSPEND's `ParseSignature: %w`, is formatted with dcrd's text in validate.rs; a property fuzz target, and `dcrec_diff_schnorr`, which reads each input as a case of one of the signature soak's classes and compares it with dcrd through the oracle. Unreachable-by-construction kinds (`ErrPrivateKeyIsZero`, `ErrPubKeyNotOnCurve`) not represented |
| `dcrec/edwards` (type 1) | `dcroxide-dcrec` | vectors + diff | Ed25519 on curve25519-dalek with dcrd's exact (2017-agl) acceptance implemented explicitly: sig-R parse accepts non-canonical encodings incl. x=0/sign-bit; pubkey parse rejects x=0/sign-bit (the `X >= P` quirk) and re-serializes canonically; raw verify checks only S's top 3 bits (full `s < L` at parse, the consensus path). RFC8032 TEST 1 KAT. The signature soak's witness vectors (`tests/sigdiff_witnesses.rs`) hold raw verify to dcrd's answers for small-order keys in non-canonical encodings and for a zero S with R the identity, canonical and as y + p, each derived again on curve25519-dalek. Differential: sign+pubkey byte-equality, edge-biased parse verdicts, raw-verify verdicts incl. s+L malleation; a property fuzz target; `dcrec_diff_ed25519`, which reads each input as a case of one of the signature soak's classes and compares it with dcrd through the oracle; and `dcrec_ed25519_model`, which reads the same classes, the torsion keys among them, and holds verification to the soak's Go-free model of dcrd's with no oracle. `sign_with_pub_key_bytes` reproduces dcrd's `PrivKeyFromBytes` signing path where the embedded (last-32-bytes) public key participates in the commitment (used by `txscript/sign`). Not implemented: scalar-based keys (`PrivKeyFromScalar`/`SignFromScalar`, wallet-side legacy) and threshold signing |
| `math/uint256` | `dcroxide-uint256` | vectors + diff | Full API port (arithmetic incl. Knuth 4.3.1D division, u64 variants, bitwise/shifts, comparisons, BE/LE bytes, text in bases 2/8/10/16) with dcrd's wrap-around and panic-on-zero-divisor semantics; differential across all 22 ops with boundary-biased operands; u128-reference + algebraic property tests; fuzz target. Not ported: `big.Int` interop (Go-specific), `fmt.Formatter` (Rust fmt traits provided; `text` is the parity surface) |
| `wire` | `dcroxide-wire` | vectors + diff | Framing (24-byte header, BLAKE-256 checksum, dcrd's exact validation order + error kinds) and **all 41 message types** at protocol 12 (40 decodable; reject is write-only per QK-0001): version (optional-field semantics), addr/getaddr, inv/getdata/notfound, getblocks/getheaders/headers/block, ping/pong/mempool, miningstate/getminingstate, sendheaders/feefilter, deprecated cf family + cfilterv2 + batched cfiltersv2, initstate/getinitstate, write-only reject (QK-0001), and the eight StakeShuffle mixing messages (`MsgMixPairReq`/`KeyExchange`/`Ciphertexts`/`SlotReserve`/`FactoredPoly`/`DCNet`/`Confirm`/`Secrets`) + `MixVect`. Frame differential: every message type × structured rounds byte-identical across all 4 networks + mutation verdict/kind parity, both for corrupted frames (which stop in the framing, at the checksum) and for payloads reframed with a valid checksum so every decoder sees them: random writes biased toward the var-ints, a count one past each per-message limit swept over the front of every payload, and every truncation (verdict and kind compared on each, io texts where both fail with one); 3 fuzz targets + proptest laws. Also ported: the mixing identity hashes and signed-data preimages (`WriteHash`/`WriteSignedData`) as `mix_hash`/`signed_data` on each mix message, pinned through the mixing crate's vectors (dcrd's `WriteHash` discards the encode error and hashes whatever prefix was written; for seven of the eight types the port surfaces that error instead, which is unreachable through decoding because every check that can fire is one dcrd also enforces at decode; `mixdcnet` is the exception, since an empty DC-net decodes but does not re-encode (QK-0010), so its `mix_hash`/`signed_data` write in dcrd's hashing mode, which skips the structural checks as `msgmixdcnet.go:130-145` does).  Decode error text matches dcrd wherever an RPC returns it: every error the tx, header, block and eight mixing message decoders produce, including the shared var-int, var-bytes, readScript and var-string readers, renders Go's `EOF`/`unexpected EOF` or dcrd's `MessageError` `Func: Description`. This is pinned against the oracle row by row for each coded check (`tests/review_decode_error_text.rs`) and by a mutation differential over mixing payloads (`frame_differential::mutated_mix_payloads_match_dcrd_text`). Kinds only other P2P codecs raise, the pver gate and `ReadMessage`'s framing errors print their kind name, and those texts reach only logs; the wrong-network check is the exception and renders dcrd's text, for example `ReadMessage: message from other network [TestNet3]`, naming the network through `CurrencyNet.String` (`Unknown CurrencyNet (<n>)` for an unknown magic; `tests/review_wrong_network_text.rs`).  `PowHashV2` is `BlockHeader::pow_hash_v2` (BLAKE3 over the serialized header per DCP0011, added with the standalone crate), used where dcrd uses it: the context-free proof-of-work check accepts either hash, as `checkProofOfWorkSanity` does, and the contextual check the one the BLAKE3 agenda selects. The 2.2 campaign (upstream master 452c1a6c) ports the version 12 surface: the `addrv2` message and typed `NetAddressV2` (IPv4/IPv6/TorV3, u64 timestamp with Go's overflow rejection, little-endian v2 port — unlike the legacy big-endian one — and dcrd's exact write-then-validate order so an erroring encode leaves the same partial bytes), the `ErrTooFewAddrs`/`ErrUnknownNetAddrType` kinds, `ADDR_V2_VERSION`, and the legacy `addr` retirement gates at v12.  The wire package has changed twice since `452c1a6c`. The first change is `SerializeSize` on every message (`e51406d1`, `29f17894`), ported as `Message::serialize_size`. It is exact against the encoder for every message (`message.rs` `serialize_size_is_the_encoded_length`). As in dcrd's `WriteMessageN`, `write_message` uses it to frame header and payload in one buffer. The outbound queue and the getdata send pipeline both charge with it, as dcrd's `msgSize` does, so no message is encoded just to be measured.  The second, `3dde63a8` at `6f6cf21b`, has `ReadOutPoint` refuse a negative outpoint tree once the tree byte is read and `WriteOutPoint` refuse one before writing, as `ErrNegativeTxTree` (`msgtx.go:1174-1218`). `read_out_point` and `write_out_point` (`msgtx.rs:184`, `:201`) carry it into every outpoint the crate reads or writes: the transaction prefix decoder (so blocks, mix confirmations, and the RPC, peer and database reads), the mix pair request UTXOs, and `MsgTx::encode_into`, which now returns a `Result`, so `write_message` refuses a tx or block message carrying one; where dcrd checks something ahead of the outpoint, the port does too (`msgmixpairreq.go:320` before `:325`, `msgmixconfirm.go:137` before `:148`). Pinned by dcrd's `TestOutPointSerialize` (its short-write rows, which need Go's `fixedWriter`, become boundary checks of the full encoding) and an oracle text differential (`tests/review_decode_error_text.rs` `negative_tree_errors_match_dcrd_text`). The new kind shifts dcrd's numeric error codes after it, which the port does not copy: kinds are compared by name. One divergence is kept on purpose: `MsgTx::serialize`, `tx_hash`, `tx_hash_full` and `MsgBlock::serialize` stay infallible and write a negative tree as its byte (`msgtx.rs:568`), where dcrd's `Bytes` returns `ErrNegativeTxTree` and its `TxHash` and `TxHashFull` panic (`mustHash`, `msgtx.go:418-429`). Making them fallible would reach a few hundred call sites, consensus code among them, for a difference nothing can reach: no decoder yields a negative tree, and every RPC that builds an input checks its tree first (`internal/rpcserver/rpcserver.go:815-816`, `:914-915`, `:1059`). The live oracle now refuses negative trees as well, so the stake, txscript and standalone differential generators draw trees from 0 to 2 instead of -1 to 1.  Decoders cap each pre-sized list at what the remaining payload bytes could hold (`capped_capacity`, `msgtx.rs`), where dcrd reserves the declared count. The capped lists are a transaction's prefix inputs, outputs and witness-only inputs (`msgtx.go` 648/677/726), the block's two transaction trees (`msgblock.go` 108/132), and the `inv`/`getdata`/`notfound`, `headers`, `miningstate` and `initstate` lists (`msginv.go` 64-65, `msggetdata.go` 56-57, `msgnotfound.go` 54-55, `msgheaders.go` 57-58, `msgminingstate.go` 85/106, `msginitstate.go` 101/120/139). A ten-byte `tx` declaring 3,728,271 outputs therefore reserves nothing instead of ~149 MB, and a list that decodes is reserved once, at exactly its length. Counts, verdicts and error texts are unchanged; only the address space of a failing decode differs.  One cargo feature, `count-header-hashes`, is for tests only and has no dcrd counterpart: it counts the calls of `BlockHeader::block_hash` on each thread (`header_hashes_on_this_thread`, `blockheader.rs:29`), so that a test in another crate can pin how often a path hashes a header (the `internal/blockchain` row has the counts). It needs `std`, so the crate is not no_std with it, and the no_std check builds without it.
