Only the dev-dependencies of `dcroxide-blockchain`, `dcroxide-database`, `dcroxide-netsync` and `dcroxide-node` name it, so it is off in every build made without dev-dependencies (`cargo build`, `cargo install`, a release build of the node) and on, in every crate of the build, in one made with them (`cargo test`, `--all-targets`), the `dcroxide` binary such a build makes included.  The advertised `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is 12, matching master's own `wire/protocol.go`, since the daemon's addrv2 handlers landed with the campaign's peer/addrmgr pieces.  Parity pinned by a mechanical behavior dump generated inside dcrd master over a full case sweep — every type, wrong lengths, unknown discriminators, count and timestamp bounds, version gates, and max payloads (`tests/data/addrv2_vectors.txt`) |
| `chaincfg` | `dcroxide-chaincfg` | vectors + diff | All four networks' `Params` ported field-for-field: genesis blocks built from the wire types (reproducing testnet's `TxHash`-instead-of-`TxHashFull` merkle root and simnet's zero-instead-of-null-sentinel genesis input witness), canonical agenda vote definitions shared across per-network deployment schedules, block-one ledgers extracted mechanically from `subsidytables.go` (mainnet: 3,146 payouts / 1,680,000 DCR). Parity pinned two ways: dcrd's own serialized-genesis test vectors + genesis hashes, and a canonical full-params dump compared byte-for-byte against dcrd's `chaincfg/v3` at the parity pin (the `v3.3.1-0.20261019113547-3360ae4dbef8` pseudo-version the oracle links) across all networks (every field, every deployment/vote/choice string, serialized genesis, BLAKE-256 ledger commitment). dcrd's init-time deployment validation (`init.go`) ported as data sanity tests; the validation `internal/blockchain` adds when it makes a chain's agendas (`makeAgendas`) is ported in `dcroxide-blockchain` and runs whenever a chain is constructed. The mainnet `Seeders` list carries `dcr-seed.jz.bz`, which is upstream since dcrd `b9b64533` (`mainnetparams.go:608`), so it is no divergence: the dump is compared unfiltered and the differential also asserts the entry's presence.  Not ported: deprecated `Checkpoints` (no network sets it), trivial Go-interface getter methods (fields are public), `DNSSeed.String`. The exceptions among the getters are the accessors through which dcrd's `*Params` satisfies an interface by renaming fields, each ported once as a trait impl for `Params`. The ten behind `standalone.SubsidyParams` (`params.go:678-750`, `BlockOneSubsidy` at `:809`; `VotesPerBlock` is `TicketsPerBlock`, the deprecated `WorkSubsidyProportion` is the pre-DCP0010 `WorkRewardProportion`) are this crate's `dcroxide_standalone::SubsidyParams` impl, and every subsidy cache in the node (block validation, mempool, template generation, RPC) is built over `Params` or `&Params` through it. The five `AddrID*V0` accessors behind `stdaddr.AddressParamsV0` (`params.go:642-672`; `AddrIDPubKeyV0` is `PubKeyAddrID`) are `dcroxide-txscript`'s `AddressParamsV0` impl (`stdaddr.rs:119`). The two impls sit on opposite ends of their dependency edges: this crate depends on `dcroxide-standalone`, so standalone stays free of the chain parameters as dcrd's `blockchain/standalone` module is (its `go.mod` requires only chainhash and wire), while `dcroxide-txscript` depends on this crate, so the parameters stay free of the script engine (dcrd's txscript module requires `chaincfg/v3` too, though only its tests import it). `tests/params_vectors.rs` `subsidy_params_follow_dcrd_accessors` pins the subsidy mapping and standalone's subsidy differential runs over it against dcrd's `*chaincfg.Params`; txscript's `stdaddr_differential` encodes and decodes every address kind through the address mapping on all four networks against dcrd |
| `txscript` (engine) | `dcroxide-txscript` | vectors + diff | The version-0 script engine ported from `txscript/v4 v4.1.2`: allocation-free tokenizer (exact `ErrMalformedPush` semantics for scripts under 2 GiB; dcrd's `int32` offsets past that are under Open), `ScriptNum` with minimal-encoding rules (and `MakeScriptNum`'s `uint8(8*i)` shift-count wrap, which only callers allowing inputs past eight bytes can see), the full 256-opcode table (generated from dcrd's `opcodeArray` and machine-verified entry-for-entry, incl. Decred's stake/treasury opcodes and the consensus-frozen `OP_SUBSTR`/rotation/shift quirks), the engine with all seven flags, P2SH (incl. stake-tagged) handling, conditional-execution tracking and clean-stack/push-only rules, split prefix/witness sighash under BLAKE-256 (the witness buffer hashed at dcrd's computed size, so an index with no input -- a transaction with no inputs, or an index past the last without AnyOneCanPay -- commits dcrd's zero padding), DER/pubkey/hashtype strict-encoding checks, `CHECKSIG`/`CHECKMULTISIG`/`CHECKSIGALT` across all three signature suites via `dcroxide-dcrec`, and the `ScriptBuilder`. Every error carries an `ErrorKind` whose `kind_name` matches dcrd's. Parity: dcrd's entire `script_tests.json` (2,285 rows), `tx_valid`/`tx_invalid`/`sighash.json` corpora ported verbatim and, from dcrd's `txscript/testdata`, `many_inputs_tx.hex` (verbatim) and block 432,100 (`block432100.hex`: dcrd's `block432100.bz2` decompressed and written as hex), whose mainnet signatures are run through the engine with and without the transaction hash (`tests/mainnet_signatures.rs`: 89 + 2 + 5 inputs, all verifying, the public key scripts rebuilt from the signature scripts), plus a live differential fuzzer (random scripts × random flags × versions) with a limit arm straddling each engine bound (255 counted opcodes, 1,024 stack items, 2,048-byte pushes and `OP_CAT` results, 16,384-byte signature and public key scripts), comparing verdict, kind and dcrd's error text, which `sendrawtransaction` returns to clients inside the rejection, and a sighash byte-equality differential over random txs (kind and text on failure); `txscript_engine` fuzz target. Also ported: `SigCache` — the bounded map memoizing successful verifications keyed by sigHash with random-entry eviction at capacity (the port compares raw signature/pubkey bytes instead of parsed forms, evicts one uniformly drawn entry in place of Go's random map start (a slot in a key vector kept beside the map, drawn by a keyed hash of a counter under a per-cache `RandomState`; a `HashMap`'s first key is always its lowest occupied bucket, which made each new entry the next victim once the cache had churned), reserves `maxEntries` only when the allocation succeeds (`try_reserve`, as Go's `make` hint does), so an unallocatable `--sigcachemaxsize` still starts the node, extends the cache to the Ed25519/Schnorr suites with a per-entry suite tag, consults it in `OP_CHECKSIG`/`OP_CHECKSIGALT` before parsing the key and signature rather than after (dcrd must parse first because its cache compares parsed values; an entry exists only for bytes that parsed and verified, so a hit implies both parses succeed and the verdict is unchanged), makes `add` and `exists` crate-private where dcrd exports `Add` and `Exists` (so no crate but txscript, whose engine adds an entry only after its own parse and verify, can create an entry the pre-parse lookup trusts; a `compile_fail` doctest in `sigcache.rs` pins this), exports `len` and `is_empty`, which dcrd's cache does not have and which only read, so that tests can count the entries a run left (those of *Signature pre-verification ahead of the block thread* do), and skips the proactive `EvictEntries` SipHash machinery — all result-invariant; hits and fresh verifies are indistinguishable, and failures are never cached; the sigcache unit tests pin, cold, warm and uncached, the Ed25519/Schnorr CHECKSIGALT caching, the raw-byte keying (including a non-canonical Ed25519 key encoding that shares one sig hash) and CHECKMULTISIG's shared sig-hash entries). Past dcrd, deliberately: the `SigHashAll` prefix-hash path that dcrd carries behind `optimizeSigVerification` and ships as `false` (`txscript/opcode.go:27-32`, with its warning that it has not been vetted; `sighash.go:296-305`) is taken when a caller hands the engine its transaction's hash (`Engine::set_tx_hash`). It is keyed as dcrd keys it: the masked hash type is `SigHashAll` and `SigHashAnyOneCanPay` is clear. For those hash types the prefix a signature hash commits to is byte for byte the no-witness serialization `MsgTx::tx_hash` hashes, so that hash stands in for the prefix hash; every other hash type, and every call without the hash (mempool acceptance and mining's per-transaction check through `validate_transaction_scripts`, signing, the treasury spend signature check, `calc_signature_hash_checked`/`calc_signature_hash_as_hash`, the public `check_block_scripts`), serializes and hashes the prefix as dcrd does. Only the block connect checks supply the hash, from the block's `BlockTxHashes`; the template checks are among them, through the public `check_connect_block`, which hashes its own block. Nothing is stored on a transaction: dcrd's `CachedTxHash` (`wire/msgtx.go:442-449`) is not ported. Unlike `SigCache::add`/`exists`, `Engine::set_tx_hash` is public, because its caller is another crate, and it compares the hash with its transaction's under debug assertions only: a release build rests on the pairing rule of `blockhashes.rs` (see *A `SigHashAll` signature hash at block connect* under Accepted below) |
| `txscript/stdaddr`, `txscript/stdscript` | `dcroxide-txscript` (`stdaddr`/`stdscript` modules) | vectors + diff | All seven version-0 address kinds (P2PK/P2PKH across ECDSA-secp256k1, Ed25519, and Schnorr-secp256k1, plus P2SH) as one enum mirroring dcrd's interface set (stake methods `None` for non-`StakeAddress` kinds); decode with dcrd's exact error kinds; a rejected address string is echoed through a port of Go's `strconv.Quote` (the `%q` verb), which carries a second copy of Go 1.26.5's `strconv.IsPrint` tables beside `dcroxide-dcrjson`'s (the same as go1.26.2's, the reference Go's; re-copy both when the reference moves to a release whose tables differ, as Go 1.27's, on Unicode 17.0.0, do), and an over-long address is cut at 54 bytes, as dcrd slices it; payment plus all six stake/treasury script constructors including the log2-encoded commitment fee limits. | `blockchain/stake` (tx primitives) | `dcroxide-stake` | vectors + diff | The transaction-level surface of `blockchain/stake/v5` at the parity pin `3360ae4d`, the v5.0.3 release (its two changes since `452c1a6c`, `036b7090` and `5dd4ca59`, are carried; `7e3dd854`, which prepares v5.0.3, changes only its module files and one test's copyright line): classification and format checks for all stake types (`CheckSStx`/`CheckSSGenVotes`/`CheckSSRtx` plus the TADD/TSPEND/treasury-base checks and `DetermineTxType`, all 72 error kinds with dcrd's names), SStx commitment/change extraction (`TxSStxStakeOutputInfo`, commitment address/amount/fee-limit decoding), SSGen extraction (block voted on, vote bits, vote version, `'TV'` treasury-vote discriminator), the `Hash256PRNG` ticket lottery (IV derivation, draw/rollover semantics, `uniformRandom`, `findTicketIdxs`), the 64.32 fixed-point vote/revocation reward calculations including the PRNG-driven remainder distribution for auto-revocations, with dcrd's wrapping int64 sums and signed `big.Int` semantics (Euclidean `Div`, flooring `Rsh`, `Int64` narrowing) reproduced for out-of-contract signed inputs, pinned by dcrd-generated rows and a signed live differential (`tests/review_stake_amount_math.rs`), and `CreateRevocationFromTicket` (whose out-of-range amount message prints `dcrutil.MaxAmount`, an untyped floating-point constant, as Go's `%v` does, `2.1e+15`; compared with the oracle's by `tests/oracle_differential.rs` `create_revocation_amount_range_text`). Parity: every `wire.MsgTx` vector from dcrd's `staketx_test.go` frozen with dcrd's own verdicts and replayed oracle-free (`tests/data/staketx_vectors.txt`, generated mechanically inside dcrd at the tag), the 100k-draw PRNG state vector and fixed reward tables from dcrd's tests, `treasury_test.go`'s TAdd, TSpend and treasurybase error and classification tables ported by hand (`tests/review_treasury_errors.rs`), plus live differentials: full-surface analysis dumps over structured near-valid tickets/votes/revocations/treasury txs and their mutations (output scripts and versions, input trees and indexes, signature script bytes biased to the TSpend shape's pinned offsets, and input values at the revocation zero-fee boundary), a sweep of every TSpend public key prefix byte, PRNG sequence + winner-selection byte-parity, reward amounts, and revocation construction byte-for-byte with error-kind parity. Also ported: the immutable ticket treap from `internal/tickettreap` — key-ordered by ticket hash and min-heap-ordered by the ticket height as the priority, with dcrd's exact rotation cascades (ties stay below on insert; the delete push-down picks the left child on equal priorities), structural sharing via reference counting in place of Go's garbage-collected pointers, iterative insert/delete rebuilds so degenerate sorted spines cannot exhaust the stack, the path of a descent kept in a stack of borrowed nodes that holds its first 64 entries in place and the rest in an overflow (`NodeStack`; dcrd's `parentStack` holds 128 in place, `common.go:116-191`, `:23`: the number decides when the stack allocates and never what it holds, and each entry carries the side the descent took, which dcrd recovers by comparing child pointers), keys compared as four big-endian words (`compare_keys`), which is the order of dcrd's `bytes.Compare` (`immutable.go:89`, `:168`, `:261`) computed without the call to the C library's `memcmp` that the array's own comparison makes at every node a descent visits, `ForEachByHeight`'s heap-pruned iteration, `FetchWinnersAndExpired` (incl. its empty-index panic), and dcrd's node size accounting fixed at 64-bit pointer widths; pinned by dcrd's own tickettreap test battery ported in full (empty/sequential/reverse/unordered/duplicate/early-stop/snapshot with exact size accounting and the heap invariant; the unordered keys come from a deterministic generator rather than Go's sha256 counter, and the nil-value no-op `Put` is unrepresentable with by-value semantics). Every treap an operation returns is the one the implementation before the borrowed path and the inline comparison returned, node for node, with the same nodes shared between versions: that implementation is kept as a test-only module, `src/tickettreap/reference.rs`, and held equal by `every_operation_builds_what_the_reference_builds` (random puts, replacements, deletes, takes and lookups over five live versions, every live version compared in its keys, values, priorities, sizes and shape, and in the nodes it shares with the others, after every step), `a_path_longer_than_the_stack_holds_in_place_is_rebuilt_whole` and `a_cascade_longer_than_the_stack_holds_in_place_is_folded_whole`, and the order by `keys_compare_as_their_bytes_do`. The stake node's connect changes its treaps in place: `Immutable::put_new_in_place` and `Immutable::take_in_place` make, in the version they are called on, the version `put_new` and `take` return, changing a node where it stands when no other version holds it and copying it first otherwise (`Arc::make_mut`), so no version anyone else holds ever changes, on any thread. Each descends once, comparing keys and changing nothing, and records the side it took at each level, then follows the record down to change the path. The insert keeps above the new node every node of its path whose priority is at most the new one's and splits the subtree below them by the key, which is the shape dcrd's rotation cascade builds (`immutable.go:186-243`); the removal merges the removed node's subtrees top-down with dcrd's push-down choice, the left on equal priorities (`immutable.go:311-366`), and puts a side left alone in place as it stands, where dcrd walks on down its spine and rebuilds each node unchanged. A removal copies at most what the reference copies and an insert exactly that, on a version every node of which is shared; on one that holds its nodes alone, neither copies a node. Pinned by `every_operation_in_place_builds_what_the_reference_builds` (inserts, refused inserts, removals and removals of missing keys over the eight profiles `every_operation_builds_what_the_reference_builds` runs, each on a clone of one of five live versions or on a version taken out of them, compared node for node with the reference's `put` and `delete`, every other live version unchanged node for node after every step, and every node a version held alone still where it stood) and `an_operation_in_place_takes_a_path_of_any_length` (spines of 600 nodes, more than the 256 levels the record of a descent's sides holds in place, and the root removed from between two spines of 300). Also ported: the ticket database serialization formats from `internal/ticketdb` — the database info row (LE version with the upgrade bit, 32-bit truncated creation time), the best chain state (hash ‖ height ‖ live ‖ missed ‖ revoked ‖ per-block ‖ winner list, incl. the panic on more winners than the per-block count and zero-filled winner slots past the provided list), per-block undo data (37-byte entries with the packed missed/revoked/spent/expired flag byte), ticket hash lists, the individual ticket bucket row value, all nine error kinds with dcrd's names, and the bucket/key name constants; the `database.Tx`-coupled wrappers are now ported as the ticket database persistence layer over the dcroxide database (ffldb's bucket layout over redb, with the per-height undo and maturing tickets rows keyed by big-endian height where dcrd's keys are little-endian, `stakedb::height_key`; see "Seven per-block buckets" under Known remaining gaps): every `Db*` function except `DbRemoveAllBuckets` (`DbCreate` with a caller-supplied creation date since dcrd stamps the wall clock, the database info, best chain state, per-height undo and maturing tickets rows, ticket bucket rows incl. `DbDeleteTicket`'s read-before-delete `ErrMissingKey` check, so a bucket move over on-disk ticket state that has drifted from the stake node aborts the block's update as in dcrd, and `DbLoadAllTickets`) plus the four entry points — `InitDatabaseState`, `LoadBestNode` (tip verification, treap reconstruction with count corruption checks, and the lottery final state recomputed from the tip header), `WriteConnectedBestNode` (the undo-driven bucket moves), and `WriteDisconnectedBestNode` (the above-tip row drops and the reverse-order undo application). Pinned by dcrd's own ticketdb serialization tests ported with their exact hex vectors, plus a persistence replay generated over a real ffldb inside dcrd's package: sixty connected blocks with misses, revocations, and expiries, ten disconnects restoring bookkeeping through the database rows, every bucket row compared byte for byte at five checkpoints (the two per-height buckets' keys after turning dcrd's little-endian heights into the port's big-endian ones; every value, and every other key, as dumped), and `LoadBestNode` round trips matching on pool size, winners, missed tickets, undo data, and the recomputed final state. A missing ticket database bucket, where dcrd dereferences nil, reports `ErrUninitializedBucket` naming the bucket; both this and the `ErrMissingKey` check are pinned in `tests/review_stakedb_integrity.rs`. A best-node write (`write_connected_best_node`, `write_disconnected_best_node`) looks each of the live, missed and revoked ticket buckets up once, when a ticket first needs it, and keeps the handle for the rest of the call, where dcrd's `DbDeleteTicket` and `DbPutTicket` look their bucket up for every ticket (`internal/ticketdb/chainio.go:609`, `:625`); here each lookup is answered from the copy of the bucket index the database holds in memory (*Nested bucket lookup, and the bucket index at open* under Deliberate divergences), so the handles save a lookup in that map for every ticket a block moves; before that copy, each was a keyed read of the bucket index inside the block's write transaction. No step of such a write creates or removes a bucket, so the rows written and deleted, their order and the errors are unchanged: a missing bucket is reported at the first ticket that needs it, with the rows of the tickets before it staged, and never by a write that needs none. `db_put_ticket` and `db_delete_ticket` themselves still look the bucket up on every call. No lookup reads the store any more, so no read fault reaches a ticket bucket's lookup, in a best-node write or outside one: `ErrUninitializedBucket` is reported only for a bucket the bucket index does not hold. The fault the handles once made a call see differently (a read fault reaching a ticket bucket's index row after that bucket's first lookup in the call, which the next ticket's lookup had reported as `ErrUninitializedBucket`) is seen by no lookup, with the handles or without them; where the row itself cannot be read, dcrd's lookup finds no bucket and this one finds it, and a fault in the pages of the ticket rows is met by the reads of those rows, `DbDeleteTicket`'s read before its delete among them (*Nested bucket lookup, and the bucket index at open*, (a)). Pinned in the same file by `a_best_node_write_moves_tickets_as_one_named_call_a_ticket_does`, `a_missing_ticket_bucket_stops_the_write_where_a_ticket_first_needs_it` and `each_kind_of_move_alone_stops_over_its_own_missing_bucket`. It was measured with the two exists-address index changes of 2026-10-12 and not on its own (the `internal/blockchain/indexers` row has the figures). `ResetDatabase` and `DbRemoveAllBuckets` are not ported: nothing in dcrd calls `ResetDatabase`, not even its tests, and `DbRemoveAllBuckets` is called only by it. Also ported: the ticket pool state machine from `tickets.go` — the per-block stake `Node` over the live/missed/revoked treaps with `genesisNode`, `connectNode` (winner voting/missing, expiry sweeps via the heap-pruned iteration, revocations, ticket maturation, and the lottery draw of the next winners plus the 6-byte final state from the PRNG state hash), `disconnectNode` (reverse undo-data replay restoring the parent's winners and final state), the accessor surface (`SpentByBlock`/`MissedByBlock`/`ExpiredByBlock`/`ExpiringNextBlock` etc.), and `fetchWinners`; the database-coupled entry points are the `stakedb` ones above; disconnecting takes the parent's undo data and ticket list directly (the chain fetches those rows itself when the parent node holds none), making the `ErrMissingDatabaseTx` fallback unrepresentable. `safePut` and `safeDelete` each make one descent here (`Immutable::put_new` and `Immutable::take` in a disconnect, `put_ticket` and `delete_ticket` over the operations in place in a connect) where dcrd asks `Has` and descends again (`tickets.go:487-511`), and a winner's or a revocation's `safeGet` followed by `safeDelete` is one descent (`take_ticket`) where dcrd makes three (`tickets.go:552-576`, `:644-651`); an operation in place then walks the path it recorded once more, comparing no key. The error kinds, their texts and the order a connect or a disconnect reports them in are dcrd's: a winner or a revocation that is missing is refused with `safeGet`'s text, the first dcrd reaches, and `safeDelete`'s own cannot follow a lookup that found the ticket in the same treap. A connect changes the child's treaps in place, where dcrd's builds a new version at every transition: `Node::connect` starts from clones of the parent's treaps, so the first change to each copies its path and the later changes of the same connect find those copies the child's own; `Node::connect_in_place` takes the parent node and changes the parent's own treaps, for a connect that drops the parent's node when it ends (the `internal/blockchain` row). A connect in place records every ticket it moves with the value the ticket held. A move that takes a ticket out of one treap and is refused by the next puts it back; a block the treaps refuse has the recorded moves undone and the parent returned with dcrd's error (`InPlaceRefusal`); and `ParentRestore::rebuild` undoes them on copies of the child's treaps, should the block fail after its stake node was made. The parent back from either holds every ticket in the treap it was in with the value it held, recorded rather than read back from the undo flags, so it answers every lookup, walk and index as before; its treaps' shapes can differ where heights tie, as a ticket taken out and put back goes below its equals, which a node regenerated by `disconnectNode` can do as well, and nothing reads a treap's shape. Pinned by `the_helpers_return_what_the_lookup_and_the_operation_return` and the four connect and disconnect tests beside it in `src/ticketnode.rs`, and by `every_connect_makes_the_node_the_connect_by_versions_makes`, which holds `connect` and `connect_in_place` to the connect as it was before (kept beside the tests as `connect_by_versions`) over 4,000 random parents and blocks, with tied heights, expiring tickets, odd flags and every kind of refusal: the same node field for field and treap node for treap node, or the same error; the parent back from a refusal or a rebuild answering as the original; every ticket a connect in place left where it was still in the node that held it; and a clone of the parent, a handle on its missed tickets or a clone of its live treap, kept across the connect, unchanged node for node. The borrowed path, the inline comparison and the single descents were measured together on m3 on 2026-10-13, as one step of a series of builds and one run against one: a default mainnet sync took 458.1 s against 466.7 s, 6.9 s less with the flushes' commit seconds taken out and 7.5 s less by the node's clock less its flush time, which a step between two single runs resolves only to 3.5 to 6 s either way, and the block thread's CPU time outside its flushes fell by 7.7 s; every build of that series held a developer variable this tree does not have, so the code as committed was first run in a sync as committed inside `8fc9160`, four times on 2026-10-16 at a median of 430.7 s, where its own step was not measured apart (docs/bench-ledger.md). Pinned by 153 frozen state vectors from dcrd's own package: 120 connected blocks of purchases, votes with misses, revocations, and expiries over a small parameter set followed by 30 disconnects, comparing pool sizes, lottery final states, winner lists, and complete undo data at every step, plus the connect error kinds. The expiry accessors, `ExpiringNextBlock` (the DCP0009 revocation set) included, along with `ExistsExpiredTicket` and `SpentByBlock`/`MissedByBlock`/`ExpiredByBlock`, are compared at every one of those 150 steps against rows generated by dcrd's package from the same replay (`tests/review_ticketnode_expiring.rs`). The node's height gates (`connectNode`'s stake-enable and next-winner gates, `disconnectNode`'s, `LoadBestNode`'s, and the winner copies in `WriteConnectedBestNode`/`WriteDisconnectedBestNode`) compare at dcrd's width, truncating the `int64` parameter to `uint32` as `uint32(StakeValidationBeginHeight()-1)` does (`tickets.go:420`, `:537`, `:705`, `:892`, `:998`, `:1136`). They once compared in `i64`, which differs only when the compared value does not fit a `uint32`: with a stake validation height of 0 the port ran the lottery at every height and failed on the empty pool, where dcrd's gate never opens. No network has such parameters; `tests/review_stake_height_gates.rs` pins the width against results taken from dcrd's package. Also ported: `checkTicketRedeemers` (votes must spend lottery winners, revocations must spend missed or expired tickets, and under the automatic revocations agenda every newly missed or expired ticket must be revoked; the nondeterministic error choice among multiple missing revocations in dcrd's map iteration is avoided in the vectors by crafting at most one), pinned together with the completed header stake commitments by 56 vectors incl. a real dcrd stake node built over a throwaway database attached to the shell chain. Also ported: `checkBlockContext` — the full contextual block assembly: `determineCheckTxFlags` (all five agenda flags), the coinbase/treasurybase first-transaction and unique-height rules, per-transaction context checks with the second-coinbase/second-treasurybase and misplaced-tree rules, the stake tallies (vote target block and vote-bits approval congruence, header vote/revocation/fresh-stake commitments, early-stake restrictions, treasury add and revocation bounds), cumulative quick sigop counting, the merkle roots (since `ddd3b6d2` right after the header context checks, against the variant the header commitments agenda selects, and skipped for a block the recent merkle checks hold) and the agenda-driven `maxBlockSize` (both on the fast-add path too since `ead8ba7a`), and under full validation transaction finality (past-median-time under the LN features agenda), the ticket redeemer checks against the parent stake node, and the treasury spend vote-interval/expiry-window rules — pinned by 36 crafted simnet blocks over 16 verdict kinds with the parent stake node rebuilt step-for-step through the ported ticket state machine (154 recorded connects whose pool size and final state match dcrd's exactly, doubling as a large-scale cross-validation of the state machine). dcrd's recent-context-checks cache (`recentContextChecks`, a 25-entry LRU of block hashes filled by `maybeAcceptBlocks` and the reorg attach loop, and cleared by `InvalidateBlock`/`ReconsiderBlock`) is reproduced in `process.rs`. It is not a pure optimization: a block linked by a fast-added parent is checked with the fast-add flags on acceptance and then skips the full-flag context checks on attach (QK-0015). Its sibling `recentMerkleChecks` (`b56e78f5`), 25 blocks whose data commitment is proven, is reproduced beside it; both are `LruHashSet`s. Also ported: `checkTransactionsAndConnect` — the per-tree connect loop composing the cumulative sigop checks, the fee-computing input validation with fraud proofs, and the view connection, then enforcing the vote-scaled fee penalty, the coinbase subsidy commitment and overpayment rules (`ErrBadCoinbaseAmountIn`/`ErrBadCoinbaseValue`), the treasurybase subsidy commitment, the empty-stake-tree rules, and the stakebase payout bound — plus `consensusScriptVerifyFlags` (agenda-driven script flags for CSV/SHA256/treasury) — pinned by 9 vectors over prepared simnet views with valid coinbase/vote/ticket/treasurybase trees and targeted mutations, incl. spend journal lengths. `ErrBadStakebaseValue` is unreachable with exact-payment votes and is exercised only through its bound; noted. Also ported: `checkConnectBlock` — the final connect battery: the treasury payout fork (`checkTreasuryBase` under the agenda, `coinbasePaysTreasuryAddress` before it), the treasury spend checks (`tspendChecks` — supplied by the chain engine through a closure seam since the duplicate-mine lookup, vote tallies, and expenditure bound need prior-block data; the seam is a required parameter, so chainless callers must pass the stateless vote-interval/expiry-window subset (`tspend_checks_stateless`) by name rather than falling back to it by omission), the disapproved-parent undo, both tree connects with the stake-tree fee carry-forward, sequence lock enforcement over both trees under the LN features agenda, the version 2 GCS filter built over the post-connect view, its previous scripts read from the spend journal the connect built where there is one (the `internal/blockchain` row's *Filter scripts*), and checked against the header stake root once header commitments are active (`CalcCommitmentRootV1` is the identity over the sole leaf), then handed back to the caller for storage, as dcrd's `hdrCommitments` out-param does, so each fully validated block builds its filter once, `checkBlockScripts` (engine execution with the coinbase-input and version-2-auto-revocation skips across the script workers (the script pool or the scoped workers, as *Script checks of a connected block* under Deliberate divergences records), each engine sharing the chain's `SigCache` exactly as dcrd threads its `sigCache` into `NewEngine`), `blockOneCoinbasePaysTokens` against the block one ledger, and `calculateAddedSubsidy`; script execution is gated by a caller-supplied flag standing in for dcrd's bulk-import mode and assumed-valid ancestry — pinned by 35 vectors over a real 154-node simnet chain built inside dcrd's own package: a fully valid block connecting with a byte-identical filter hash and spend journal, the natural bad-commitment-root verdict, all four treasurybase kinds, coinbase and vote corruption propagating from the tree connects, an unmet relative sequence lock, the treasury spend window/value-in kinds, the pre-treasury organization payout kinds, the complete block one ledger battery, added-subsidy totals, and script validation over both trees with the auto-revocation skip both honored and refused.  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master `452c1a6c`) ports the stake treasury delta: `CheckTAdd`/`CheckTSpend`/`CheckTreasuryBase` rewritten to master — the private wrapper layer collapsed, every message restated in master's wording, the count checks split per failure mode (inputs before outputs), and the treasurybase null-outpoint check hoisted ahead of the output validations — same acceptance sets throughout, with the one failure-precedence change pinned natively (`treasurybase_null_outpoint_precedence`).  dcrd's `IsTAdd`, `IsTSpend` and `IsTreasuryBase` are their checks with the error dropped (`treasury.go:120-122`, `:227-230`, `:316-318`), and each check begins with the transaction's version and its input and output counts and formats a message when one of them fails (`:56-71`, `:142-158`, `:244-260`). The port's `is_tadd`, `is_tspend` and `is_treasury_base` test the version and those counts themselves and run the check only on a transaction that passes; the test is the condition of the check's first three failures and nothing more, so the answers are the checks'. `tspend_signature_and_pub_key` is a port-only function with the same test that returns what `check_tspend` returns without its error, for `check_transaction_inputs`, which reads `CheckTSpend`'s error only as a refusal (`internal/blockchain/validate.go:3473-3474`). `check_tadd`, `check_tspend` and `check_treasury_base` are unchanged, and `is_sstx`, `is_ssgen` and `is_ssrtx` still build a message for each transaction they refuse. Pinned by `the_predicates_answer_as_their_checks_do` (the 31 transactions of dcrd's `staketx_test.go`, a valid transaction of each treasury form and a regular one, each as it is and with one to six fields edited, more than 100,000 in all, which reach every error kind of the three checks) and by the four tables of `treasury_test.go` in `tests/review_treasury_errors.rs`; one of the seven changes the `internal/blockchain` row measures together.  The `checktreasurybase` analysis row was excluded from the differential while the oracle still linked v2.1.5; the oracle now links stake at the parity pin and the row is compared again |
| `blockchain/standalone` | `dcroxide-standalone` | vectors + diff | The full package at `blockchain/standalone/v2` at the parity pin `3360ae4d` (its one change since `452c1a6c`, `ae4c5818`, is not observable; see above): merkle roots (copying and in-place variants, transaction trees over full hashes, the DCP0005 combined root), merkle inclusion proof generation/verification (incl. the 32-level proof-size cap), compact-bits conversions over arbitrary-precision signed integers with dcrd's exact semantics (negative encodings, beyond-256-bit exponents, and the floor-shift mantissa quirk for negatives), `CalcWork`, the three proof-of-work check functions with dcrd's error kinds, the DCP0011 ASERT difficulty algorithm (64.16 fixed-point + cubic approximation, ported step for step incl. Go's lossy `Int64` narrowing on extreme exponents; for shift counts of 8192 or more, where dcrd always shifts and then clamps, the port clamps directly, and only where that is provably exact: any right shift, and a left shift while the limit has at most 8208 bits. The shortcut is pinned against dcrd on both sides of both bounds and live out to 10,000 half-lives (`tests/review_asert_shift_bounds.rs`)), the complete subsidy cache (all three split regimes across V1/V2/V3 APIs, vote/treasury variants), treasury spend expiry/window math, and coinbase/treasury-base identification + `CheckTransactionSanity` (whose amount range messages print `maxAtoms`, an untyped floating-point constant, as Go's `%v` does, `2.1e+15`; `tests/treasury_tx_vectors.rs` `check_transaction_sanity_amount_range_texts`). Its duplicate-input search compares up to 16 inputs with one another on the whole hash, the index and the tree, which allocates nothing, and files the inputs of a transaction with more in an ordered set, where dcrd fills a map for every transaction (`tx.go:218-226`); the verdict and the error, which names no input, are the same (`has_duplicate_inputs`, pinned by `tests/review_duplicate_inputs.rs`: every count from 1 to 40 inputs with the equal pair at every two positions, outpoints one bit apart in the hash, the index or the tree, and 20,000 random transactions, each judged as the set judges it; it belongs to one of the seven changes the `internal/blockchain` row measures together). `wire.BlockHeader` gained `pow_hash_v2` (BLAKE3 per DCP0011). Parity: every dcrd test table ported (large tables extracted mechanically), dcrd's ASERT reference vectors replayed verbatim, all four total-supply schedule walks land on dcrd's exact totals, plus live differentials over random trees/txs/bits/ASERT params/subsidy surfaces/treasury windows and both pow hashes. Uses `num-bigint` for the big.Int-based API (documented in the crate docs); dcrd's RWMutex'd subsidy cache is `&mut self` here (result-invariant), and `SubsidyParams` is also implemented for any `&T` whose `T` implements it, so a cache can borrow its parameters where dcrd's holds a `*chaincfg.Params` pointer. The legacy EMA difficulty retarget lives in dcrd's `internal/blockchain`; its port is `calc_next_blake256_diff` in `dcroxide-blockchain`.  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master `452c1a6c`) ports the standalone delta: `CheckTransactionSanity` gained the per-input fraud-proof value range checks (`ErrFraudAmountIn` for negative — the `NullValueIn` sentinel excepted — and above-max input value commitments), a new kind the chain layer maps alongside the newly-mapped `ErrInvalidTSpendExpiry`→`ErrInvalidTreasurySpendExpiry` |
| `blockchain/fullblocktests` | `dcroxide-blockchain` (vectors) | vectors | Not ported as code: dcrd's generator is the oracle for `tests/data/fullblock_vectors.txt`, dumped by `tools/fullblockgen` running inside the package at the parity pin. Mapped here so `tools/pinbump` resolves it instead of reporting it unmapped — a delta touching this package changes the expectations the replay asserts, which is exactly how `a38c0195` surfaced. The battery seeds its block timestamps from the wall clock, so a regeneration rewrites every block hash in the file; the row counts and the reject kinds are the reviewable content, not the diff. Regenerated at `036b7090` and again at `6f6cf21b`, where `2bd17b22`, `70ba63ea` and `90accde2` made `brt7` a rejected block, renamed the accepted block it had been to `brt8`, and added `bti1` to `bti3`: 205 accept, 353 reject, 3 orphan-or-reject, 14 tip, 1 non-canonical and 1 skipped row. Not regenerated at `382ff4dd`, where the package and the code it links are unchanged. `tools/fullblockgen` sat at `036b7090` through the `b9634e01` move because nothing checked its `go.mod`; `the_dcrd_pin_matches_ci_and_the_oracle` now does. |
| `gcs` (+ `blockcf2`) | `dcroxide-gcs` | vectors + diff | The full `gcs/v4 v4.1.1` package: version 1 and 2 Golomb-coded set filters (bit-exact writer/reader, SipHash-2-4 via the `siphasher` crate matching dchest/siphash, the v1 modulo and v2 multiply-shift reductions, v2 empty-entry skipping and hash deduplication, big-endian-uint32 vs varint entry-count serialization), `Match`/`MatchAny`/`Hash`, `FromBytes` for both versions with dcrd's error kinds, `MakeHeaderForFilter`, `MaxFilterV2Size`, and the DCP0005 version 2 block committed filters (`blockcf2`: B=19/M=784931, merkle-root keying, the full entry-selection rules across regular and stake trees incl. ticket commitment→payment script conversion and the zero-value change exclusions). Parity: dcrd's `TestFilter` serialization vectors (bytes, hashes and member matches of every version 1 and 2 case, `tests/filter_vectors.rs`) and `TestBitWriter` vectors (`bits::tests`), and live differentials over random entry sets/keys for both versions (serialization, hashes, match verdicts) and over structured blocks with real ticket/vote/revocation/treasury transactions and randomized previous-script sets incl. missing-script error parity. The bitstream writer gathers bits in a 64-bit word and appends whole words, where dcrd's `bitWriter` writes a bit at a time into the last byte and whole bytes once a write reaches a byte boundary; the stream is identical, which unit tests check against a copy of dcrd's bit-at-a-time writer (every write width and unary quotients across word boundaries after every count of bits already written, random write runs, and random filters of both versions), and a one-off check outside the tree on 2026-10-17 built every block filter of mainnet to height 1,116,035 with both writers and found them byte-identical. `blockcf2::regular_with_types` (`blockcf2.rs:257`) is `Regular` handed the type of each stake transaction, which the chain classifies once per block (the `internal/blockchain` row's *Transaction types*), where dcrd's `Regular` classifies each itself (`blockcf.go:338`); `regular` classifies as dcrd's does. The two build the same filter, which `tests/oracle_differential.rs` holds over 600 blocks drawn from the differential's generator, with no oracle, and a one-off check outside the tree on 2026-10-19 built every block filter of mainnet to height 1,116,035 with the crate at `797a213` and both ways with this one and found them byte-identical. The typed build panics in every build on a list that is not one type per stake transaction, and under debug assertions on a type that is not its transaction's. The list of entries is allocated once, at the number of inputs and outputs of both trees, which bounds the entries a block can commit (`entries_bound`, `blockcf2.rs:471`), where dcrd starts its slice at `numEntriesHint` and grows it; the commitment converter keeps dcrd's hint for its own buffer, and no filter byte depends on either. The same whole-chain check, run again with the list so sized, found every filter byte-identical to the crate's at `797a213`. `blockcf2::regular_by_input` (`blockcf2.rs:275`) builds the filter from a source that gives each previous script by the place of the input that spends it (`InputScripts`, `:198`), which dcrd has no counterpart for: its `Regular` looks each script up by its outpoint, as `regular` and `regular_with_types` do here through the same build. The chain hands it the spend journal its connect built (the `internal/blockchain` row's *Filter scripts*); `tests/oracle_differential.rs` holds it to `regular` over 600 blocks drawn from the differential's generator, through a source that finds each input at the place the build names. The list so sized and the build by input over the chain's spend journal were first run in syncs on 2026-10-19, in R1, the build the `internal/blockchain` row's *Transaction types* describes, which holds both, that classification and one other change; R1's step is the four together and not the effect of either, and no block filter was compared after those syncs (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Where `797a213`'s block thread waits, a deeper read-ahead and a rung of four changes"). Confirmed quirk (reproduced against dcrd live): dcrd's `MatchAny` panics with an index out of range when the search data contains empty entries alongside a filter entry above every hashed value; this port returns false for that case instead — the outcome for every non-panicking input is identical, and the panic is unreachable through the block filter paths |
| `database` (+ `ffldb`) | `dcroxide-database` | semantics | The `database/v3 v3.0.3` interface semantics over redb 4.3.0 per accepted [ADR-0004](adr/0004-storage-backend.md): all 22 error kinds with dcrd's names plus a 23rd, `Fatal`/`ErrFatal`, that dcrd has no counterpart for (the fail-closed latch, in the divergence table below), transactions (managed `view`/`update` with dcrd's panic-on-commit/rollback semantics, unmanaged `begin`, single-writer/multi-reader, snapshot isolation, read-your-writes), buckets and cursors over ffldb's key layout (4-byte bucket IDs, `bidx` bucket index rows, `bidx-cbid` counter) so iteration order and nested-bucket behaviors match ffldb observably, with one exception that reaches the interface: the internal block index row is keyed by the block's big-endian height and then its hash where ffldb keys it by the hash (`ffldb/db.go:1142`, `:1239`, `:1717`), so `has_block`, `fetch_block`, `fetch_block_header`, `fetch_block_region` and their plural forms take the block's height beside its hash (see "Seven per-block buckets" under Known remaining gaps), plus a windowed cursor ffldb has no counterpart for: ffldb's is a pair of lazy merged iterators, so dcrd's batched index drop can restart it per batch for free, while this one materializes the keys it walks and restarting it once re-read the whole bucket (66,494,886 rows for mainnet's `existsaddridx`) — every scan, windowed or not, is one merge join of the store range, the overlay's merged layers and the transaction's pending puts, with its pending deletions as a mask, pulled only as far as the window reaches, so a window costs O(limit) wherever its keys live. `Bucket::for_each` streams in 4,096-row windows and reads each value once, and `Cursor::delete` parks the deleted value lazily rather than reading it (ffldb's `Delete` reads nothing). A store read error ends the store's side of a scan while the overlay and pending rows carry on, as ffldb's merged cursor treats a goleveldb iterator error as exhaustion and `ForEach` never checks it. redb's range iterator instead repeats `PreviousIo` forever, so a scan that skipped errors had spun. Also ported: flat `*.fdb` block files in dcrd's exact record byte format (LE network ‖ LE length ‖ block ‖ CRC-32C), block index rows as location ‖ header with headers always served from metadata, pending-block visibility within a transaction, and `reconcileDB` recovery on both `open` and `create`, as dcrd runs it for both: block-file bytes past the stored write cursor are rolled back, so `create` over leftover `*.fdb` files deletes all but the first and truncates it, as dcrd's create-time reconcile does. A block region is read at dcrd's `uint32` file offset: the record's offset, its 8-byte prefix and the region offset are summed with `readBlockRegion`'s wrap (`blockio.go:582`), so a corrupt or crafted block index location whose sum passes 4 GiB reads the bytes at the wrapped offset as dcrd does, where the port's `u64` sum once failed at end of file (`blockfile::tests::a_region_read_offset_wraps_at_u32_as_dcrd_computes_it`). Block data short of the cursor is `ErrCorruption` with dcrd's text, and a store with no cursor is `ErrCorruption` "write cursor does not exist". It also ports `handleRollback`'s reposition of the write cursor whatever fails. A block record goes to its file in one positioned write at the write cursor (`BlockStore::write_block`, `blockfile.rs:425`, through `write_block_as`, `:451`; a short count or an interrupted call is continued where it stopped), where dcrd's `writeBlock` makes four `WriteAt` calls, one a field (`blockio.go:466-489`). The bytes in the file, the rollover to the next file and the error are dcrd's: a failed write is `ErrDriverSpecific` with the text `failed to write <field> to file N at offset O: <err>`, and the field named is the one holding the first byte that did not land, with the offset that field starts at, which is what dcrd's call for that field reports. A filesystem that cuts one large write at a different byte than it would cut four small ones can therefore name a different field than dcrd would for the same fault. The seek that came before the four writes is gone, and with it the port's own `failed to seek write file` error. The cursor after a failed write is not dcrd's, and was not before the single write either: the port advances the write cursor by every byte that landed, those of the failing write included, so that a rollback finds a torn record, where dcrd's `writeData` advances it by the count Go's `WriteAt` returns (`blockio.go:386-387`), which is 0 for a call that failed whatever it had written, so dcrd's cursor stops at the start of the field that failed. The outcome differs in one case: the write fails inside the 4-byte network field after 1 to 3 of its bytes landed while the cursor is still on the rollback point (the first record of the commit, with no rollover); dcrd's rollback is then a no-op (`blockio.go:658-660`) that leaves those bytes past its cursor, and the port's truncates them. Pinned in `blockfile::tests` by `the_block_files_hold_the_records_dcrd_writes`, `a_block_record_is_written_with_one_call`, `a_short_or_interrupted_write_is_continued_where_it_stopped`, `a_failed_write_reports_what_landed_before_it`, `a_stopped_write_is_blamed_on_the_field_holding_the_next_byte` and, on Linux, `a_record_write_that_fails_part_way_names_the_field_it_stopped_in`, which tears the write with a file size limit. The single write was measured as one of a batch of four and not on its own, the others being the wait timer described further on and two changes in `dcroxide-blockchain` (the best state's missed tickets held as a handle, in the `internal/blockchain` row, and an inline comparison for the keys of the UTXO view's and the UTXO cache's ordered maps, with a view committed to the cache in one pass, which changes nothing dcrd can observe; the same row has its key type, the one public type it changed and its tests): on m3 on 2026-10-12, on a quiet machine, one default mainnet sync to block 1,116,035 took 673.8 s with the four (`a83f9a1`) and 728.5 s in the run before it without them (the code of `b4a5d2d`), 54.7 s or 7.5% less, one run each, and the node made 5.86 million write calls where it had made 9.15 million. A block can also reach the store already laid out as its record (`BlockRecord`, `blockfile.rs:143`, made only by `BlockRecord::lay_out`, `:186`, which writes the network and the block's length into the eight bytes ahead of the block in the caller's buffer, appends the CRC-32C and takes the hash of the record's header): `Transaction::store_block_record` (`transaction.rs:920`) makes `store_block_raw`'s checks in its order, refusing a hash that is not the record's header's with the same text, and the commit writes the record as it stands (`BlockStore::write_block_record`, `blockfile.rs:436`), the same bytes at the same place through the same one positioned write, with its rollover, its error texts and its cursor rule. A record laid out for another network than the store's is not written; the store lays out its own from the block the record holds. `Database::records_written_as_laid_out` (`lib.rs:3127`) counts the records written as they were handed in. dcrd has none of the three: `StoreBlock` writes the bytes its block was made from (`ffldb/db.go:1175`, `dcrutil/block.go:54-58`), and `writeBlock` takes the checksum as it writes them at the commit (`blockio.go:466-489`). The one caller is the chain's acceptance of block data, for a block a connection's reader thread laid out (*Reading ahead of the block handler* under Deliberate divergences). Pinned by `a_block_stored_as_a_laid_out_record_is_stored_as_store_block_stores_it` and `a_laid_out_record_is_refused_as_mismatched_bytes_are` (`tests/blockstore.rs`), `a_commit_writes_the_record_it_was_handed` and `a_laid_out_record_is_written_as_it_stands_for_its_own_network_only` (`blockfile::tests`), and the power-loss, rollback and torn-write tests (`tests/crash.rs`, `tests/review_storage_recovery.rs`), which store records as well as blocks. dcrd's BCDB lines for all of it (`Detected unclean shutdown - Repairing...`, `Database sync complete`, `***Database corruption detected***`, and the `ROLLBACK:` debug line and warnings) go through `Options::log`. The daemon points it at `BCDB` as dcrd's `log.go:88` does, and addblock does the same as dcrd's `cmd/addblock/addblock.go:76` does (`dcroxide_node::logging::bcdb_log_sink`). `create` refuses with `ErrDbExists` a directory that holds dcrd's own `metadata/` leveldb store: dcrd's create refuses its own existing metadata store (`ErrorIfExist`), and both daemons name the directory `blocks_ffldb`, so without the check a dcroxide pointed at a dcrd data directory would roll back and delete dcrd's block files. Open-time failures that redb reports as corruption (header validation, a repair that finds every root corrupted, an aborted repair) are `ErrCorruption` as `convertErr` would make them. A file that is not a redb database at all stays `ErrDriverSpecific`. A data directory on an older redb file format is refused on open with an upgrade message rather than misread: redb 4 reads only file format 3 and there is no in-place migration, so such a directory has to be re-synced (the chain is not damaged). No directory in an older format exists to refuse — dcroxide has never been released — so the message is held by its own wording and not by a test. Parity instrument: the ported ffldb interface-test battery plus a 12-test crash-consistency rig (torn-write and missing-data tears, randomized tear soak, cross-bucket atomicity over the multi-bucket commit `Chain::flush` makes, both desync directions of its state markers, a kill between the block-file sync and the metadata commit, and deletes torn from the puts beside them) and an ENOSPC fault-injection test that fills a 2 MiB filesystem under a live database (Linux-only, and CI sets `DCROXIDE_REQUIRE_FAULT_INJECTION=1` so a skip is a failure) — a live Go oracle is the wrong tool for stateful storage. Durability is asserted rather than inherited: every write transaction is opened by one helper that sets `Durability::Immediate`, and a source-scanning test fails on any other `begin_write` call site (`tests/durability_policy.rs`). Two port-only hooks have no ffldb counterpart and change nothing for a caller that does not use them ([ADR-0011](adr/0011-exists-address-layout-3-and-the-flush-participant.md)): a flush participant, whose rows are written inside the metadata flush's own durable commit under one bucket's prefix that no overlay row may lie under, and `Transaction::on_commit`, whose closures run once a commit's own flush has captured the write cache and sealed the participant's work, and before the commit's rows are published, and never for a commit that is refused; when that flush is handed to a thread of its own, its rows, the participant's and its commit may all still be to come, and may still fail, after they ran. `tests/participant.rs` pins both, cutting power at every storage operation of a scripted run under the crash rig's power-loss backend, now shared from `dcroxide-testutil`. A third port-only addition only observes. The writer semaphore is what a writable transaction takes as dcrd's takes `writeLock` (`ffldb/db.go:1824`), and a flush and a close here take it too. A caller that finds it held is timed until it holds it, and the wait is added, with a count, to a running total for its kind of thread: the one named `index` (`INDEX_THREAD_NAME`, which the daemon names its index thread by) or any other. A caller that finds it free reads no clock and is not counted. Each `FlushObservation` carries the totals since the database was opened, and the daemon's flush log, written only when `DCROXIDE_DB_FLUSHLOG` is set, ends each line with the name of the thread that ended the flush and with those totals, as `thread` and `writer_wait` (docs/operating.md describes the log). No behaviour changes; pinned by `tests/writer_wait.rs`. A fourth port-only addition moves the sync of a flush's block files, the writing of its rows and its redb commit off the writer, for a flush that a commit's own check trips or that `Database::flush_in_background` asks for, when `Options::background_commit` is set: it is off in `Options::new`, so for addblock, `dcroxide-bench` and every caller that does not ask, and on in the daemon unless `DCROXIDE_DB_SYNC_COMMIT` turns it off (*Where a cache flush's commit runs* under Deliberate divergences). The capture, the taking of what the block-file sync owes (its plan, every file written to since a sync discharged it), the opening of redb's write transaction and the participant's `seal` run on the committing thread under the writer semaphore. The block-file sync, the insert loop, the participant's `contribute`, the transaction's commit, the retirement of the captured layers, the latch on a failure, a `BCDB` error line for it and the participant's `finished` then run on a thread named `dbcommit`, one for the open store, started by the first flush handed to it and stopped by the close or the drop of the last handle, which holds a read snapshot of the store across each commit (`Options::commit_snapshot`, on by default) so that redb frees the pages the commit replaced as it does after a commit made on the committing thread, and frees the retired layers outside the cache lock before its flush's end is read. At most one flush is under way on it: every capture waits for the one before it inside `flush_locked`, so at most one flush is captured and not retired, and a capture made while one is would latch the store. A flush reaches the thread over a channel that holds one flush, put there without waiting on the thread, and its end is signalled once the thread has let go of the store; every wait for the flush waits for that signal, and between flushes the thread holds nothing of the store. `Database::flush` and `Database::close` wait for the flush under way on it and write and commit on the calling thread, the close stopping the thread first; so does a flush in which a participant has work without letting its `contribute` and `finished` be deferred (`FlushParticipant::allows_deferred_contribute`, false by default and true for the exists-address index, whose `seal` sets apart at the capture the keys its `contribute` then writes), and one for which the operating system refuses the thread, which keeps the snapshot it was to hand over; the next flush handed over asks for the thread again. A thread found dead at a hand-off, which only a panic between flushes in a build that unwinds can leave, latches the store, fails the commit that tripped the flush or the `Database::flush_in_background` call that asked for it, and is not replaced. While a commit is under way, a commit that finds the write cache and the captured layers together past `cache_max_size` waits for it, holding the writer, rather than flushing. `Database::is_fatal_settled` waits for a commit under way before it reads the latch, and `Database::wait_background_commit`, hidden from the documentation, waits for it and reports its failure. `Database::flush_in_background` flushes as `Database::flush` does, waiting for the flush under way on that thread first, but hands its flush over as a commit's own flush is handed over and returns at the hand-off, or writes and commits before it returns in the cases where a commit's own flush stays on the calling thread; `Database::commit_mark` and `Database::is_durable` say whether a flush that captured every transaction committed up to a given point has landed, which a flush records once its commit has returned and its layers are retired, and never after a failure. The chain's periodic UTXO cache flush uses the three (*Periodic UTXO cache flush interval*). The observation's `explicit` is unset for a flush `flush_in_background` asked for. `FlushObservation` gains `explicit`, `background`, `foreground`, `waited`, `snapshot` and `memory` (`FlushMemory`: what the flush pinned in the write cache, and the process's memory around it from `ProcessMemory` and `ArenaMaps`, which read `/proc/self/status` and `/proc/self/maps` on Linux, only for an observer, and are `None` elsewhere), which the daemon's flush log writes as `explicit`, `bg`, `fg_ms`, `wait_ms`, `snap`, `layers`, `overlay` and `mem`. dcrd commits every flush on the goroutine that holds its write lock. What changes for a caller is when a failure in syncing a flush's block files, writing its rows, the participant's or the commit is reported (*Metadata store after a failed durable write* and *Commit that trips a cache flush*); the rows go into the transaction in the same order on either thread. Pinned by `tests/background_commit.rs` and the in-crate `background_commit_tests` and `flush_sync_tests`, by the power-cut sweeps of `tests/participant.rs`, of the exists-address index and of `dcroxide-blockchain`'s `tests/utxo_flush_crash_sweep.rs`, the ENOSPC test and dcrd's full block battery, each run in both modes, and in the node by `tests/background_commit_env.rs` and `tests/background_commit_latch.rs`. Two more port-only entry points store what dcrd's would without work the caller has already done, and change nothing for a caller that does not use them. `Bucket::put_owned` (`transaction.rs:1877`) is `put` for a value the caller built for the row and has no more use for: it stages the buffer itself where `put` stages a copy, as dcrd's `Put` hands the caller's slice to `putKey`, which keeps it (`ffldb/db.go:1058-1065`), with `put`'s checks in dcrd's order and their errors (`:877-893`), and gives spare capacity back first, so the pending set and the cache retain what they would for the copy; ten of the chain's and the ticket database's row writers use it (pinned by `an_owned_put_leaves_what_a_borrowed_put_leaves` and `an_owned_put_is_refused_as_a_borrowed_put_is`). `Transaction::store_block_hashed` (`transaction.rs:848`) is `store_block` for a caller that holds the hash of the block's header, which is then not hashed again: the same checks in the same order, the same row and the same bytes, as dcrd's `StoreBlock` reads the hash its `dcrutil.Block` holds (`ffldb/db.go:1169`). Unlike `store_block_raw` it cannot refuse a hash that is not the header's: only a debug build compares the two, and its one caller, the chain's acceptance of block data, hands in the hash of the very header it lends. Pinned by `a_block_stored_under_its_hash_in_hand_is_stored_as_store_block_stores_it` (`tests/blockstore.rs`). `put_owned` belongs to one of the seven changes the `internal/blockchain` row measures together, and `store_block_hashed` was measured only with the merkle trees built on the reader thread (*Reading ahead of the block handler*). Documented divergences, all within the interface contract: the metadata write cache is ported (dcrd ffldb's `dbCache`: transaction-local pending key/remove sets, a shared in-memory overlay committed to redb in one durable transaction only at flush — 100 MiB / 300 s thresholds with dcrd's 1.5x `needsFlush` rule — with the flat block files synced *before* the metadata commit so metadata can never describe unsynced bytes, and flushes on `Flush`, `Close`, and the chain's clean-shutdown flush; the overlay is a stack of immutable layers where dcrd snapshots an immutable treap, so a transaction's snapshot is an O(1) clone of the layer handles and a commit writes into the newest layer in place unless a reader has frozen it, with the size accounting counting the bytes the layers retain rather than the overlay's logical size; commit is accordingly durable only at flush points, dcrd's own cache contract, and an unclean shutdown loses only the cached window — pinned by the crash rig's lazy-window test), no LRU file-handle cache, snapshot cursors, and `Cursor::delete` on a read-only tx returns the contract's `ErrTxNotWritable` where ffldb silently discards it; `Flush` and `Close` wait out the writer but not open read-only transactions, where dcrd's take `closeLock` exclusively and wait for readers too (`ffldb/db.go:1969-1971`, `:2010-2012`). A reader here holds its own overlay layers and redb snapshot, which a flush neither changes nor frees, so the wait would buy nothing, and a long-lived reader does not hold up a flush or a close. A close that succeeds closes the metadata file, as dcrd's closes leveldb (`ffldb/dbcache.go:635-639`): it lets go of the store's redb handle, which every transaction also holds from its begin to its end, so a reader begun before the close reads on and redb closes the file, clearing the recovery flag in its header and releasing its lock, when the last such reader ends; a clone of the closed handle holds neither (`tests/clean_close.rs`). Until 2026-10-23 the close left that handle to the drop of the last clone, which in the daemon never came, so a cleanly stopped node's file kept the flag (*An unclean stop costs a full redb repair* under Known remaining gaps). The overlay layers a flush retires are freed by the thread that flushed once it has released the writer semaphore, on a commit that flushed and in `Database::flush`, under the writer in `Database::close`, behind which no transaction begins, and, for a flush whose commit ran on a thread of its own, by that thread once it has released the cache lock; dcrd swaps in empty treaps under its cache lock and leaves the flushed ones to the garbage collector (`ffldb/dbcache.go:505-509`). A retired layer is in neither the published snapshot nor the published stack, so nothing new can come to read it, and a reader that began before the flush holds handles of its own; only the moment the rows are freed changes (pinned by `the_layers_a_flush_retired_are_freed_once_the_writer_is_released` and `a_flush_hands_back_the_layers_it_retired_alive` in `dbcache.rs` and `a_committer_frees_the_layers_it_retired_unlocked_and_before_its_end_reading` in `lib.rs`; measured only with the exists-address index change the `internal/blockchain/indexers` row records). ffldb quirks reproduced: empty-key `delete` silently succeeds; a bucket and a plain key may share a name. The `addblock` bootstrap format (LE net ‖ LE len ‖ block): `bootstrap::read_block` is the one port of dcrd's `readBlock`. It uses Go's `EOF`/`unexpected EOF` texts and dcrd's mismatch and payload-cap texts, treats a trailing fragment as an error, and retries an interrupted read. Two callers read with it: the daemon's `addblock`, which runs every block through the chain as dcrd's importer does, and the bench. Export is byte-identical. `Database::import_blocks` is a storage-test helper with no production caller. It stores blocks with no block index, UTXO or consensus work and is not an importer. Since ported: the compressed UTXO encodings (with the blockchain serialization piece).  Documented architectural divergence: dcrd v1.10 keeps the UTXO set in its own separate database (`LoadUtxoDB` over `utxobackend.go`'s leveldb backend); the port persists the UTXO set, its set-state marker, and the best state in the block database, with the entry writes and the marker coupled in one transaction everywhere the cache flushes (dcrd's backend `PutUtxos` coupling).  When a block database backs it, the chain keeps only a recent window of block bodies, spend journals, GCS filters, header commitments, per-height ticket rows and treasury state rows in memory; without one the mirrors are the only copy and are never evicted. Every one is written through to the block database per connect and read back through database fallbacks when absent. They are evicted below `minMemoryStakeNodes` (288) of the tip by a port of dcrd's `chainPruner` (time-gated on the target block interval on the monotonic clock, as dcrd's `time.Now()` differences are, called from `ProcessBlock`; the stake-node clearing matches `pruneStakeNodes`). They are also evicted for each connected block's parent as soon as that block is more than 288 below the best header, which is when dcrd's `connectBlock` drops the parent's stake node (`chain.go:783-796`). This keeps the window flat during initial sync instead of letting it grow for a whole prune interval. One list is kept past that eviction and mirrors no row: the ticket-purchase hashes of the last `2 * TicketMaturity` blocks whose data was accepted (`Chain::ticket_purchases`; 512 blocks on mainnet, at most `MaxFreshStakePerBlock` hashes each), which `maybe_fetch_new_tickets` reads in place of the block `TicketMaturity` below a node. dcrd fetches that block on every such lookup (`fetchBlockByNode`, `stakenode.go:43`), from the database unless it is among the 12 in its recent block cache, so a record that no longer reads back fails the lookup there; here it fails only when no list is held, past the window or after a restart, which starts with none, and the block is then read as in dcrd. A treasury row read back follows dcrd's handling of `dbFetchTreasuryBalance`: a missing row ends `sumPastTreasuryChanges`' walk; a row that does not decode is an error that fails the expenditure check as `ErrInvalidExpenditure`; `calculateTreasuryBalance` reads either as a zero balance. A restart warms only the window. dcrd keeps none of these mirrors: its treasury rows, in particular, are read per lookup, where the port once loaded the whole treasury bucket; and the UTXO set itself lives only on disk behind a full port of dcrd's `UtxoCache`: a read-through cache (backend misses are cached, absent outputs negatively cached, fetched clones handed out with the modified/fresh flags cleared) and write-back cache with dcrd's fresh/modified/spent entry states (a commit's spend of an output the cache already holds spent leaves it as it is, as dcrd's does since `9112e4e6`, `utxocache.go:340-355` at `382ff4dd`, and a zero-confirmation spend that is not also marked spent, or that the cache holds an unspent entry for, panics with dcrd's texts, as dcrd's commit does since `e60cedc5` and `22fd2431`, `utxocache.go:594-606` at `382ff4dd`; all pinned by `tests/utxo_cache_commit.rs`; an output a disconnect marked spent and the view then adds again is not marked fresh over a missing slot, where dcrd's `addEntry` marks it fresh: *UTXO cache entry for an output added again after a disconnect* under Deliberate divergences), dcrd's exact size accounting (mapOverhead 57 + outpoint 56 + pointer 8 per entry plus baseEntrySize 56 + script length), height-based eviction of 15% of the depth since the last eviction once the configured `--utxocachemaxsize` is reached, and dcrd's full trigger set — size, the two-minute periodic interval during the initial sync, a forced flush per connect once the chain believes it is current, per disconnect, on the is-current latch flipping after a reorganization, and before `FetchStats` folds the set.  `UtxoCache.Initialize`'s catch-up replay runs at every open: a fresh backend records its state at the tip, a lagging state disconnects blocks to the fork point through their spend journals and replays forward through the cache to the tip — so an unclean shutdown mid-sync recovers instead of silently serving a stale set.  As in dcrd, the replay checks the shutdown interrupt before every block it detaches or replays, and it stops with `errInterruptRequested` ("interrupt requested"). The daemon passes its interrupt flag in `OpenConfig::interrupt` to `Chain::open_with_config` and logs a failed chain open the way dcrd logs `newServer`'s error (`dcrd.go:243`), so an interrupted start reads "Unable to start server: interrupt requested". The unflushed tail is replayed again at the next start, as in dcrd.  The replay runs at the configured `--utxocachemaxsize`: the daemon passes the size to `Chain::open_with_config`, as dcrd builds its cache with it ahead of `blockchain.New` (`server.go:4005-4009`), and addblock opens with dcrd addblock's fixed 100 MiB (`cmd/addblock/import.go:318`). dcrd's "UTXO cache initializing (max size: N MiB)..." and "UTXO cache initialization completed" bracket it under CHAN (see the `internal/blockchain` row).  Pinned by the persistence vectors' restart round trips, the prune test replaying dcrd's full block battery over a one-byte cache limit, DB-path stats tests over seeded utxo rows, and a crash-recovery test replaying the battery under an initial-sync clock, dropping the chain with the recorded state mid-chain, and verifying the reopened set folds to the byte-exact stats (serialized merkle hash included) of a cleanly flushed run.  Retained divergences: one database rather than dcrd's separate leveldb `utxodb` (so no separate `UtxoBackendInfo` versioning or upgrade path — the block database's own versioning covers the combined store, and the single-transaction flush coupling is strictly stronger than dcrd's cross-database flush ordering); a build of `dcroxide-blockchain` without its `std` feature has no monotonic clock, so there the periodic flush interval, the chain pruner and the cached-tip prune run on the adjusted time `ProcessBlock` is given, each stamped by the first processing call that reaches it (so none fires within its interval of startup, even after an earlier flush such as gettxoutsetinfo's) and stalled by a backward step of that clock; the daemon, addblock and the bench always enable `std`, and there all three are timed on the monotonic clock, as dcrd's `time.Now()` differences are; utxo backend corruption found by a point read panics (the resolver seam has no error channel) where dcrd propagates an error, and so does a redb *read fault* on that seam. UTXO rows and the UTXO set state are read with `Bucket::try_get`, which returns the error, as dcrd's `levelDbUtxoBackend.Get` separates `ErrNotFound` from a real error and `dbFetchUtxoEntry`/`FetchState` propagate it. The `gettxoutsetinfo` walk uses `Bucket::try_for_each`, which fails the way `FetchStats` does on `iter.Error()` (pinned by `dcroxide-blockchain/tests/review_utxo_read_faults.rs`). Every other ffldb-layout read of a row keeps ffldb's `dbCacheSnapshot.Get` answer of absence for an error, and a `begin` whose metadata table cannot be opened fails rather than reading every key as missing. The lookup of a nested bucket is not a read: it is answered from a copy of the bucket index held in memory, which the open and the create read, failing if they cannot, and the answers in which it differs from dcrd's are under *Nested bucket lookup, and the bucket index at open* in Deliberate divergences. Error-as-absence on the UTXO set was not bounded to one transaction: after an I/O error redb keeps serving cached pages and fails every uncached one with `PreviousIo` until restart; and the flush hit-ratio debug logging is not reproduced, so the catch-up's own flushes, which dcrd logs at debug, pass silently between the two initialization lines. The copy of the bucket index was measured on m3 on 2026-10-13 as one step of a series of builds, one run against one: the same by wall, 481.7 s against 481.4 s, with that run's flushes 8.6 s longer in their commit phase, 8.4 s of it asleep; 8.3 s shorter with the commit seconds taken out and 9.1 s shorter by the node's clock less its flush time, which a step between two single runs resolves only to 3.5 to 6 s either way; and the block thread's CPU time outside its flushes 8.4 s lower. Neither build of that pair held this tree's changes to the exists-address index's commit hook, the retired overlay layers, the ticket treap, the block-file record, the reader's merkle trees and carried header hash or the script checks of a connected block, both held a developer variable this tree does not have, and the code as committed was first run in a sync as committed inside `8fc9160`, four times on 2026-10-16 at a median of 430.7 s, where its own step was not measured apart (docs/bench-ledger.md). |
| `internal/blockchain` | `dcroxide-blockchain` | vectors | The UTXO serialization layer from `compress.go`, `utxoentry.go`, `utxoio.go`, and `chainio.go`'s minimal-outputs size reader: VLQs (MSB base-128 with the offset subtraction), the domain-specific script compression (six special forms incl. uncompressed-pubkey point recovery via dcroxide-dcrec; unrecognized scripts behind VLQ(len+64)), the base-10 exponent amount compression, compressed txout encode/decode with dcrd's error text, txout/entry flag bytes (raw stake-type bits survive round trips like dcrd's unchecked cast), `UtxoEntry` with its state/flag accessors, outpoint keys (prefix [3,3] ‖ hash ‖ VLQ tree ‖ VLQ index), entry serialization incl. the ticket-submission minimal-outputs tail, and the UTXO set state row. dcrd keeps these in an internal package the oracle cannot import, so parity is pinned by dcrd's own test tables extracted mechanically from the Go test sources (57 vectors) plus randomized round-trip properties. Quirk noted: dcrd's `putCompressedScript` opens with a dead always-panic `len(target) == 0` branch; unreachable via any caller and not reproduced. Corrupt compressed scripts diverge. An overlong size VLQ can wrap to a special discriminant, or to a general size shorter than the VLQ, and then claim fewer bytes than its payload. dcrd's `decompressScript` slices past the script into the backing array or panics. The port's decoder, `decode_compressed_tx_out` and the crate-private `decode_compressed_tx_out_script` beneath it that the chain database calls, returns a deserialize error, which the chain database reports as corruption. The node never writes such an encoding. A stored minimal-outputs count of 2^63 or more is read as dcrd's `i < int(numOutputs)` loop reads it (`chainio.go:238`): no outputs, so the size is the count's own VLQ, which a ticket-submission UTXO entry or spend-journal stxo keeps as its tail. The port once rejected such a row as corrupt, aborting a fetch that succeeds in dcrd and failing `gettxoutsetinfo`, which counts the row in dcrd; both now follow dcrd (`utxoio::tests::minimal_outputs_count_above_i64_max_reads_no_outputs`). Decoding that tail panics in both, in `deserialize_to_minimal_outputs`' capacity reservation as in dcrd's `make` (`chainio.go:204`), wherever dcrd calls `TicketMinimalOutputs`: the vote and revocation input checks, auto-revocation generation and `createrawssrtx`. The port's `gettxout` decodes it too, where dcrd's does not (under Open). Also ported: the legacy difficulty algorithms from `difficulty.go` — the exponentially-weighted BLAKE-256 work retarget (64.32 fixed point, genesis-parking window walks, testnet reduce-min-difficulty and max-difficulty rules), the BLAKE3 (DCP0011) difficulty from an anchor node, `mergeDifficulty`, `estimateSupply`, and both stake difficulty algorithms (original and DCP0001), with the chain walk abstracted behind a height-indexed `ChainView`. Parity: 35 synthetic-chain scenarios generated inside dcrd's own package with dcrd's outputs frozen and replayed (three networks, retarget-boundary lengths), plus pure-function samples. `mergeDifficulty` and `calcNextStakeDiffV2` divide with Go's Euclidean `big.Int.Div` at every division (`go_big_div`), where the port once truncated. The two rules part only on a negative numerator: a negative stake difficulty, which only a header that skipped the stake-difficulty check as an assume-valid fast-add ancestor can carry, or, in the original algorithm, a fresh-stake candidate that `Int64` wrapped negative.
