No chain whose headers all passed that check sees a different value, and for one that did not, the consensus path and the `estimatestakediff` estimators now compute dcrd's. Pinned against dcrd's own functions by `difficulty::tests::merge_difficulty_divides_as_go` and `calc_next_stake_diff_v2_divides_as_go`. Also ported: the stake version machinery from `stakeversion.go` plus `calcWantHeight` and the 11-block past-median-time (even-count quirk preserved) — interval-final node location, header stake version majorities, prior stake version, voter version interval walks, block version majorities, and `calcStakeVersion` including its no-return nil-ancestor fall-through; pinned by 18 synthetic simnet chains generated inside dcrd's package plus 60 `calcWantHeight` samples, all replaying exactly (dcrd's four per-hash memoization caches are reproduced on the block index's node store through the view's `cache_*` hooks, keyed as dcrd keys them. `calcStakeVersionCache` is keyed by the voter version node, and the stake majority and prior stake version caches by the prior interval's final node, so every block of an interval shares one entry and the interval walks run once per interval. They are result-invariant, and the vectors replay the uncached path). Also ported: the threshold state machine from `thresholdstate.go` (`nextThresholdState` with the full Defined→Started→LockedIn→Active/Failed transitions, vote tallying with choice-index masking, quorum and activation thresholds, deployment version lookups, and forced-choice resolution for test networks; the winning choice is carried by ID since `05c31a8e`), pinned by 195 state-and-choice verdicts over 15 synthetic simnet chains covering every deployment, all replaying exactly; dcrd's per-deployment hash-keyed cache is reproduced through the view's threshold-state hooks. That includes the defined state dcrd caches at the boundary whose median time precedes the start time, and the boundaries below the stake validation height. The cache is result-invariant, and the vectors replay the uncached path. Also ported: the agendas of `agendas.go`, separate from deployments since the `b9634e01..6f6cf21b` series (above): `makeAgendas` with its full validation, run when a chain is constructed (`Chain::open` returns its error as `ChainDbError::Rule`, as `New` returns it, and the infallible `Chain::new` panics with its text), the thirteen required agendas with their per-network defaults, the mainnet and testnet3 historical activation tables (pinned against dcrd's display-order hashes), the activation anchors a tally discovers, kept per chain in the block index store, each with the node most recently shown to descend from it (among the deviations the port keeps at `6f6cf21b`, above), the positional query (`isAgendaActivePositional`/`ByID`), and the consolidated `isAgendaActive` (unknown ID, then the genesis block, then positional, then tally) behind `is*AgendaActive` for all twelve agenda IDs incl. the treasury genesis special case; the BLAKE3 work-difficulty anchor search; and the fallible `maxBlockSize` (the larger size only when the agenda is active and a second size exists) and `calcNextRequiredDifficulty`/`calcNextRequiredStakeDifficulty` selectors. The port's chain holds no parameters, so each query builds its one agenda with `lookup_agenda`, which shares every per-agenda step with `make_agendas`. Pinned by 156 verdicts over 12 synthetic simnet chains from dcrd's own package, and by dcrd's `TestDeploymentParamsValidation`, `TestHistoricalAgendaInvariants`, `TestMakeAgendasDefaultRequiredAgendas`, `TestIsAgendaActivePositional` and `TestMaxBlockSizeChoice`, ported in `tests/agenda_tables.rs` and `tests/agenda_positional.rs`. Also ported: the chain persistence serialization formats from `chainio.go` — ticket minimal outputs, block index keys/entries (header ‖ status ‖ VLQ vote tuples), the spend journal (reverse-serialized compressed stxos with the vote-stakebase skip and ticket minimal-outputs tails), header commitments, and the best chain state (LE fields ‖ stripped big-endian work sum, incl. the all-zero-keeps-32-bytes quirk, SetByteSlice truncation on load, and the `uint32` work-sum length check and slice end of `chainio.go:1235-1240`, which part from `usize` arithmetic only for a record of 4 GiB or more, beyond redb's 3 GiB value cap, and are pinned by `chainio::tests::best_chain_state_work_sum_length_check_is_uint32` and `best_chain_state_work_sum_slice_end_is_uint32`) (the block index flush points and cadence follow dcrd's -- the load path, block connect and disconnect, every accepted header (`process.go:258-262`; like ffldb's, each flush is a commit into the database's metadata write cache, not a sync, which reaches disk only when the cache flushes), and the administrative invalidate/reconsider paths dcrd flushes warn-only) — pinned by 150 vectors from dcrd's own package with Go-side round trips proven before freezing. Also ported: all 161 consensus rule error kinds (machine-generated; `6f6cf21b` added `ErrUnknownAgendaID` and `ErrHistoricalForcedChoice` and removed `ErrStakeFees`) and the context-free validation layer from `validate.go` — `CheckTransaction` (standalone sanity + the agenda-driven context checks across coinbase/vote/revocation/treasury shapes, script version and stake-opcode rules), `checkProofOfStake`, and the dual-hash `checkProofOfWorkSanity` — pinned by 520 verdicts over structured and mutated transactions, blocks, and headers from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the block sanity layer — `checkBlockHeaderSanity` (PoW sanity, the +2h future-time limit against an adjusted-time parameter standing in for dcrd's `MedianTimeSource`, the pre-stake-validation-height commitments incl. the 0x0001 early vote bits and zero final state, and the vote/fresh-stake count limits; dcrd's sub-second timestamp precision check is unreachable here since wire timestamps are whole seconds by type) and `checkBlockDataSanity` (proof of stake, non-empty regular tree, `MaxBlockPayload` and header size commitment, per-tree standalone transaction sanity, the fresh-stake header commitment tally, and duplicate transaction detection), which `checkBlockSanity` runs after the header checks since `024899e4` — pinned by 400 verdicts over boundary-biased headers and assembled blocks from dcrd's own package. Block processing makes `checkBlockDataSanity` where `ProcessBlock` does, after the data preconditions, or, for a block the reading thread checked with the same `MinimumStakeDiff` and `MaxTxSize` (*Reading ahead of the block handler*), returns the verdict reached there at that same point (`check_block_data_sanity_with_verdict`, `validate.rs:762`); the vectors' 150 blocks and dcrd's full block battery pin the carried verdict to the checks' own (`blockhashes::tests::a_carried_data_sanity_verdict_is_the_checks_own`). Also ported: the sequence lock (relative lock time) calculation from `sequencelock.go` — `calcSequenceLock` (DCP0003: per-input disable/seconds/mask bit handling, mempool-height inputs viewed from the next block, block-based locks from input heights, time-based locks from the past median time of the block before the input's, and the coinbase/stakebase/version<2 exemptions incl. the internally derived treasury agenda state) and `LockTimeToSequence` — with dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint` lookup abstracted to a confirmation-height closure, which the engine fills from its utxo view and the mempool adapter from its own; pinned by 264 vectors over synthetic simnet chains (treasury forced-active there) and mainnet chains (inactive branch) from dcrd's own package. Also ported: the positional validation layer — `checkDifficultyPositional` (the forced-BLAKE3, EMA, and candidate-anchor-walk paths, incl. dcrd's signed-to-unsigned header version cast; both of dcrd's cached BLAKE3 anchors are reproduced on the block index store. The first is the confirmed anchor (`cachedBlake3WorkDiffAnchor`): every contextual BLAKE3 difficulty calculation records it, and it then holds descendant headers to the ASERT difficulty alone. That is a rule rather than an optimization, since a synced node rejects EMA-bits headers that a cold one accepts. The second is the candidate anchor (`cachedBlake3WorkDiffCandidateAnchor`), which is tried before the candidate walk. The vector views leave both empty and so replay a cold dcrd; `tests/review_blake3_anchor.rs` pins the warm verdicts), `checkBlockHeaderPositional` (past-median-time, the testnet3 minimum-time rule and the height-962928 max-diff checkpoint, height commitments, and old-version-by-majority rejection with the DCP0005 violation exemptions and all six pinned block hashes; the fork rejection checkpoint check landed with the headers-first processing layer once the block index arrived), `checkBlockDataPositional`/`checkBlockPositional` (expired transactions), and the pure helpers (`voteBitsApproveParent`, `IsExpiredTx`, `SequenceLockActive`, `IsFinalizedTransaction`) — pinned by 276 vectors over six scenario chains spanning all four networks, incl. a base-offset testnet3 chain around the max-diff activation height and regnet headers ground to solve BLAKE3 so the candidate anchor walk is exercised both succeeding and falling back. Found and fixed in the process: the difficulty code's testnet3 detection used the old testnet2 network magic (`0x48e7a065` instead of wire's `0xb194aa75`); the frozen difficulty vectors were unaffected (regenerated identical) but the max-difficulty clamp would never have applied on real testnet3 data. Also ported: the contextual header validation layer — `checkProofOfWorkContext` (hash algorithm selected by the DCP0011 agenda state), `checkBlockHeaderContext` (agenda-driven work and stake difficulty, the version-3 stake version majority enforcement, and — now that the ticket state machine is ported — the pool size and ticket lottery final state commitments against the caller-supplied parent stake node state), `checkMerkleRoots` (combined root under DCP0005 header commitments, dual roots otherwise; since `ddd3b6d2` it takes the variant as an argument, `checkMerkleRootsContext` picks the variant from the agenda state, and `checkBlockDataPreconditions` checks the wire size limit and the variant the positional query settles before any other check of the block data; where the query settles it, the port also checks there the header's commitment to the block's size before it counts the data commitment as proven, which dcrd leaves to `checkBlockDataSanity`, *The header's size and a proven data commitment* under Deliberate divergences), the `is{Blake3Pow,HeaderCommitments}AgendaActive` wrappers, and the coinbase/treasurybase unique-height checks (`checkCoinbaseUniqueHeight`/`checkTreasurybaseUniqueHeight` with the consensus-specialized OP_RETURN null-data parsing, incl. the 256-byte cap and 4-byte little-endian height) — pinned by 175 vectors over simnet (both agendas forced active), mainnet, and regnet chains with headers ground for the agenda-correct hash algorithm and crafted null-data scripts. Also ported: the stake transaction input validation layer — the stake-tagged P2PKH/P2SH script extractors, `isAllowedTicketInputScriptForm`/`isStakeSubmission`, the ticket commitment field extractors (hash/amount with the P2SH flag bit/fee limits), `checkTicketPurchaseInputs` (dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint` lookup abstracted to a `UtxoEntry`-returning closure), `calcTicketReturnAmounts` (64.32 fixed point over arbitrary precision with Go's lossy `Int64` narrowing, incl. the auto-revocation remainder distribution via Hash256PRNG seeded with the previous header, and Go's `int` `(len(ticketOuts)-1)/2`, so no ticket outputs give no return amounts where the port's `usize` subtraction once panicked; only a direct call can pass none, since the vote and revocation input checks panic on empty minimal outputs first, as dcrd's do (`validate::tests::ticket_return_amounts_of_no_outputs_are_empty`)), `checkTicketSubmissionInput`, `checkTicketRedeemerCommitments` (payee type/hash/value against the ticket commitments with the log2 fee limit semantics incl. the ≥63 full-fee rule and the treasury-vote output exclusion honoring Go's nil-slice `CheckSSGenVotes` semantics), and `checkVoteInputs`/`checkRevocationInputs` (stakebase subsidy commitment via the V3 subsidy cache over the chain parameters, which implement `SubsidyParams` themselves as dcrd's `*chaincfg.Params` does, submission input/index/maturity rules with the +1/+2/auto-revocation variants) — pinned by 149 vectors over crafted tickets, votes, and revocations against a fabricated utxo view from dcrd's own package. Also ported: `CheckTransactionInputs` — the input validation orchestrator returning the transaction fee: coinbase/treasurybase early-outs, delegation to the ticket/vote/revocation input checks, treasury spend Pi-key validation (`verifyTSpendSignature` over the empty-script `SigHashAll` sighash with EC-Schnorr-DCRv0), the stakebase-subsidy and tspend value-in accumulation exceptions, zero-value spend bans, the fraud proof triple, coinbase/expiry/OP_TGEN/OP_SSGEN/OP_SSRTX/stake-change maturities (with the treasury-dependent stake output maturity), the OP_SSTX spend restriction, the unspendable treasury add rule, and the amount range/overflow/fee accounting — pinned by 88 vectors on simnet incl. treasury spends actually signed with the published simnet Pi test key, all replaying exactly. Its vote and revocation input checks are also replayed against dcrd's `TestImmatureTicketSpend`, `TestInvalidTicketInput` and `TestAutoRevocations` (through `b5`), from chains and verdicts a scratch exporter dumped inside dcrd at `6f6cf21b` (`tests/ticketspend_vectors.rs`). Also ported: the signature operation and stake amount accounting — `countSigOps` (imprecise per-script counting with the stakebase/treasurybase skips), `countP2SHSigOps` (precise redeem-script counting over the utxo lookup with the overflow guard), the exported `CountTotalSigOps` that sums the two, the cumulative enforcement against the 5,000 per-block limit, inline in `checkTransactionsAndConnect` (`validate.go:4033-4050`), `checkStakeBaseAmounts`/`getStakeBaseAmounts` (vote subsidy limits aligned to the height voted on), the stake tree's fees returned by `checkTransactionsAndConnect` and carried into the regular tree's total (the 2.2 campaign delta below records how both took this shape), and `checkDupTxs` (a no-op at this dcrd version per its disabled `checkForDuplicateHashes` constant, ported with the dormant logic) — pinned by 170 vectors over sigop-bearing P2SH scripts and vote sets from dcrd's own package. Also ported (beginning the Phase 8 chain engine proper): the in-memory block index and chain view from `blockindex.go`/`chainview.go` — block nodes with the deterministic skip-list ancestor traversal, accumulated work sums (via the pinned `CalcWork`), the stable status bit flags, header reconstruction, lottery IVs, past median times, and the `betterCandidate` ordering (work, then data availability, then received order, then the little-endian-uint256 hash tiebreak); the block index with chain tip tracking, best header/invalid selection, best chain candidates, unlinked-children data linking (`AcceptBlockData`), and `MarkBlockFailedValidation`'s descendant invalidation walk over the (cached) chain tips; and the height-indexed chain view with efficient tip switching, fork finding, and block locator construction (dense-then-doubling with `fastLog2Floor` sizing). Go's parent-pointer graph is an arena (`NodeStore` with index handles) shared by the index and views; dcrd's short-key/collision map pair (a memory optimization) is not reproduced, and neither are its mutex wrappers, because the daemon keeps the chain, index included, behind one lock. The modified-node set and its flush are ported (`BlockIndex::mark_modified`/`take_modified`, written by the engine's `flush_block_index` after every accepted header, connect and disconnect; a failed flush is under Open). The cached-tips prune timer (`MaybePruneCachedTips`) lives in the engine's connect path and runs on the monotonic clock, as dcrd's `time.Since` does. Parity: dcrd's own blockindex_test.go and chainview_test.go batteries ported in full (header round trips, the median time table, chain tips, the 250,000-node skip list with 2,500 random probes, the ancestor relationship table, the complete betterCandidate table, three-branch view containment/next/fork/locator layouts, tip switching, and empty views), plus a supplementary invalidation-and-linking test for the paths dcrd covers via integration tests. Also ported (the chain engine scaffolding proper): the headers-first processing layer from `process.go` — a `Chain` state over the arena-backed block index carrying the assumed-valid and old-fork-rejection configuration (`expectedBlocksInTwoWeeks` derived from the target block time, fork rejection disabled when configured off or the network has no hard-coded assumed valid hash), `checkKnownInvalidBlock`, `maybeAcceptBlockHeader`/`ProcessBlockHeader` (known-status short circuit, header sanity, run on every unknown header since `024899e4` removed the flag that skipped it, orphan and invalid-ancestor rejection, the positional checks, index insertion; `ProcessBlockHeader` then flushes the block index, as dcrd does after every accepted header), automatic fork rejection checkpoint discovery (`maybeSetForkRejectionCheckpoint`), assumed-valid node tracking (`maybeUpdateAssumeValid`), and `isAssumeValidAncestor` with its two-weeks-behind-best-header clamp — plus the `NodeBranchView` adapter that walks a block tree branch through the deterministic skip list to serve the height-indexed chain views every previously ported validation function consumes, and the completed `ErrForkTooOld` check inside `checkBlockHeaderPositional` (the fork rejection facts dcrd reads from its index mid-check are supplied by the processing layer; a `None` checkpoint skips the check, keeping the earlier frozen vectors byte-compatible). Pinned by 48 state-machine vectors from dcrd's own package: a 30-header simnet chain with ground proofs of work fed through the real `maybeAcceptBlockHeader` over a real block index, covering acceptance, duplicates, orphans, sanity failures, positional failures, side chains above and below the discovered checkpoint, a mid-chain invalidation with the known-invalid short circuits, and the clamped assumed-valid ancestry checks, comparing verdicts, node status bytes, the best header, and the tracked checkpoint and assumed-valid nodes at every step. The file was regenerated at `6f6cf21b` without the flag: the five headers that had relied on skipping sanity were reground, and every verdict and status was kept (derivation in `tests/processheaders_vectors.rs`). Also ported: the stake node attachment layer from `stakenode.go` — `maybeFetchNewTickets` (the maturing tickets loaded by walking back the ticket maturity distance, with the `None`-versus-empty distinction preserved; the ancestor's ticket purchases come from the list filed when its data was accepted, `Chain::ticket_purchases`, and from its block as in dcrd when no list is held, as the `database` row describes; pinned by `tests/ticket_purchase_cache.rs`), `maybeFetchTicketInfo` (vote and revocation info populated from the block data via the already ported `FindSpentTicketsInBlock`; dcrd tracks population through slice nil-ness. The port's flag is cleared wherever dcrd leaves or sets the slices nil: the block index load and both stake-node pruners. The block is read through the database fallback, a missing body is an error rather than a panic (as in dcrd), and the node is marked modified as dcrd's `PopulateTicketInfo` marks it. `flushBlockIndex`'s reload uses dcrd's `canValidate` gate), and `fetchStakeNode` with all three of its paths: the cache hit, the parent connect, and the full regeneration walk that disconnects from the current best chain tip back to the fork point — restoring each pruned node's own bookkeeping from the per-height ticket database undo and tickets rows exactly as dcrd loads them inside the disconnect — and then replays any side chain blocks up to the requested node; the `Chain` state grew the best chain view, the block storage, and the per-height ticket rows (recent-window mirrors of what the database holds: the block store mirrors the blocks stored as their data is accepted, standing in for dcrd's recent block cache, and the per-height ticket rows mirror the `WriteConnectedBestNode` undo and new-tickets rows `connect_block` writes in its connect transaction; entries that leave the window are read back from the database, and a chain without a database keeps every entry, since the mirrors are then the only copy), and block nodes now carry the optional stake node and maturing tickets like dcrd's. Pinned by 215 vectors from dcrd's own package: a 154-block simnet chain of real skeleton blocks (ticket purchases maturing on schedule, votes with random misses, and revocations) advanced through the real `fetchStakeNode` with ticket database rows written at every height, a side chain forked below the tip, then 55 prunes followed by fetches that force the tip-to-fork disconnect walk over the undo rows, the side chain replay, the fork-point path, and the cache hit — comparing pool sizes, lottery final states, winner lists, and missed tickets. Also ported: the reorganization engine from `chain.go` — `BestState`/`newBestState` (incl. the genesis initial snapshot from `createChainState`), `connectBlock` (the block index flush; best state accumulation of transactions and added subsidy; the best state, spend journal record, ticket database rows, treasury balance and treasury spend rows, filter, and header commitment leaves written in one database transaction; the view commit and conditional UTXO cache flush; the tip move followed by the timed cached-tip prune (`MaybePruneCachedTips`, every 5 minutes on the monotonic clock, stamped by the load's prune as dcrd's `pruneCachedTips` stamps it); the connected and new-tickets notifications; and the immediate prune of the parent's stake node. Only the difficulty retarget debug log lines and `addRecentBlock` are not reproduced; the recent-window `blocks` mirror stands in for dcrd's recent block cache and, like it, hands the connect path and the notifications one shared copy of each block), `disconnectBlock` (the parent snapshot with the disconnected block's own stake difficulty commitment, the ticket row drops above the new tip per `WriteDisconnectedBestNode`, and the journal record removal), `loadOrCreateFilter`, `reorganizeChainInternal` (the tip-to-fork detach loop over the spend journal and the attach loop with the validated fast path and the full `checkBlockContext` + `checkConnectBlock` path, which marks failed validation only for consensus rule violations (`RuleErrorKind::is_rule_violation`, dcrd's `errors.As(err, &RuleError)`), and since `ddd3b6d2` not for a contextual `ErrBadMerkleRoot`. Local corruption such as an undecodable parent spend journal row fails the reorg without branding the block, and the recent-context-checks cache skips the context checks for a block that just passed them), `reorganizeChain` with the failed-reorg fallback to the best valid candidate (which the port also takes past a block whose contextual merkle check fails: *Reorganization past a block whose contextual merkle check fails* under Deliberate divergences), dcrd's shutdown interrupt checks in both (before every block detached or attached, and at the top of every `reorganizeChain` attempt), and the is-current latch (`isOldTimestamp`/`maybeUpdateIsCurrent`/`isCurrent` against a caller-supplied adjusted time). An interrupt ends the call at once with `errInterruptRequested`'s "interrupt requested" alone and tries no other candidate; only `NTChainReorgDone` still fires, when `NTChainReorgStarted` did, as dcrd's deferred send does. The error travels as the `ErrUtxoBackend` kind, which is not a rule violation, because the port's one error type needs a kind and dcrd's error is a plain one (pinned by `dcroxide-blockchain/tests/review_reorg_interrupt.rs`).  Three pieces of dcrd's utxo cache semantics proved observable and are reproduced exactly over the in-memory backend+cache stand-ins: the spend journal serialization is round tripped because dcrd reconstructs spent entry amounts, heights, and indexes from the spending inputs' fraud proofs on load; fresh (never flushed) cache entries are removed outright on spend with an explicit spent marker while flushed entries become tombstones that disconnects restore in place with original fields; and the forced cache flush on every disconnect drops the remaining tombstones so blocks detached later in the same reorganization resurrect their outputs from the journal instead.  Pinned by 771 vectors generated against a complete real `BlockChain` (`New()` over real block and utxo databases), regenerated at `382ff4dd` by `tools/chainopsgen` with fraud proofs that name the outputs spent: two competing branches of pre-validated skeleton blocks reorganized forward, across, back, and extended, comparing the best state snapshot and all 732 presence-and-field checks over the full utxo universe after every reorganization.  With fraud proofs that match, an output restored in place and one rebuilt from the journal carry the same fields, so neither these checks nor the utxo universes of the block processing and manual chain manipulation vectors below tell the two apart any more or show that a rebuilt entry takes its amount, height and index from the fraud proofs; the forced flush on every disconnect is pinned now by the persistence replay's check that the disconnect flushes recorded a utxo set state (`tests/persistence_vectors.rs`), by `process::tests::a_disconnect_ends_in_a_durable_commit`, by `every_durable_state_reopens_to_the_right_set_with_the_cache_at_its_limit` (`tests/utxo_flush_crash_sweep.rs`) and by the disapproval replay's log of every cache flush (`tests/disapproval_vectors.rs`).  Also ported: the full block intake path completing `process.go` — `maybeAcceptBlockData` (prunable ticket info population, which marks the node modified as dcrd's `PopulateTicketInfo` does, the positional data checks, whose failures `ProcessBlock` marks since `024899e4`, block storage, and the index data acceptance producing the newly fully-linked descendants; the `chainPruner` call dcrd makes here runs from `ProcessBlock` once the call's reorganization is done, which changes only when the memory is released), `maybeAcceptBlocks` (contextual checks over each linked block, with first-error semantics, the recent-context-checks cache, and the early new-tip-checked notification for a block that extends the tip of a current chain. A failure is marked as failed validation only when it is a consensus rule violation, dcrd's `errors.As(err, &RuleError)` gate, other than `ErrBadMerkleRoot`, which `ddd3b6d2` exempts. `addRecentBlock` is not reproduced: the `blocks` mirror, which holds the block from its data acceptance, stands in for the recent block cache), and `ProcessBlock` itself (duplicate and known-invalid rejection, header acceptance reusing the headers-first path, the data preconditions, data sanity, the bulk-import/assumed-valid fast path that pre-marks validation, data acceptance, tentative acceptance of all linked blocks, reorganization to the best chain candidate regardless of acceptance failures exactly like dcrd, and the fork length result; that order is `024899e4`'s and `ddd3b6d2`'s, under which a precondition failure never marks the block and a data sanity or data positional failure marks it only when the preconditions proved its data commitment; the index flush dcrd makes after data acceptance, `process.go:549-554`, is not reproduced, since `connect_block` flushes moments later and a data-stored bit lost to a crash is healed by the store's duplicate-block check).  Pinned by 261 vectors generated against a complete real `BlockChain` driving the real `ProcessBlock`/`ProcessBlockHeader` over ground proof-of-work simnet blocks (the sanity checks accept either proof of work hash while the contextual checks demand the agenda-selected BLAKE3 hash, so the generator grinds for both), regenerated at `382ff4dd` by `tools/chainopsgen` with fraud proofs that name the outputs spent: sequential intake, a duplicate, an orphan, a headers-first delivery with out-of-order data linking, a mid-processing reorganization to a longer branch, sanity and positional rejections, a full-validation connect failure with the known-invalid (reported as a duplicate per dcrd's check order) and invalid-ancestor short circuits, and a losing-branch extension — comparing verdicts, fork lengths, node status bytes, best state snapshots, and the final utxo universe.  Also ported: the manual chain manipulation surface — `InvalidateBlock` (the genesis and unknown block rejections, the already-failed no-op, the mark-only path for side chain blocks, and for best chain blocks the rollback to the parent, the descendant invalidation, the current-latch reset, the best chain candidate rescan over all chain tips walking each back to its first valid validatable ancestor, and the reorganization to the best remaining candidate), `ReconsiderBlock` (clearing invalidity up the ancestor chain while tracking the earliest failure, clearing the invalid ancestor flag across descendants that are not otherwise invalid, restoring candidates and unlinked children entries, recomputing the best invalid block and best header over all tips, and reorganizing), and `forceHeadReorganization`/`ForceHeadReorganization` (the same-block, wrong-chain, missing-child, known-invalid, and no-data rejections before reorganizing to a tip sibling).  Pinned by 37 operation vectors (plus the intake and 201 utxo universe rows) against a complete real `BlockChain`, regenerated at `382ff4dd` by `tools/chainopsgen` with fraud proofs that name the outputs spent: sibling force-reorgs with every error kind, side chain marking round trips, a best chain invalidation whose fallback reorganization itself fails because invalidation clears the validated status and the skeleton blocks cannot pass revalidation (the chain settles on the deepest valid tip), the no-op repeat, reconsiderations that fail revalidation and stay put, and a deep invalidation taking every branch with it — comparing verdicts, tips, the status byte of every block after every operation, and the final utxo universe.  Also ported: the chain state persistence — `createChainState` (every bucket, the versioned database info rows, the genesis block index row, best state, stake database initialization, the stored genesis block and its empty filter, and the deployment version row) and the `initChainState` load path (the database, compression and block index version downgrade rejections with dcrd's messages; height-ordered block index reconstruction, including the new-consensus-rules failed-block unmarking, which stays in memory only as in dcrd: its post-load flush writes none of the unmarked rows, so a second restart before the block is revalidated reloads it as failed; the best chain and candidate restoration, the stake node load, and the best state snapshot rebuild — reproducing dcrd's own restart quirk where `NumTxns` is recomputed from the regular transaction tree only), `flushBlockIndex` over the tracked modified nodes with pruned ticket info repopulation, and write-through persistence in the connect, disconnect, and cache flush paths (best state, spend journal, ticket database rows, filters, and commitment leaves, the per-block rows under the port's height-first keys; dcrd houses the UTXO set in a separate database with its own backend while dcroxide colocates it in a dedicated bucket of the one redb database using the same pinned row formats — a fresh-sync schema decision — and the chain data is held in recent-window mirrors over those written rows and read back from the database past the window, as the `database` row describes).  dcrd's `upgradeDB` migration paths are not applicable to dcroxide's fresh-sync databases and are intentionally not ported: a database older than the port's version is refused with a message that names the directory to delete (see "Seven per-block buckets" under Known remaining gaps).  Pinned by the reorganization ground truth replayed over a database-backed chain with a flush, restart, and reload at the end: the frozen final best state and utxo universe hold against the reopened chain along with the block index statuses, spend journals, filters, tip, and stake state (restarting mid-scenario is not exercised; an output a disconnect brings back takes its amount, height and index from the spending input's fraud proof unless the cache still holds the spent entry, which it no longer does after a restart, and the regenerated file's fraud proofs name the outputs they spend, so either way the entry is the same).  Also ported: `CheckConnectBlockTemplate` (the tip-or-parent template parent rule with dcrd's two message forms, the data preconditions, sanity without proof of work, the positional and contextual checks, and the full connect checks — over the as-is chain state when extending the tip and over the tip block's spend journal undo when building on its parent; templates never enter the block index so no scaffolding nodes are created), `checkTicketExhaustion`/`CheckTicketExhaustion` (the maturity-window live pool projection with the pre-stake-validation voting block adjustment), and the chain query surface (`MainChainHasBlock`, `BlockHeightByHash`, `BlockHashByHeight`, `HeaderByHash`/`Height`, `BlockByHash`/`Height`, `MedianTimeByHash`, `ChainWork`, `TipGeneration`, and `HeightRange`).  Pinned by 172 vectors against a complete real `BlockChain`: seven template verdicts (a valid template on the tip before and after the commitment root fixup, a valid template on the tip's parent exercising the journal undo path, and the invalid-parent, duplicate-transaction, height-commitment, and coinbase-subsidy rejections), eight ticket exhaustion verdicts over a header-only chain approaching stake validation height (thresholds, rescue by sufficient purchases, and the unknown block), and the query surface outputs.  Also ported: the treasury account and spend tracking from `treasury.go` — the treasury state rows (the balance as of each block plus its yet-to-mature values with dcrd's exact VLQ serialization incl. the negative-debit storage convention and sign sanity checks), the treasury-spend-to-blocks rows (corrupt rows of both kinds diverge: a negative treasury spend count, or a spend or value count too large to allocate, is reported as corruption where dcrd's `make` panics, since the port caps each reservation at what the row's bytes can hold; a value type outside 1-4 after the `0x07` type mask is also rejected as corruption, where dcrd's `deserializeTreasuryState` keeps it as a credit of that raw type; and dcrd reads both kinds per lookup, where the port loads every treasury spend row and the recent window's state rows at startup, so a corrupt one there refuses the start; state rows past the window are read per lookup as in dcrd, including by `gettreasurybalance`, whose RPC adapter reads such a row from the treasury bucket after releasing the chain mutex, as dcrd's `TreasuryBalance` reads it outside `chainLock` (`treasury.go:522-530`), and `calculate_treasury_balance` reads a failed fetch as a zero balance as `calculateTreasuryBalance` does), `calculateTreasuryBalance` (the coinbase-maturity window, whose `CoinbaseMaturity - 1` is taken at `uint16` as `treasury.go:369` takes it, so a network with a zero maturity looks back 65,535 blocks where the port once found no ancestor and read a zero balance; no built-in network has one, and `process::tests::treasury_balance_maturity_subtracts_at_u16_like_dcrd` pins it), the per-block record writes wired into the block connect path (inside that path's single database transaction, where dcrd writes them, so a durable best state can never be missing its own treasury row -- `calculate_treasury_balance` reads a missing row as a zero balance that every descendant would then inherit, and nothing on the startup path repairs it), `checkTSpendExists` with fork awareness, `tSpendCountVotes`/`checkTSpendHasVotes` (the window walk over vote-carrying blocks with the quorum and remaining-votes short-circuit math), `sumPastTreasuryChanges`, all three expenditure policies (DCP0006 window averages with the bootstrap fallback, DCP0007 income-based, and DCP0013 balance-percentage with the spend limit floor) with the agenda-driven selection, `checkTSpendsExpenditure`, and the complete `tspendChecks` — wired into `checkConnectBlock` through its closure seam at dcrd 2.2's position (after the stake tree connect), covering the full window, duplicate-mine, vote tally, and expenditure battery (the value-in commitment check moved into `CheckTransactionInputs`' `checkTreasurySpendInputs` per dcrd 2.2).  Pinned by 264 vectors against a complete real `BlockChain`: a 240-block simnet chain with treasurybases, treasury adds, treasury-vote-carrying votes across a full voting window, and a spend mined mid-window — treasury balances at five depths, exists checks across the mine boundary, vote tallies mid-window and at the window end, maximum expenditures at two vote interval boundaries, all five `tspendChecks` verdict kinds (ok, exists, expenditure, votes, and the non-interval no-op), and the raw treasury rows byte for byte.  The DCP0006 and DCP0007 expenditure paths are exercised structurally but not pinned against dcrd on simnet since the DCP0013 agenda is force-activated there; they await the full-chain cross-validation work.  Cross-validated end to end: dcrd’s own full block test battery (`fullblocktests.Generate`) replayed through the real `ProcessBlock` with full validation on — 577 instances (at `6f6cf21b`) over a scripted regression network chain of fully signed blocks with real ticket purchases, votes, revocations, P2SH redemptions, and reorganizations: 205 accepted blocks matching dcrd’s main-chain and orphan verdicts, 353 rejections matching dcrd’s exact rule error kind across 65 distinct kinds (incl. `ErrScriptValidation` from real signature failures), the orphan-or-reject instances, 14 expected-tip checkpoints, and the non-canonical block encoding rejected at the wire layer.  The dump records its generation wall clock so the too-far-in-the-future rejection replays, and the nanosecond-precision timestamp rejection is a documented skip: wire timestamps are whole seconds by type, so the instance only exists for in-memory blocks and dcrd itself cannot hit it from serialized bytes either.  Also ported (completing the `internal/blockchain` surface): the RPC and network-sync query layer — `stateLastChanged`/`StateLastChangedHeight` (the interval-by-interval walk back from the current confirmation interval), the public `NextThresholdState` (both, since `476d2da3`, failing an unknown ID with `ErrUnknownAgendaID` and answering a forced agenda without its deployment), `GetVoteCounts`/`CountVoteVersion` (invalid vote choices tallied as abstains, wrong-version votes ignored), `GetStakeVersions` (with dcrd’s count clamp and error shapes, the clamp's `int32` truncation aside, which is under Open), `GetVoteInfo`, the block locator surface (`locateInventory`/`LocateBlocks`/`LocateHeaders` with the wire max-headers cap, and `BlockLocatorFromHash` falling back to the best tip), and the RPC stake difficulty estimators (`estimateNextStakeDifficultyV1` with its fake-chain overlay distributing the estimated ticket purchases across the remainder of the interval, `estimateNextStakeDifficultyV2` over the DCP0001 pool projections, and the agenda-driven `EstimateNextStakeDifficulty` selector).  Pinned by 1,464 vectors over three deterministic fake chains generated inside dcrd’s own package: a mainnet block index with a side chain replaying dcrd’s own locator test layout, a 1,200-block regnet chain that runs the DCP0001 agenda through Started, LockedIn, and Active with real votes (wrong-version and invalid-choice votes included) exercising both estimator algorithms on either side of the activation and the real state-change walks, and the simnet forced-choice fast paths — the fake chains replay hash-identical. Two error rows were re-expected by hand to `ErrUnknownAgendaID` at `6f6cf21b`, derivation in `tests/query_vectors.rs`.  Also ported: the committed filter serving from `headercmt.go` — `FilterByBlockHash` (the version 2 GCS filter for a block with its header commitment inclusion proof, gated on the block being known with data available, surfacing the no-filter error kind otherwise) and `LocateCFiltersV2` (the batched cfilters response over an inclusive block range, with the unknown-block, not-an-ancestor, and over-the-maximum-batch error gates, the parent walk collecting the range hashes, and the per-filter inclusion proofs), both serving from the in-memory filter and header-commitment caches the engine keeps in step with the database; `Chain::new` now caches the genesis block's committed filter to match `createChainState` and the open path, so the genesis filter is served like any other block's.  Pinned by a real regnet chain built from dcrd's own fullblocktests generator (fifteen blocks) with dcrd's `FilterByBlockHash` and `LocateCFiltersV2` exercised directly: the block bytes are frozen so the engine here rebuilds the same chain and serves byte-identical filters, proof indices, and proof hashes across mid-chain, tip, and genesis blocks, a range and single-block batch, and the unknown-block/not-ancestor/no-filter error kinds; the server `OnGetCFilterV2`/`OnGetCFiltersV2` handlers wrap these (pushing the response on success and ignoring the error otherwise, confirmed in the dump by driving dcrd's real handlers over a piped serverPeer).  Also ported: the live-ticket queries from `stakeext.go` — `CheckLiveTicket`, `CheckLiveTickets`, and `LiveTickets` delegating to the best node's stake state, and the `BestSnapshot` accessor.  Pinned by a real regnet chain built past the stake validation height from the fullblocktests generator (one hundred fifty blocks): the full ordered live ticket set (two hundred eighty-five tickets) matches dcrd's `LiveTickets` byte for byte, the per-ticket and batched membership checks agree for live tickets, an unknown hash, and a block hash, and the best snapshot hash and height match.  The snapshot holds its missed tickets as a handle on the tip stake node's immutable treap (`BestState.missed_tickets`, a `MissedTickets`, `process.rs:737`), where dcrd's `BestState.MissedTickets` is a list built by walking that treap at every connect, every disconnect and the load at startup (`chain.go:658`, `:857`, `chainio.go:1771`). The handle costs a reference-count increment a block and keeps naming its own block's set whatever the chain does next: a connect made in place copies every node of the treap the handle shares before it changes one. The list's one reader in dcrd is template generation (`internal/mining/mining.go:1650`), and no RPC reads the field: the node's template adapter takes the handle under the chain lock and lists the tickets from it after releasing the lock, in key order, as `stakeNode.MissedTickets()` does, so a template is built from the set dcrd's would be. Pinned by `dcroxide-stake`'s `tests/missed_ticket_set.rs` (every way of reading the handle against the list at every node of dcrd's ticket state vectors, 120 connects and 30 disconnects, with handles taken earlier still reading as they did), `tests/missed_ticket_handle.rs` (the best state's handle against its tip's list after each block of the full-block battery, after a disconnect and after a restart) and the node's `tests/mining.rs` (`the_template_snapshot_lists_the_tips_missed_tickets`, `the_template_snapshot_lists_every_missed_ticket_in_key_order`). It was measured in the batch of four that the `database` row records and not on its own.  Also ported: the utxo viewpoint from `utxoviewpoint.go` — entry addition with the unspendable-output and treasury-add-change exclusions and the ticket-submission minimal-outputs tails, transaction and block connect/disconnect producing and consuming the spend journal (incl. the reverse journal replay, the disapproved-parent regular-transaction undo/redo on connect and disconnect, and the grandfathered same-block stake spends, whose output is added before the input fetch on connect and spent again once both trees are disconnected, `addSBSSViolations` and `removeSBSSViolations`, `utxoviewpoint.go:613-631`, `:974-1004` at `382ff4dd`, with dcrd's panic text when it is missing; the block connect and disconnect take the chain parameters, `96a55a74`), the zero-confirmation spends, decided once per block when its inputs are fetched (`2115b56b`): a later transaction's spend of an output created earlier in the regular tree bypasses the cache only when the view holds none of the creating transaction's regular outputs at the fetch, decided once per creating transaction (`addRegularInputUtxos`, `hasAnyRegularTxOut`, `utxoviewpoint.go:1106-1198` at `382ff4dd`), and the eligible outputs are kept in a set on the view that the connect and disconnect of that block's regular tree mark from and clear (`:365-369`, `:396`, `:498-502`, `:562` at `382ff4dd`), as does `checkTransactionsAndConnect` after the regular tree (`validate.go:4092-4094` at `382ff4dd`); a copy of the view does not carry the set, as dcrd's `clone` does not; the assertions of `22fd2431`: the connect panics when the view holds an input already spent (`utxoviewpoint.go:281-284`, `:349-352` at `382ff4dd`), and `assertBlockUtxoView` (`:633-917` at `382ff4dd`, with dcrd's texts, the grandfathered same-block stake spends its one exception) checks the view every attached block leaves, after either branch and before the connect (`chain.go:1236-1238` at `382ff4dd`), run unconditionally as in dcrd and not measured; the input-fetching set construction with dcrd's exact in-flight index comparison, and the spent-output counting helpers; dcrd's utxo cache/database lookups are abstracted behind a caller-supplied resolver and the disapproved-parent journal is supplied directly — pinned by a full connect/disconnect round trip computed inside dcrd's own package by `tools/utxoviewgen` over a block that disapproves its parent while spending externals, an in-block zero-conf chain, a live ticket via a vote, plus a ticket purchase, treasurybase, and treasury spend, comparing every entry field, both spend journals, and the best hashes at each step (`tests/utxoview_vectors.rs`); by `tests/reincluded_zero_conf_spend.rs`, the shape of dcrd's disapproval scenario s4 (`utxoviewpoint_test.go:443-477` at `382ff4dd`), and `tests/disapproval_round_trips.rs`, three chains of disapproving blocks that reinclude a parent's transaction run through `reorganize_chain` over a database-backed chain with flushes and a restart at chosen points; by `tests/disapproval_vectors.rs`, dcrd's eight disapproval scenarios (`TestDisapprovalScenarios`, `utxoviewpoint_test.go:259-755` at `382ff4dd`) replayed through `process_block` over a database-backed chain in both of dcrd's flush modes, from the chains `tools/disapprovalgen` dumps inside dcrd's package at `382ff4dd`, checking each block's fork length, the tip, every spendability check, every cache flush and the backend's utxo set state after each step against dcrd's; by `tests/attach_view_assertion.rs` (a block marked validated whose input's fraud proof names another output panics on attach, with nothing of it written); and by `utxoview::tests` (eligibility once per creating transaction and over every output of it, the disconnect's mark, the set's life from the fetch to its regular tree, the grandfathered output and eligibility, the already-spent panic, every family of the block view assertion with dcrd's text, the treasurybase, treasury add and treasury spend under the treasury agenda, the last spender rule over the regular tree's outputs alone, the grandfathered exception for the stake tree's spend of output 0 of the grandfathered transaction alone, and the grandfathered additions over a lookup the tests supply, while the same add in `check_connect_block` is reached by no test).  The per-block UTXO view and the UTXO cache are ordered maps, and since 2026-10-12 they hold each outpoint key in a crate-private wrapper, `UtxoKey` (`utxoview.rs:44`), whose comparison reads the hash as four big-endian words, then the output index, then the tree, and is inlined into the search, where the tuple's derived comparison went through the C library's `memcmp` for the 32 hash bytes at every key a descent visits. The order is the tuple's own, which it has to be: the view hands its changes to the cache in key order and a flush writes its rows in key order. `UtxoView::commit` (`utxoview.rs:287`) takes the view's map and walks it once in that order, where it copied the keys, looked each entry up again and removed it, so the cache is applied the same changes in the same order. `OutPointKey`, `UtxoView::entries` and `UtxoView::commit` kept their signatures in that change (`UtxoView::commit` has taken a probe of the cache since the port of `22fd2431`, for the commit's zero-confirmation check). One public type changed: the field `Chain::utxo_cache` (`process.rs:346`) is now a `UtxoCacheMap` (`process.rs:177`), a newtype over the map with the outpoint-keyed `len`, `is_empty`, `get` and `insert` that code outside the crate uses. dcrd's view and cache are Go maps (`utxoviewpoint.go:31`, `utxocache.go:155`), and nothing it can observe changes. Pinned by `the_map_key_orders_exactly_as_the_outpoint_tuple` and `commit_drains_the_view_as_the_searching_commit_did` in `utxoview.rs` and `committing_a_view_leaves_the_cache_as_the_searching_commit_did` in `process.rs`. It too was measured in that batch of four and not on its own.  A UTXO entry and a spent output hold their public key script in a `PkScript` (`pkscript.rs`), which keeps a script of up to 30 bytes inside itself and only a longer one on the heap, so pay-to-pubkey-hash and pay-to-script-hash scripts and their stake-tagged forms are copied from the cache into a view and into the spend journal without an allocation; the ticket minimal outputs an entry carries become a boxed slice, which keeps `UtxoEntry` at 72 bytes on 64-bit targets. One public type changed: the field `SpentTxOut::pk_script` (`chainio.rs:254`) is now a `PkScript`, which dereferences to the script's bytes and compares and prints as they do; `UtxoEntry::new`, `UtxoEntry::pk_script`, `decompress_script` and `decode_compressed_tx_out` keep their signatures. dcrd holds the script as a byte slice, and nothing it can observe changes. Pinned by the vectors and by `pkscript::tests`, `an_entry_keeps_its_size`, `scripts_on_the_heap_survive_the_journal_and_a_disconnect` and `a_ticket_restored_from_the_journal_keeps_its_script_and_minimal_outputs`. Scripts held in place were first run in syncs on 2026-10-18, on m3, from a dcrd v2.1.7 node on another machine on the same LAN, in builds that were states of a worktree and not the tree of a commit, in a series not comparable with the ledger's loopback figures: the build that added them, together with a block's input fetch queue sized once, which the runs did not take apart, took 18.70 s less than the build below it by the means of two runs each, beyond the morning session's 95% interval of about ±3.5 s, 17.35 s of it before the assume-valid block, and the block thread's time on a processor fell 22.3 s, for the two changes together on top of the changes below them and not the effect of either alone. No profile of either build was made, so what the allocations the scripts no longer make cost the block thread is not known, and the step's peak memory is not resolved. The three end-state checks of that section, of two later builds that hold the change, each ended on the UTXO set of every full sync of 2026-10-17 (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Rungs from a block server on the LAN, two changes held for their memory, and dcrd v2.1.7", build E and "The end state (v18)").  Header acceptance hashes the header once. `maybe_accept_block_header` hashes it to look it up in the block index, as dcrd does (`process.go:165`), and hands that hash to the context-free checks, the positional checks and the new block index node (`check_block_header_sanity_with_hash`, `validate.rs:533`; `check_block_header_positional_with_hash`, `:1438`; `NodeStore::new_node_with_hash`, `blockindex.rs:404`), where dcrd's hash the header again in each (`validate.go:753`, through `PowHashV1`, which is the block hash; `:1303`; `blockindex.go:235`). The chain hashes an accepted header once where it hashed it four times; the sync manager's own hash of each header of a headers message remains, so twice on that path, the chain's part held by a test and the manager's read from the code. The public `check_proof_of_work_sanity`, `check_block_header_sanity`, `check_block_header_positional` and `new_node` keep their signatures and hash for every other caller. Under debug assertions each of the three hashes the header again and compares; a release build takes the hash as handed, and only `maybe_accept_block_header` holds hash and header together. Pinned by `process::tests::accepting_a_header_hashes_it_once` and, in `validate.rs`, `the_header_sanity_checks_hold_a_handed_hash_to_its_header`, `the_positional_header_checks_hold_a_handed_hash_to_its_header` and `a_new_block_node_holds_a_handed_hash_to_its_header`, with dcrd's generated vectors for header processing, the positional checks and block sanity passing unchanged.  `is_majority_version` (`stakever.rs:411`) walks as dcrd's `isMajorityVersion` does (`chain.go:558-572`) and also stops once the count and the blocks of the window still to be looked at together fall short of the required number, where dcrd walks on: each block left adds at most one, so dcrd's count then falls short too, and until then the two loops are the same loop. The answer is dcrd's for every input, and its callers read only that answer. On the main network that ends the 950-of-1,000 walk header acceptance makes for a header below the latest block version (`validate.go:1060`) at the 51st block when no block of the window carries the newer version, where dcrd looks at all 1,000. Pinned by `tests/majority_version_walk.rs` (dcrd's loop transcribed statement for statement, held against the port through a list of versions and through the block index's parent links, which never looks at more blocks than dcrd's loop).  The connect is handed the number of outputs the block spends by the caller that sized the spend journal with it (`chain.go:1185-1186`), where dcrd's `connectBlock` counts them again to check the journal's length (`:605`). The count is a function of the block alone; a debug build recounts and compares, and the public `Chain::connect_block` counts for itself. Pinned by the connect's refusal of a journal of another length either way and, under debug assertions, of a count that is not its block's, with dcrd's full block battery, reorganizations included, connecting every block with its own count.  The acceptance of block data stores the block with one call to the store and reads its `ErrBlockExists` as already stored, where `dbMaybeStoreBlock` asks `HasBlock` and then `StoreBlock`, which asks again (`chainio.go:461-472`, `ffldb/db.go:1168-1173`): the same question with the same hash and height inside one transaction, so the same answer, and a block already stored still ends accepted with nothing written. `fetch_stake_node` (`process.rs:3382`) hands out the stake node the block index holds, shared, as dcrd returns `node.stakeNode` (`stakenode.go:99-118`), where it handed out a copy: `Chain::fetch_stake_node` returns an `Arc<Node>` and `BlockNode::stake_node` is an `Option<Arc<Node>>`, and no caller changes a node it is handed. A connect moves the block's spend journal, filter and commitment leaves into the mirrors and writes each row from the mirror's entry, where it copied them. Pinned by `the_stake_node_handed_out_is_the_one_the_index_holds`, `a_connect_writes_the_rows_its_mirrors_hold` and `a_connect_writes_the_commitment_leaves_its_mirror_holds` (`process.rs`).  A connect that drops its parent's stake node when it ends, which is when the block is more than 288 below the best header (`chain.go:783-796`), makes the block's node of the parent's in place (`Chain::fetch_stake_node_taking_parent`, `Node::connect_in_place` in the `blockchain/stake` row): the parent's node leaves the index, its treaps become the block's, changed where nothing else holds them, and nothing is left of it for the prune to free. It takes the parent's node only when the index alone holds it, so a node handed out is never taken while anything still holds it; when anything else holds it, the node stays in the index and the block's is connected from it as before, as it is when the index holds the block's node already. The connect decides whether it drops the parent's node once, before it makes the block's, and drops it at its end by that answer; only accepting a header, marking a block invalid, reconsidering one and the load at startup move the best header, and none of them happens inside a connect. A block the parent's treaps refuse is refused with the error a connect from the parent's node gives, and the parent's node, its moves undone, goes back to the index. A connect that fails after the block's stake node was made of the parent's rebuilds the parent's node from it and puts it back in the index before it returns the error, since the parent is still the tip and every later fetch of a stake node starts from the tip's; the block's node stays in the index, as it does when it was connected from a copy. Pinned by `the_stake_node_made_in_place_is_the_one_fetch_stake_node_makes` (`process.rs`: the node made in place against the one `fetch_stake_node` makes along the battery's main chain, the parent's node taken when the index alone holds it and rebuilt from the block's, and left, the same node, when something else holds it), `a_block_the_parents_treaps_refuse_gives_the_parent_its_node_back` (`process.rs`: each block of the battery's main chain made again three ways the parent's treaps refuse, with a vote for a ticket that is not a winner, refused before any ticket moves, a revocation of a ticket the block voted, refused after the winners' moves, and the block's last vote dropped, that winner revoked and a ticket bought twice, refused after a spent, a missed, a revoked and a bought ticket; the error `Node::connect`'s, the block left without a stake node, the parent's node back in the index answering as before, or the same node when something else holds it, and the block's node made of it again as before) and `tests/stake_node_in_place.rs` (the full-block battery replayed from `bsv0` on below a header far ahead, so every connect drops its parent's node, with the best snapshot and its missed tickets, a copy of the tip's node walked three times on a thread of its own as the RPC server walks one, and one block in four the tip's node itself kept across the block, which sends that connect down the copying path; every one reading after the block as before and the replay ending as one without the far header does; and the power under the metadata store cut at every write and sync of the processing of `brt1` and of `brt8` in turn, two blocks whose stake nodes between them spend, miss, revoke and buy tickets, after each of which the tip holds a stake node that answers as before, the cuts after the block's stake node was made finding the parent's own node consumed and a rebuilt one in the index). The connect made in place was first run in syncs on 2026-10-18, on m3, from a dcrd v2.1.7 node on another machine on the same LAN, in builds that were states of a worktree and not the tree of a commit, in a series not comparable with the ledger's loopback figures: the build that added it took 16.40 s less than the build below it by the means of two runs each, beyond the morning session's 95% interval of about ±3.5 s, 13.85 s of it before the assume-valid block, the block thread's time on a processor fell 19.2 s and the index thread's rose 5.5 s, a step on top of the changes below it and not its effect alone. No profile of either build was made, so what the copies and the frees it leaves out cost the block thread is not known, and no record says how many connects took the parent's node (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Rungs from a block server on the LAN, two changes held for their memory, and dcrd v2.1.7", build C).  **Seven changes measured together.** The node remembered beside each activation anchor and the shorter majority walk (above and among the deviations kept at `6f6cf21b`), one hash per accepted header, the spent-output count handed to the connect with the treasury predicates of the `blockchain/stake` row, the single store call, the shared stake node and the moved mirror entries with `Bucket::put_owned` of the `database` row and the duplicate-input search of the `blockchain/standalone` row, the filter reset of the `container/apbf` row, and the rebroadcast gate of the daemon row were measured as one build and not one at a time: on m3 on 2026-10-13, a default mainnet sync to block 1,116,035 from a dcrd on the same machine took 481.4 s with the seven, in one run, against 505.6 s and 502.8 s in two runs of the build they were added to, 22.6 s less with the flushes' commit seconds taken out and 22.9 s less by the node's clock less its flush time, good to 3 to 5 s either way with one run on one side. About 5 s of it is the initial headers sync, 7.5 s in that run against 12.5 s and 12.7 s. What each change contributes was not measured, and the builds of that day added the changes in another order and held a developer variable this tree does not have, so the code as committed was first run in a sync as committed inside `8fc9160`, four times on 2026-10-16 at a median of 430.7 s, where its own step was not measured apart (docs/bench-ledger.md).  **The block hash.** dcrd's `dcrutil.Block` computes a block's hash once, as the block is wrapped, and every step reads it (`dcrutil/block.go:92-94`, `:288-293`; `process.go:452`, `validate.go:2031`, `:2111`, `ffldb/db.go:1169`, `chain.go:989`, `internal/netsync/manager.go:1222`). `MsgBlock` caches nothing. The processing call takes the hash once (`process.rs:5314`), out of the `HashedBlock` the block arrived in or by hashing the header where the call starts, and hands it to the duplicate and known-invalid checks, the record of a proven merkle commitment, the acceptance of the data, which keys the mirror of recent blocks and the ticket purchases with it and stores the block under it (through `Transaction::store_block_hashed`, or as its record; the `database` row), the contextual checks made as the block is accepted (the key of the recent merkle checks and, under the version 1 proof of work, the hash compared with the target), and the filter lookup of a block attached without full validation; those last two read it from the slot filed beside the block's transaction hashes, for that very allocation only (`accepted_block_hash`, `process.rs:6377`, by `Arc::ptr_eq`). Still hashed per connected block: the parent's header, by the reorganization's check that it was handed the parent; for a block added without full validation, its own header once more, by the view that connects it, for the best hash it records (`UtxoView::connect_block_with_hashes` through `connect_block_with_lookup_typed`, `utxoview.rs:1939`); a header not yet in the index, by header acceptance; and the block's header by the contextual check the attach loop makes, when it runs at all. No hash is taken from a key of the `blocks` mirror or from an index entry. Under debug assertions every function handed a hash hashes the header again and compares; a release build takes it as handed. The sync manager asks the chain's block type for the hash (the `internal/netsync` row). `tests/header_hash_count.rs` pins the counts on the processing thread for a block that extends the tip with its header already indexed: one header hash for a fully validated block that arrived hashed and two for one added without full validation, two and three for a block that arrived without hashes, whatever the header commitments agenda's state, and one for `Chain::maybe_accept_block_data` alone; before, with the agenda decided, a fully validated block cost 7, one added without full validation 9 and data acceptance alone 2. It was measured only together with the merkle trees built on the reader thread (*Reading ahead of the block handler* under Deliberate divergences).  **Transaction types.** dcrd classifies a transaction (`stake.DetermineTxType`) at each step of a block's connect that reads its type: `checkTransactionContext` (`validate.go:442`), `checkBlockContext`'s own loops over both trees (`:2204`, `:2243`), the adding of its outputs to the view (`utxoviewpoint.go:99`), the version 2 filter (`blockcf.go:338`) and the view assertion, once for a stake transaction's inputs and once for its outputs (`utxoviewpoint.go:687`, `:800`): six classifications of each stake transaction and three of each regular one for a block validated in full. The processing call classifies each transaction of its block once, into a `BlockTxTypes` (`blockhashes.rs:236`), where it files the block's hashes (`process.rs:5058`), on the thread that processes the block; the contextual checks made as the block is accepted, the connect checks, or for a block added without full validation (a bulk import's, an ancestor of the assumed valid block) the view's connect of the block (`UtxoView::connect_block_with_hashes`), the connect of each transaction, the filter (`regular_by_input` or `regular_with_types`, the `gcs` row) and the view assertion read the types from the slot, for that very allocation only (`accepted_block_tx_types`, `process.rs:6362`, by `Arc::ptr_eq`), and any other block the reorganization attaches is classified once by its attach loop (`tx_types_for`, `:6352`). The classification is a function of the transaction alone and reaches no verdict, so every check, error kind and text and their order, the view, the filter and the assertion's texts are those of a classification at each step. The public `check_transaction_context`, `check_block_context`, `check_connect_block`, `check_transactions_and_connect`, `UtxoView::connect_block`, `connect_stake_transactions`, `connect_regular_transactions`, `connect_regular_transaction`, `connect_stake_transaction`, `add_tx_outs_with_hash` and `Chain::load_or_create_filter` keep their signatures and classify for themselves, and every classification the crate makes goes through one function (`blockhashes::classify_tx`); the disconnect paths and the partial classifications the connect and the spend journal's count make (`is_ssgen`, `is_tspend`, `is_treasury_base`) are unchanged. As with the hashes, every step checks the types against its block with `assert_for`, one type per transaction in every build and a full classification under debug assertions. Pinned by `process::tests::a_tip_extension_classifies_its_transactions_exactly_once` (`process.rs:12046`: each transaction once, counted at every classification the crate makes, the filter build handed no types included, for each of 30 tip extensions of dcrd's full block battery, validated in full or in a bulk import, arriving hashed or not) and `hashes_are_only_reused_for_the_very_block_they_were_filed_with`. It was first run in syncs on 2026-10-19, on m3, from a dcrd v2.1.7 node on another machine on the same LAN, in a build that was a state of a worktree and not the tree of a commit, in a series not comparable with the ledger's loopback figures: that build, R1, which holds it and three other changes, took 6.80 s or 2.5% less than a build of the tree of `797a213` by the means of four runs each, beyond the session's 95% interval of about ±2.6 s, 4.1 s of it before the assume-valid block, and the block thread's time on a processor fell 4.45 s by the means, for the four changes together and not the effect of any one; one sync of R1 from the loopback server ended on the UTXO set of every full sync from that server since 2026-10-17, its block filters not compared (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Where `797a213`'s block thread waits, a deeper read-ahead and a rung of four changes").  **Filter scripts.** dcrd's `blockcf2.Regular` looks the script of each output a regular non-coinbase input, a ticket input or a treasury add input spends up in the view the block's connect has connected it to: `checkConnectBlock`'s for a block validated in full (`validate.go:4575`), and for one added without, that of `UtxoViewpoint.connectBlock` (`chain.go:1194`), which `loadOrCreateFilter` hands it (`:1200`). The chain here reads each of those scripts from the spend journal that connect built, by the input's place, instead (`connected_block_filter`, `validate.rs:6510`), on both paths: the connect checks the attach loop makes, which it always gives a spend journal, and the attach loop's filter of a block added without full validation, as a bulk import's blocks and the ancestors of the assumed valid block are (`load_or_create_filter_with_hash`, `process.rs:4379`). The connect spent each output and appended it, script version and script, as it went, and `check_transactions_and_connect_typed`, or on the other path the view's connect of each tree (`UtxoView::connect_stake_transactions_typed`, `utxoview.rs:691`, and its regular twin), records the journal's length before it connects each transaction and after each tree, so a transaction's entries are found by where its own connect began (`journalscripts.rs`). A transaction is read so only when its connect journaled one entry for each of its inputs; any other input, and every input when there is no journal, as for the template checks and the public `Chain::load_or_create_filter`, is looked up in the view as dcrd does, so a missing script is `PrevScriptError` for the same input with the same text. The journal holds the entry the view held at the spend, so the scripts, and the filter, are the view's; under debug assertions the filter is built from the view too and compared. Pinned by `process::tests::an_attached_blocks_filter_reads_its_scripts_from_the_spend_journal` (`process.rs:12122`: over dcrd's full block battery validated in full, and in a bulk import over the 162 blocks the battery accepts onto its main chain before the first it accepts onto a side chain, every script read from the journal and none from the view) and `journalscripts::tests`; a one-off check outside the tree on 2026-10-19 connected every block of mainnet to height 1,116,035 through the view's public connect functions, recording the journal as the connect checks do, and found the filter built from the journal byte-identical to the one the gcs crate at `797a213` builds from the view, every input it commits to read from the journal, and every commitment root from height 431,488 its hash. Reading the scripts from the journal was first run in syncs on 2026-10-19, in R1, the build *Transaction types* above describes, which holds it and three other changes; R1's step is the four together and not this change's own, and no block filter was compared after those syncs (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Where `797a213`'s block thread waits, a deeper read-ahead and a rung of four changes").  Also ported: `LotteryDataForBlock` — the next tickets eligible for voting, the ticket pool size, and the lottery PRNG final state for a block's stake node, empty below stake enabled height and returning the unknown-block error otherwise.  Pinned over the same real past-stake-validation regnet chain: the tip's winning tickets, pool size, and final state match dcrd byte for byte, as do the empty-winner rows for blocks between stake enabled and stake validation heights (with their growing pool sizes), the blank data below stake enabled height and for the genesis block, and the unknown-block error.  Also ported: `TicketPoolValue` — the sum of the stake submission output amount of every live ticket, resolved through the utxo set; pinned over the same real chain against dcrd's exact total (five million seven hundred thousand atoms across two hundred eighty-five tickets).  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master `452c1a6c`) ports the consensus delta: `checkedmath.go`'s overflow-checked adders with dcrd's exact flag expressions (`addUnsigned` ok = sum ≥ a; `addSigned` ok = (sum > a) == (b > 0), over wrapping adds); the sigops rework — `countSigOps`/`countP2SHSigOps` to fallible `u32` with per-script overflow guards and master's messages, the new `CountTotalSigOps` composition, `checkNumSigOps` deleted into `checkTransactionsAndConnect`'s inline cumulative check, and the real behavior change where a vote's ticket input now contributes P2SH sigops (the old code skipped the whole vote) — with the mempool/mining/template consumers moved to `CountTotalSigOps` like upstream; `checkTransactionsAndConnect` returning the accumulated fees (`getStakeTreeFees` deleted; the stake-tree return feeds the regular tree) with `addSigned` fee accumulation and the pre-stake-validation-height expected-stakebase-output arm removed; `CheckTransactionInputs` letting treasurybases flow through the input loop (fee 0 for valid ones, range errors for malformed — the coinbase early-out now gated on !isTreasuryBase), the `sumTotalAtomIn` overflow/max closure, the vote stakebase summing its `ValueIn` commitment directly, and the new `checkTreasurySpendInputs`/`extractTreasurySpendCommitAmount` battery (input range + spend-amount commitment, absorbing the value-in check `tspendChecks` used to run); `tspendChecks` relocated after the stake-tree connect — and the port's full chain-backed battery wired into `checkConnectBlock` through a new closure seam at exactly that position, closing a gap where only the stateless subset ran in the live path — with `addSigned` total accumulation and `u32` vote tallies under `addUnsigned` overflow guards (`ErrTooManyTreasurySpendVotes`, unreachable in practice) and master's window message as a `RuleError`; `checkTreasuryBase` dropping its params argument; `checkProofOfStake`/vote/revocation context messages restated (the new revocation null-outpoint arm is unreachable defense in depth — `CheckSSRtx` forces a stake-tree input while `isNullOutpoint` requires the regular tree — pinned natively on the reachable general-rule path); and the `ErrFraudAmountIn`/`ErrInvalidTreasurySpendExpiry` kind mappings.  Pinned by re-dumping the affected corpora from a throwaway exporter inside master's `internal/blockchain`: the sigops file (one cpso vote verdict changed 0→1 per the behavior change, plus 40 new `CountTotalSigOps` rows) and the `CheckTransactionInputs` file (a degenerate treasurybase flipping ok→`ErrSpendTooHigh` and a mismatched treasury spend flipping ok→`ErrInvalidTSpendValueIn`), retiring the `checkConnectBlock` suite's stateless value-in row (the check moved out of `tspendChecks`), the unchanged suites replaying as before, plus native pins for the `checkTreasurySpendInputs` battery.  The `internal/blockchain` machinery not otherwise reproduced is documented plumbing: the database upgrade paths (fresh-sync) and the mutex/notification scaffolding.  Logging is a second, separate gap: the crate takes an injected package log sink the way dcrd's package does (`slog.Disabled` by default, installed through `UseLogger`, which `dcrd/log.go:85` calls with the CHAN logger), and reproduces `flushBlockIndexWarnOnly`'s warning and the chain-open lines. Those are `initChainState`'s "Loading block index...", debug "Block index loaded in ..." and debug "Assumed valid node is ..." (`chainio.go:1691`, `:1716`, `:1761`), `maybeSetForkRejectionCheckpoint`'s debug line (`process.go:86`, at open and wherever else it runs), "Deployment version N loaded" (`chainio.go:1570`, logged inside the transaction that records the version, so it precedes a commit failure as dcrd's does), `UtxoCache.Initialize`'s two Info lines (`utxocache.go:825`, `:863`, `:1050`), and the version, "Best known header" and "Chain state" lines that end `blockchain.New` (`chain.go:2254-2305`). The version line reads "chain: 15" where dcrd's reads "chain: 14", the port's chain database version (see "Seven per-block buckets" under Known remaining gaps). They go out at dcrd's levels and in its order, a fresh database included, since dcrd's `initChainState` loads what `createChainState` has just written. `Chain::open_with_config` takes the sink in `OpenConfig::log`, so the open's own lines reach it; the daemon and addblock install the CHAN sink there, as dcrd's `log.go:85` and `cmd/addblock/addblock.go:77` install theirs before `blockchain.New`. The timing line needs a clock, so a build without the `std` feature leaves it out; the daemon and addblock build with `std`. "UTXO database version info" names the versions the combined store's UTXO rows are in (database 3, compression 1, utxo set 3), the values a fresh dcrd `utxodb` records, since the port keeps no separate UTXO backend info to read. Two neighbours of these lines are not reproduced (see Known remaining gaps): `LoadUtxoDB`'s CHAN "Loading UTXO database from '...'" and "UTXO database loaded", and `stake.LoadBestNode`'s STKE "Stake database version N loaded". Pinned by `dcroxide-blockchain/tests/chain_open_logs.rs` and `dcroxide-node/tests/review_chain_open_logs.rs`; the expected lines in the second are dcrd's own output for the same simnet runs, timestamps aside. The rest of `internal/blockchain`'s CHAN lines (roughly 150 slog calls over the non-test sources) and all of `treasury.go`'s `trsyLog` lines are unported, as are `upgrade.go`'s (fresh-sync).  Rule-error descriptions carry dcrd's text field for field; `validate.rs` unit tests pin a sample, and `tests/review_error_texts.rs` the amount range texts that print `dcrutil.MaxAmount` as Go's `%v` does (`2.1e+15`) and both `ErrInvalidTemplateParent` forms. That includes the treasury spend `ParseSignature` wrap, which prints dcrd's `schnorr.Error` description rather than the dcrec port's kind name. dcrd prints the header and median times with `%v` in the host's local zone, because its wire decoder builds `time.Unix` values. dcroxide renders them in Go's `time.Time` format pinned to UTC (`2026-09-23 14:00:01 +0000 UTC`), so the text matches a dcrd running in UTC. The block import progress log makes the same choice through the same renderer (`dcroxide_blockchain::gotime::go_time_utc_string`). The indexers' catch-up progress line keeps a private copy of it, because `dcroxide-indexers` does not depend on the chain crate.  A failed chain database update reports dcrd's text. dcrd returns the error unchanged (`chain.go:676-679`, `:866-869`), and `submitblock` and the sync manager print its bare description; for a ticket bucket that has drifted from the stake node, that is `missing key <hash> to delete`. The port hands the failure over as a `RuleError` from `persist_rule_error` whose description is that same text. The ticket database layer's errors keep their bare descriptions (`stake_db_to_db_error`), and so do the chain's own failures raised inside an update (`chain_db_to_db_error`). A database `ErrCorruption` is the one exception: the `RuleError` carries the corruption kind only in its text, and `is_persisted_db_corruption` reads it back from there for the sync manager's corruption-only `Critical failure` line, so a corruption prints `chain database failure: ` followed by the error's debug form, for example `Db(Error { kind: Corruption, description: "..." })`.
