Printing dcrd's bare text for a corruption as well needs another carrier for that kind first (review finding RG03#2). |
| `internal/blockchain/indexers` | `dcroxide-indexers` | vectors | Complete: the shared indexer machinery (the `idxtips` bucket with its 36-byte hash+height tips, the `v`-prefixed version keys and `d`-prefixed drop markers, index creation/upgrade, the interrupted-drop resume on init, the incremental flat drop in 2,000,000-entry batches, and main-chain recovery by repeated tip disconnection), the index update subscriber with prerequisite/dependent subscription chains, the `updateIndex` expected-height state machine (lower heights relayed to the dependent with the relay error deliberately discarded exactly as dcrd does, higher heights failing with `ErrMissingNotification`), and `CatchUp` from the lowest index tip; the version 2 transaction index (three buckets: 48-byte entries under `txbyhashidx` keyed by tx hash with block id/offset/length/tree index, plus the `idbyhashidx`/`hashbyididx` block-ID compaction pair, the forward-scan-plus-binary-search highest-ID recovery on init, and the `TxLoc` offsets derived from the serialized block exactly as `DeserializeTxLoc`); the exists address index (dcrd's address set -- 21-byte type+hash160 keys, the four address-type foldings incl. pay-to-pubkey collapsing onto its pubkey-hash twin, multisig redeem script extraction from signature scripts, ticket commitment addresses for both P2PKH and the amount-MSB P2SH flavor, the never-remove disconnect semantics, and the memory-only unconfirmed overlay moved into the index on connect -- stored in this port's layout 3, index version 3, rather than dcrd's one row per address; see "The exists-address index stores dcrd's address set in its own layout" below); and the legacy `txbyaddridx`/`cfindexparentbucket` drop helpers.  `IndexSubscriber::notify` processes one notification per call, on the caller's thread, with dcrd's state transitions, which is what the vectors drive; the sync-waiter signalling is covered natively and nothing runs dcrd's 500ms ticker (the daemon's RPC seams poll). The channel and the goroutine are the daemon's: each drained block event's notification goes onto a 128-deep bounded queue (dcrd's `bufferSize`) served by one thread named "index" (`dcroxide-node` `IndexThread`; dcrd's `handleIndexUpdates`). As in dcrd, index maintenance overlaps the processing of the blocks that follow, a full queue blocks the thread that connected the block, an index can trail the chain by a little more than the queue's depth, and a shutdown drops what is queued, which the next start's `CatchUp` indexes. For the default-on exists-address index each update is still one write transaction per block, whose candidate keys are filtered against the in-memory memtable with no store read and whose keys reach disk in the metadata flushes that carry its tip rows; such a flush may now run on the index thread's commit, and its rows, the index's and its redb commit then on a thread of its own (the `database` row), in which case the keys handed over after the capture, while the flush's rows and commit are being written, are kept apart from the flush's by its seal and go into the next flush. Four steps are not dcrd's. The exists-address index's copy of the unconfirmed overlay is made when the connect is queued, where dcrd's goroutine drains the map when it reaches the block, so a mempool address is persisted with the first block connected after it was seen and never with an earlier one still queued. For a connect queued with that copy, the index does the part of the update that needs no transaction once the subscriber holds the index mutex and before the update's write transaction begins (`Indexer::prepare_notification`, called at `crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/subscriber.rs:294`; `ExistsAddrIndex::prepare_connect`, `crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/existsaddrindex.rs:767`): it extracts the block's addresses, adds the copied keys, hashes each key to its partition, filters the keys against the memtable, sorts them and hashes the header. dcrd extracts the addresses, drains the map and filters inside the notification's `dbTx` (`existsaddrindex.go:383-444`, `:451-456`, `:461-465`). The bucket check, the tip row and the commit hook stay in the one write transaction. Each key the filter kept carries its partition's version, a count of the changes to which keys that partition holds and which of them are journaled, read under the same hold of the partition's read lock as the search and before it (`Memtable::filter`, `crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/existsaddr/memtable.rs:371`); every such change moves its version under its write lock (an insert that adds a run, a flush's fold, a flush's clear of a partition it merged, a load), and nothing sets it back. A flush seals at its capture, under the writer, the keys its `contribute` then journals or merges (`AddrStore::seal`), and `contribute`, which in the daemon runs on the commit thread beside later connects, reads only the journaled and sealed keys (`Memtable::flushed_sizes`, `flushed_sorted`, `sealed_sorted`); its `finished` folds them in or, after a failure, puts them back among the unjournaled keys (`Memtable::seal`, `fold_sealed`, `unseal`): a sealed key is held and found by every lookup, a key inserted after the seal is left for the next flush, and a seal and an unseal, which change neither which keys a partition holds nor which are journaled, leave its version, so a filter made before a seal still stands after it. Under the write lock the hook takes a partition's keys as one run, with no second search, no sort and no dropping of repeats, when every one of them carries the partition's current version and they were handed in strictly ascending order, and otherwise searches, sorts and drops repeats as it did (`Memtable::insert_candidates`, `:413`); a debug build still searches on the first path, as an assertion. The run that goes in is the same either way, and so are the journal and run rows and every answer. What was prepared goes back to the index through `Indexer::process_prepared_notification` (`crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/common.rs:149`) and is used only by the index instance that made it, told by its store's allocation, and only for the connect of the very block it was made for, the same allocation (`crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/existsaddrindex.rs:822-823`); handed to another instance or to any other notification it is dropped whole, and the work is done inside the transaction, against that index's own memtable and overlay. The keys the index holds, the tip row, every answer, the lock order (index mutex, then writer) and every error are unchanged. A connect with no copy made ahead of it (catch-up, and inline delivery in the in-process tests, the vectors and `dcroxide-bench replay`) prepares nothing and computes all of it inside the transaction as before; a disconnect only moves the tip, and the transaction index prepares nothing. One difference depends on timing: when another thread's commit flushes between that filter and the transaction, a key of a partition the flush merged is left out, where a filter made inside the transaction hands it over again as a duplicate of its run's key for a later merge to drop, so the memtable and the journal can hold one duplicate fewer and the key set is the same. Pinned in `crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/existsaddr/tests.rs` by `a_queued_connect_is_prepared_before_the_subscriber_claims_the_writer`, `the_index_lock_is_held_from_a_prepare_until_its_connect`, `a_connect_with_no_copy_takes_the_overlay_once_it_has_the_writer`, `a_prepared_connect_leaves_the_index_as_a_connect_inside_its_transaction_does`, `a_flush_between_a_prepare_and_its_transaction_loses_no_key`, `what_was_prepared_stays_good_across_a_flush_and_another_connect`, `what_was_prepared_for_one_connect_is_not_used_for_another_notification`, `an_update_that_fails_after_the_prepare_leaves_the_index_as_it_was` and `a_connect_delivered_again_after_its_update_rolled_back_starts_afresh`. The versions are pinned in `crates/dcroxide-indexers/src/existsaddr/memtable.rs` by `a_version_moves_with_every_change_to_its_partition_and_never_back`, `an_insert_searches_only_the_partitions_changed_since_its_filter`, `filters_that_race_inserts_leave_no_key_twice`, `random_interleavings_leave_what_a_search_for_every_key_leaves` and `a_seal_leaves_a_filter_standing_and_the_fold_after_it_does_not`, the seal by `a_fold_leaves_keys_inserted_since_the_seal`, `an_unseal_puts_the_sealed_keys_back` and `inserts_beside_a_fold_lose_nothing`, and in `tests.rs` beside it by `what_one_index_prepared_is_not_used_by_another`, `a_scripted_run_writes_the_rows_it_wrote_when_the_hook_searched_for_every_key`, `a_hook_that_trusts_its_versions_leaves_what_one_that_searches_leaves`, `a_connect_through_the_subscriber_searches_the_memtable_once` and `a_blocks_journal_row_holds_the_bytes_it_always_held`. They were measured on m3 on 2026-10-13 only together with the drop of retired overlay layers after the writer is released (the `database` row), as one step of a series of builds, one run against one: the flush log's total of the waits for the writer by the threads other than the index thread, which bounds the block thread's from above, less the flushes the index thread ran, was 8.1 to 9.5 s in the eleven runs of earlier code that session that committed on the flushing thread and changed no other setting and 1.6 to 1.7 s in all five runs of builds with the two, and the run was 7.0 s shorter with the flushes' commit seconds taken out and 6.5 s shorter by the node's clock less its flush time, which a step between two single runs resolves only to 3.5 to 6 s either way. How the fall in the wait divides between the two was not measured, and the code as committed was first run in a sync as committed inside `8fc9160`, four times on 2026-10-16 at a median of 430.7 s, where its own step was not measured apart (docs/bench-ledger.md). The thread checks for the shutdown request before every entry, so nothing queued is processed after it, where dcrd's `select` may still take entries. And the daemon joins the thread before its final flush, where dcrd flushes first (`server.go:3505-3506`). In-process tests, the vectors and `dcroxide-bench replay` still deliver inline through `ChainNtfnHandler::set_index_subscriber` or the subscriber itself. Until 2026-10-11 the daemon delivered inline as well, on the block-processing thread, and the cost of that was measured: over m2's 200,000-block tail the default ran at a median of 482 blk/s against 501 with `--noexistsaddrindex`, a gap inside that storage's run-to-run noise, at 9% more CPU time and 23% more bytes written (docs/bench-ledger.md, "Exists-address index layout 3"), and from genesis on m3 one sync with the index off took 17% less time than the default's median (the same ledger, "The parity commit, the index off, two levers and a full replay"). With the thread, two default syncs on m3 took 759.1 s and 756.2 s where one of the commit before it took 820.3 s (the same ledger, "Five changes on the block-processing thread"). The step before the transaction, the inline comparison of the index's keys (the exists-address entry under Known remaining gaps) and the single lookup of each ticket bucket per best-node write (the `blockchain/stake` row) were measured together and not one at a time: on m3 on 2026-10-12, on a quiet machine, one default sync took 628.0 s with the three (`6271436`) and 673.8 s in the run before it without them (`a83f9a1`), 45.8 s or 6.8% less, one run each. By the database's wait timer (the `database` row), the threads other than the index thread waited 30.7 s for the writer in 166,810 waits where they had waited 74.9 s in 401,906, and the index thread 30.4 s in 97,538 where it had waited 0.02 s in 295 (the same ledger, "Eleven changes, a background commit not kept and a pool gate"). After each update, dcrd's `maybeNotifySubscribers` reads the chain tip and the index tip back only to decide whether to signal the index's sync waiters. The port makes those two reads only while a waiter is registered. The daemon registers none, because its RPC seams poll the index tip, so each update skips a read transaction and a chain-lock acquisition. The one consequence: a tip read that would fail right after a committed update surfaces on the next update's opening tip read, not on this one.  The interior sharing is `Arc`/`Mutex` rather than `Rc`/`RefCell` — the index handles are `Arc<Mutex<TxIndex>>`/`Arc<Mutex<ExistsAddrIndex>>`, the chain queryer trait carries a `Send + Sync` supertrait so its `Arc<dyn ChainQueryer>` handles cross threads, and the interrupt and sync waiters are `AtomicBool`s instead of `Cell`s — so the indexes are `Send` and can run under the daemon's threaded server (a compile-time assertion pins it); this is thread-safety plumbing with no behavioral change, confirmed by the unchanged vectors, and the daemon consumes it through the node crate's index wiring (see the daemon row).  The package's log lines are dcrd's, at dcrd's levels, written to the `dcroxide_database::LogSink` the caller passes to `IndexSubscriber::new` (for the subscriber and every index created over it) and to each public drop function, where dcrd writes them to the package logger installed by `indexers.UseLogger`; both the daemon and addblock bind it to INDX, as dcrd's `log.go:90` and `cmd/addblock/addblock.go:78` do.  The lines: the catch-up's "Catching up from height X to Y" and "Caught up to height Y" with an `Indexed` `progresslog.BlockProgressLogger` line every ten seconds, the recovery's "<index>: recovering from tip H (hash)" and "<index>: index recovered to tip H (hash)" with a `Recovered` progress logger, "Resuming <index> drop", every drop's "Dropping all <index> entries.  This might take a while...", "Deleted N keys (M total) from <index>" per non-empty batch and "Dropped <index>", "Not dropping <index> because it does not exist" (the transaction and exists address indexes only; the legacy drops stay silent then, and log "Dropping all legacy <index> entries" otherwise), the dependent relay's trace line, and the transaction index's block-ID scan trace lines with its "Current internal block ID" debug line.  As in dcrd, the transaction index drop sets its drop marker before its "Dropping all" line and the exists address index drop sets it after.  The progress line renders its duration truncated to 10ms in Go's `Duration` form and the block time in pinned UTC where dcrd uses the host zone, as addblock's import progress line does.  Each enabled index is announced ("Transaction index is enabled", "Exists address index is enabled"; INDX in the daemon, MAIN in addblock) just ahead of its creation, as dcrd's `newServer` and `newBlockImporter` do, so a resumed drop or a recovery logs between the two announcements.  `Run`'s trace line "Index subscriber stopped" is logged once the daemon's index thread has been joined; `handleSyncSubscribers`' line has no counterpart because nothing runs the ticker, and `Run`'s "unable to stop index subscription" cannot occur; a thread that cannot be started fails startup with "Unable to start server: index thread: <error>"; a failed update is logged by the daemon (see the daemon row).  Pinned by a 4-scenario scripted session generated inside dcrd's own package over its `testChain` and chaingen blocks, replayed against a real redb-backed database: creation, catch-up, connects, disconnects, a reorg with recovery on re-init, drop and re-creation, entry queries, an address-soup block covering every key type, the unconfirmed overlay, independent and dependent subscriptions incl. the lagging-dependent relay, and the error kinds — comparing the index tips and the full byte-for-byte contents of every index bucket after each operation (the exists address index's bucket by the keys it holds, from `ExistsAddrIndex::logical_keys`, and its version row checked to read 3 and compared as dcrd's 2), run four times: at the default limits, and with a flush after every operation at tiny merge limits, with every partition but the last key's merged at every flush, and with every partition merged at every flush, so the journal, both run rewrites and the journal's garbage collection run inside dcrd's own scenarios; the log lines by `the_indexers_log_dcrd_lines_to_the_sink` over the same chain, the per-batch totals and the progress logger's throttle, pluralization and rendering by unit tests, and the daemon's and addblock's INDX wiring end to end by `crates/dcroxide-node/tests/review_index_logs.rs` |
| `internal/mempool` | `dcroxide-mempool` | vectors | Complete: the mempool error identities (all 27 `ErrorKind`s with dcrd’s exact names incl. the historical "ErrorAlreadyVoted" spelling (pinned, in dcrd's order, by a unit test ported from its `TestErrorKindStringer` table), and the `RuleError` that wraps either a mempool kind or an underlying chain rule error with dcrd’s `wrapTxRuleError` kind-retention semantics) and the relay policy layer from `policy.go`: `calcMinRequiredTxRelayFee` (with the Go overflow wrap clamped to the max amount), `isDust` (the 6030-atom p2pkh boundary at the default relay fee, unspendable outputs, and wrapped arithmetic on extreme values), `checkPkScriptStandard` (version gate plus the 1-to-3-key multisig limits), `checkTransactionStandard` (serialize type, finality, size, signature script size and push-only rules with the treasury spend input skip, output standardness with the dust and null-data-count exemptions by transaction type), and `checkInputsStandard` (referenced script forms and the 15-sigop P2SH limit, with the vote and treasury-spend first-input skips; the utxo view lookup is a closure like the other engine-coupled ports).  Pinned by 69 verdicts from dcrd’s own package over its policy test tables plus boundary, overflow, and skip-semantics cases, incl. input checks against a real `UtxoViewpoint`.  Also ported: the `TxPool` itself — the main, orphan, and stage pools with their outpoint indexes, vote and treasury spend tracking (the vote metadata in dcrd's vote tracker, `6444f057`, each block's entry pruned once the stake pruning runs more than ten blocks past the height its first vote claims, `d54099ad`), the complete acceptance gauntlet (`maybeAcceptTransaction`: duplicate and known-transaction checks, the chain sanity and input validation delegated to the ported blockchain layers, expiry, the stake-type height gates, the vote height policy (old votes, and votes claiming a height more than five above the tip, `a9b79d1b`) and the voted-block check (an unknown block, or a claimed height other than the header's, `6816d9de`), both right after the stake validation height gate, ahead of the revocation gate and every later check, as in dcrd, standardness, the ticket stake difficulty gate, the mixpool pair-request and mixing-misbehavior checks (`NonMixSpendsPairRequest` and `MisbehavingMixSpend`, the second ported from master `3c8a1734`, after `6f6cf21b`, both behind one optional `MixpoolProbe` trait object standing in for dcrd's two config closures, which dcrd's server always sets together; with dcrd's nil-closure semantics when absent, both steps skipped), pool/vote/revocation double spend checks, the already-exists check over the fetched view, orphan detection with fraud proof refresh, sequence locks, sigop limits, both fee policy directions, script verification via the newly ported single-transaction `ValidateTransactionScripts` (sharing the block path's engine setup, failure text and scoped workers, which every block check the script pool does not take still runs on (*Script checks of a connected block* under Deliberate divergences), and treating only an absent view entry as missing, as dcrd's `UtxoViewpoint.PrevScript` does), the winning-ticket check on recent votes after the scripts (`023c42ff`; a vote below the old-vote cutoff, which only `AllowOldVotes` admits, is not looked up, and a failed lookup is a plain error), and the treasury spend expiry/limit/Pi-key policy), orphan processing with cascading acceptance and double spend eviction, ticket staging with unstaging through `MaybeAcceptDependents`, batch acceptance over the transient pool (`MaybeAcceptTransactions`), the stake and expiry pruners (the stake pruner also prunes the vote metadata, last, as dcrd's `pruneStakeTx` does), and the removal/query surface.  The chain callbacks live behind a `PoolChain` trait standing in for dcrd’s config closures (its `header_by_hash` answers an `Option`, since dcrd's real lookup fails only with `ErrUnknownBlock`, so the port has no counterpart of `checkVoteBlock`'s branch for other errors, which the chain cannot reach; `winning_tickets_by_hash` is dcrd's `WinningTicketsByHash`); the fee estimator hook and notification callbacks are integration plumbing that landed with the daemon wiring (the mining view bookkeeping landed with the mempool wiring, and the pool now carries dcrd's exists-address hook: an optional `UnconfirmedAddrIndexer` trait object standing in for the concrete `ExistsAddrIndex` config field, notified with the transaction at the end of `addTransaction` exactly where dcrd calls `AddUnconfirmedTx` — dcrd has no removal hook, the overlay flushes wholesale on block connect — pinned by a native test replaying the battery's first acceptance against a recording index), and dcrd’s time-driven orphan expiration and randomized orphan eviction are ported but deliberately untriggered by the vectors since dcrd itself documents the eviction order as arbitrary; the expiration runs on a monotonic clock (`PoolChain::now_mono_nanos`), as dcrd compares `time.Now()` readings (`mempool.go:554-566`, `:612`), and a native test steps the wall and monotonic clocks apart (`orphan_expiry_follows_the_monotonic_clock`).  Pinned by a 90-row scripted session (154 rows with the `log` rows described below) against dcrd’s own pool test harness (`newPoolHarness` with real signed transactions over a mirrored fake chain): chained acceptance, orphan cascades and policy, duplicates, double spends, already-exists, fee policy both directions, block- and time-based sequence locks, expiry with pruning, ticket staging and unstaging on parent confirmation, stake difficulty gates with pruning, batch acceptance, and double spend removal — comparing every verdict, accepted list, and the full pool/orphan/stage state after every operation.  Notable dump landmine: the coinbase height lives in the signature script, which is witness data excluded from the transaction hash, so distinct funding coinbases must vary their prefixes or they alias the same outpoints.  Also pinned: the stake acceptance scenarios over a 394-row file of eight sections (533 rows with the `log` rows) from dcrd's own harness, written by `tools/mempoolvectors` (an overlay run inside dcrd's `internal/mempool`) at `382ff4dd`: a votes session — the vote orphan flow (vote rejected as an orphan, the ticket arriving as an orphan itself, the funding transaction cascading the ticket into the stage pool, and the mined ticket unlocking the vote), a second vote by a ticket on a block, the five-vote double spend limit across candidate blocks with recovery after removal, an old vote refused as old ahead of the five-vote limit, the vote and revocation stake validation height gates, revocation acceptance with the one-revocation-per-ticket rule, disapproval tallies over no-votes, and the pruning of the votes and their metadata at the ten-block boundary; dcrd's `TestDuplicateVoteRejection`, `TestVoteHeightPolicy`, `TestVoteValidity` and `TestVotePruning`, with a vote-metadata row after every step where dcrd checks it; the two clocks of the stake pruning (`voteclocks`); where the vote checks sit among the others, the stake validation height gate included (`voteorder`); and the treasury battery with a replaced Pi key set: the seven-concurrent-tspend limit with recovery, mined-on-ancestor rejection, the expiry policy in all three directions, the chain-level `ErrInvalidPiSignature`/`ErrUnknownPiKey` rejections, acceptance up to the final voting window block, treasury adds under the fee policy, and standalone treasurybase rejection.  The votes vote on the harness's mock blocks, whose headers carry the claimed height, with their tickets registered as the blocks' winners.  A variant of the tool with the earlier vote and subsidy-split scripts, not kept (the committed tool needs `382ff4dd`'s harness), gave back the earlier files byte for byte at `6f6cf21b`; the tool itself, at `382ff4dd`, gave back the 90-row transaction pool session, the treasury section and the auto-revocations section byte for byte as before, and, now that it writes the pool's log lines as `log` rows, gives back every other row of the three files byte for byte.  Vote transactions are pruned by the height they were accepted at and their metadata by the height the voted block's first vote claims, so a vote accepted late outlives its metadata, and until it leaves the pool a second vote by its ticket on the same block passes the double-vote check, up to the five-vote cap, as in dcrd (pinned by the unit test `a_late_vote_outlives_its_metadata_and_its_ticket_may_vote_again`).  A vote on a block the node knows only by its header passes the voted-block check, since the header lookup answers from the block index as dcrd's does, and then fails the winning-ticket lookup, whose stake node needs the block's data: a plain error, logged by the sync manager as `Failed to process transaction` and not relayed, as in dcrd (`chain.go:1666-1673` and `stakenode.go:63-84` at `382ff4dd`; pinned by `crates/dcroxide-node/tests/vote_header_only.rs`).  This is dcrd's behaviour at `382ff4dd`, reproduced, not judged; whether upstream intends it is not settled here.  Also pinned: the agenda-active pool semantics mainnet runs under, by a 61-row session (79 rows with the `log` rows) from dcrd’s own harness (`tools/mempoolvectors`) replaying `TestRevocationsWithAutoRevocationsEnabled`, `TestSubsidySplitSemantics` and `TestSubsidySplitR2Semantics` (`tests/review_agendas.rs`).  With automatic revocations active, a v2 revocation carrying a fee fails `ErrRegTxCreateStakeOut`, a v1 one fails `ErrInvalidRevocationTxVersion`, a zero-fee v2 one is accepted, and the next stake prune drops it; a vote paying the other side of the DCP0010 or DCP0012 split fails `ErrBadStakebaseAmountIn` and the matching one is accepted, with each agenda both off and on.  The pool and the mining transaction graph are `Send` for the threaded daemon (shared descriptors on `Arc` instead of `Rc`, the chain parameters owned instead of borrowed, including the copy the subsidy cache holds), pinned by a compile-time assertion; the frozen vectors confirm behavior unchanged.  The pool now runs in the daemon: a chain adapter binds every seam to the live chain (the header lookup from the block index, side chain and header-only blocks included; a block's winning tickets from `Chain::lottery_data_for_block`, which rebuilds a stake node no longer held in memory, as dcrd's `LotteryDataForBlock` does, so on testnet3, with a maximum vote age of 1440 blocks against 288 stake nodes in memory, a valid old vote can cost a rebuild, as in dcrd; the utxo view for a transaction's inputs and own outputs with the disapproved-tip-tree disconnect (memoized per tip like dcrd's `disapprovedView`; the port keeps one memo per chain adapter rather than one on the chain, which serves the identical view) and the view's missing outpoints resolved in one batched cache/backend read (dcrd `fetchUtxosMain` over `FetchEntries`), the sequence-lock calculation over the tip branch, the six agenda queries at the tip, the tspend-mined-on-ancestor walk, and dcrd's standard script verify flags with the LN-features and treasury additions), the policy carries dcrd's exact values (orphan size = the standard tx cap, sigops = a fifth of the block cap, the per-network vote age switch), the mixpool probe is installed by the shared mixing pool's own constructor so no daemon path can build a pool that skips the pair-request or the misbehavior step, the netsync manager drives the shared pool instead of the null stub, and the RPC server serves it (getrawmempool, mempool counts, tspend hashes, verbose descriptors with derived dependencies (one entry per redeeming input, so a parent spent twice is listed twice, as dcrd's `VerboseTxDescs` appends them; the RPC descriptors carry the serialized size rather than a copy of the transaction), the gettxout mempool probe, and sendrawtransaction submitting through the pool with dcrd's duplicate/rule error mapping over the recently-confirmed filter).  The mempool now serves the network too: a mempool request answers the pool's inventory through the ported flood guard (queued for the peer's next trickle, which sends it in inv messages of at most dcrd's `maxInvTrickleSize`, 1000 vectors), a transaction getdata serves from the pool only — confirmed transactions are deliberately not servable, matching dcrd — and answers notfound otherwise, and a transaction accepted off the wire feeds the websocket txaccepted/relevant-tx notifications (dcrd `AnnounceNewTransactions`' notification half).  Accepted transactions now relay to peers too: every registered peer carries its relay state (the handshake facts including the remote's disable-relay bit read off the version message, the last announced block, and a known-inventory set that dedups announcements and never echoes inventory the peer itself announced), the fan-out drives the ported per-peer relay decision (inventory that is not immediate waits for the peer's next trickle, and block announcements are queued at once, as dcrd's `QueueInventory` and `QueueInventoryImmediate` do), announced transactions join the recently-advertised cache (4500 entries, 45 seconds) that getdata consults before the pool, and RPC-submitted transactions announce through the connection manager's relay seam.  The pool also follows the chain now: a connected block's transactions leave the pool (without evicting their now-valid redeemers), staged tickets unstage, conflicting pool transactions and matching orphans evict, and newly acceptable orphans run the full announce cascade; a block that disapproves its parent returns the parent's regular transactions to contention, and a disconnected block's transactions re-admit — all deferred through the chain event handler and drained after each processing call, since the pool reaches back into the chain the callback holds.  The drain runs every event with the chain already at the end of the processing call, where dcrd runs each at the tip that event produced, so three pieces of pool state that depend on that tip are reproduced: each admitted descriptor records the event's height (`TxPool::with_event_height`); a run of disconnects hands each readmission the transactions of the blocks the run detaches next, and the fork block's regular tree when the next connect disapproves it, as dcrd's chain still holds them there (`DisconnectRun`, `TxPool::with_transient`); and an orphan that a later connect of the same batch mined is taken as accepted without being pooled when dcrd's chain or pool holds its inputs at the earlier event, so its orphan redeemers are processed there (`TxPool::process_orphans_accepted_in_batch`).  The remaining differences are under Open.  The pool logs every line `mempool.go` logs, at dcrd's level and with dcrd's text, through the logger the daemon binds to TXMP, and the three pool replays compare the lines logged for each call with the ones dcrd's pool logged for it (*The mempool's and the template generator's lines below info* under Accepted).  The rebroadcast machinery (the timer thread with its stake-aware prunes), block announcements over the relay, and the fee estimator hooks all run in the daemon now, each detailed in the daemon row |
| `internal/fees` | `dcroxide-fees` | vectors | The smart fee estimator behind `estimatesmartfee`: exponentially spaced fee rate buckets (incl. the extra-bucket insertion and the +inf catch-all), decaying confirmation tracking with dcrd’s exact floating point accounting, mempool tracking with the deliberate integer-division rate downsampling (at Go's `int64` semantics, `estimator.go:790`: `i64::MIN / -1` and the product by 1000 wrap as they do in dcrd, where the port's `/` once aborted on the former; no mempool entry has a fee or size that reaches either, and `tests/rate_wrapping.rs` pins both) and the tspend/below-minimum exclusions, mined transaction processing across confirmation ranges (incl. height gaps and stale block rejection), the median fee estimation with all four error kinds, and the database bucket row codec (big-endian float bits).  The leveldb persistence (`loadFromDatabase`/`updateDatabase` over `<datadir>/feesdb`) is not ported. Only the row format is pinned. The restart-visible consequence is recorded under Open.  Pinned by 425 rows from dcrd’s own package over two estimator configurations: every estimate and raw median compared, and four complete bucket state snapshots (confirmed and mempool) compared bit for bit through the row codec — the floating point decay math replays exactly |
| `internal/mining` | `dcroxide-mining` | vectors | Complete: the mining view layer — the transaction descriptor types (`TxDesc`, `TxAncestorStats`, `VoteDesc`, `UnminedHeight`), the dependency graph (`txDescGraph`) with its topological walks, the `TxMiningView` with ancestor statistics tracking bounded by the 25-entry limit (incl. the retrack-on-removal and descendant-pruning edge behaviors), cloning with source isolation, rejection cascades, the transaction priority queue with a faithful port of Go's `container/heap` sift operations so pop order matches dcrd exactly incl. among equal elements, the stake-priority ordering (votes over automatic revocations over tickets), and `CalcPriority` with the input-age math.  The redeemer/locator closures dcrd stores in the graph are passed per call for ownership; behavior is identical.  The mempool now wires the view in with dcrd's exact call order (`dcroxide-mempool` grew the `EnableAncestorTracking` policy, the add/remove hooks, and `MiningView`/`miningDescs`), re-using these types.  Pinned by 342 rows from dcrd's own package over a thin source mirroring exactly what the mempool provides the view: the dependency diamond with out-of-order arrival, removals with and without descendant stat updates, clone isolation, rejection, the tracking limit over a 27-chain with front removals and reversed re-adds (225 full stats/relationship states), the priority queue pop order incl. ties, and the priority float math bit for bit.  Notable dump landmine: a fresh `dcrutil.Tx` carries tree `TxTreeUnknown`, so a source that skips the mempool’s `SetTree` never forms redeemer edges.  Also ported: the template building blocks from `mining.go` — `SortParentsByVotes` (majority filtering, vote-descending order, and the top-block tie handling; Go’s sort is insertion sort at these sizes so the stable Rust sort matches), the `BlockTemplate` type, the standard coinbase/treasurybase OP_RETURN scripts (dcrd autogenerates the extra nonce; it is injected here and the vectors extract dcrd’s from its own output), `createCoinbaseTx` (the block-one ledger payout path, the pre-treasury organization/OP_TRUE outputs, the treasury-era version-3 form, and the pay-to-address/anyone-redeemable variants) and `createTreasuryBaseTx` byte for byte, `calcBlockMerkleRoot`/`calcBlockCommitmentRootV1`, `minimumMedianTime`, and `calcFeePerKb` with the negative-ancestor-stats fallback.  Pinned by 29 rows from dcrd’s own package: eleven coinbases across networks, heights, voter counts, agenda states, and subsidy split variants, four treasurybases, the parent sorting scenarios, the fee rate floats bit for bit, and the merkle/commitment roots.  Also ported: the full `NewBlockTemplate` assembly (`BlkTmplGenerator`) — the priority-queue driven mempool scan with dependency ordering, the vote/ticket/revocation stake tree handling with the vote-bits tally and too-few-voters parent recycling (`handleTooFewVoters` with the reorganization callback), the automatic revocation queue realized from dcrd's goto as a labeled loop, treasury base and treasury spend inclusion with the expenditure gate, the fee redistribution through the coinbase with the participation-scaled fees at stake validation height (`txFees` keeps dcrd's -1 placeholder overwritten with the negated total, and `txSigOpCounts` counts the coinbase twice like dcrd), block size accounting with the header overhead, both template roots over the accumulated `blockUtxos` view (whose `PrevScript` serves spent entries exactly as dcrd's viewpoint does), the median-adjusted timestamp (its `--miningtimeoffset` shift is dcrd's `time.Duration(-MiningTimeOffset) * time.Second` bit for bit, `mining.go:756-757`: the negation and the nanosecond product wrap in `int64`, and taking the whole-second time floors the sum, so an offset beyond ±9,223,372,036 s now moves the header timestamps of getwork, getblocktemplate and the CPU miner, and the time handed to `CalcNextRequiredDifficulty`, to where dcrd's moves them rather than by plain seconds, and `i64::MIN` no longer overflows; pinned by `a_huge_mining_time_offset_wraps_in_nanoseconds_like_go` in `tests/review_template_queue.rs`), and `UpdateBlockTime`/`updateExtraNonce` (`maybeInsertStakeTx` and the revocation builder included).  The chain callbacks live behind a `TemplateChain` trait mirroring dcrd's mining config closures, and the transaction source behind `TemplateTxSource`, implemented by the mempool's `TxPool`.  Pinned by a 138-row scripted session against dcrd's own mining harness comparing thirteen template calls byte for byte (nine templates, four injected errors), with dcrd's wall clock and random extra nonces recovered from its own emitted templates: fee redistribution below stake validation height, both payment address variants, the error injection paths, an SVH template with four votes and a fresh ticket purchase over a mirrored fake chain, the too-few-voters parent recycle, and dcrd's two automatic-revocation template tests (`TestNewBlockTemplateAutoRevocations` and `...VotesOnly`) with DCP0009 active.  Those cover revocations built for missed winning tickets (one of them because its vote fails validation) and for expiring tickets, source revocations of previously missed tickets being mined, an ineligible ticket's revocation being skipped, and the requeue after a votes-only queue drains; the votes-only case also runs under DCP0010 and DCP0012.  The TVI treasury-spend branch has no template vector, because dcrd has no template test for it.  Notable dump landmine: dcrd's test harness yields mining descriptors in Go map order, which leaks into the heap insertion order and the pop order of equal-key votes, so the dump pins a deterministic hash-sorted order mirrored by the replay.  Also ported: the background template generator's regeneration state machine (`bgblktmplgenerator`) — the regen handler state with its timers modeled as armed flags whose real countdowns the daemon drives, the block connect/disconnect/accept, vote, template-update, and force-regen event handlers with the reorg bracket and the unsynced gating, the minimum-votes lock-in ladder with the max-votes propagation timeout (a vote below the maximum with the timeout armed is deliberately a no-op), side chain vote tracking with vote-driven and timeout-driven forced reorganizations (a failed reorg candidate falls through to monitoring, and with no template yet one is generated on the parent), the notified-parents LRU of size 3 with the new-votes→new-parent first-notification upgrade, and the stale-template wait counting.  Generation launches are recorded as requests the daemon executes (`genTemplateAsync` is inherently asynchronous) with completions flowing back through `process_generated_template`, and the timer select arms are exposed as `handle_*_timeout` functions for the daemon loop.  Pinned by 28 state rows from dcrd's own package driving its real handlers over a scripted chain and transaction source with generation failing fast through a scripted policy error, across eleven scenarios compared field for field.  Notable dump landmines: `dcrutil.NewBlock` caches the block hash eagerly, so sibling variants must bake distinct nonces into the header before construction (a nonce set afterward silently leaves the hash — and the scenario — degenerate); the reorg-started path clears the template synchronously through the unbuffered regen event queue, so an in-package driver must drain concurrently or deadlock; and the dump blocks' zero `time.Time` serializes as `uint32(-62135596800)` in the header codec, which participates in their hashes.  The concurrency shell — the regen queue and subscription fan-out goroutines, the real timers, and the blocking template retrieval waits — is not in this crate; it lives in the daemon's `bgtemplate.rs`, which drives this synchronous state machine from a dedicated thread (see the daemon row).  The generation, the vote handler and the solve core log `mining.go`'s, `bgblktmplgenerator.go`'s and `cpuminer.go`'s lines through the logger the daemon binds to MINR (*The mempool's and the template generator's lines below info* under Accepted) |
| `internal/ratelimit` | `dcroxide-ratelimit` | vectors | Complete (new in dcrd 2.2; ported from master `452c1a6c`, the campaign parity target): the token bucket rate limiter behind the rewritten connection manager's inbound anti-flood machinery — `New`/`Allow`/`Tokens`/`Burst`/`UntilNextAllowed` with dcrd's exact `f64` bucket arithmetic in dcrd's operation order, incl. `Duration.Seconds()`'s whole-second/remainder-nanosecond split, the burst cap, the clock-going-backwards refill freeze, and the `UntilNextAllowed` overflow guard returning `Forever`.  Divergences per the port's conventions: dcrd's struct-embedded `nowFn` becomes an explicit monotonic `now_nanos` argument on every time-dependent call (the daemon feeds the inbound group and drop-log limiters `dcroxide_connmgr::monotonic_nanos`, a process-`Instant` reading, because Go's `Time.Sub` of two `time.Now` values is monotonic; `runtime.rs`'s `admit_inbound_now` reads the clocks for `ConnManager::admit_inbound`, and `log_inbound_drop` reads its own, as dcrd's `LogDrops` does; the wall clock is kept only where dcrd reads it: the limiter cache's TTL, which `container/lru` keeps in `UnixNano`, and the flood window's `Unix()` seconds (`InboundRateLimiter::allow(addr, now_unix, now_nanos, mono_nanos)`); the persistent-peer backoff's `time.Since(lastAttempt)` is stamped and compared on the same monotonic clock; tests at each of these call sites fail when that site reads the wall clock), the embedded mutex is left to consumers (the core is single-threaded like the rest of the port), dcrd's zero-`time.Time` initial `updated` is an `i64::MIN` sentinel that yields the saturated `MaxInt64` refill interval for every clock value — incl. after an `allow` at the sentinel itself, which dcrd likewise cannot distinguish from the initial state — and the overflow guard's `uint64(float64)` conversion (platform-defined in Go: NaN → guard fires on amd64 but not arm64) pins the amd64 oracle platform, so NaN and `+Inf` candidates return `Forever`.  Pinned by a 189-line scripted-clock dump from dcrd's own package (throwaway exporter in the clone) across twenty-one scenarios — the connmgr group and drop-log constants, fractional refill drift (incl. a case where accumulated rounding denies an event dcrd's documented average rate would allow), zero/negative-zero/negative/subnormal/NaN/infinite/huge rates (a `1e300` rate overflows the refill delta to `+Inf` before the burst cap), zero and `u32::MAX` bursts, clock reversal on both the failing and succeeding `Allow` paths, first-use far from the zero value, and the rates at the `int64` nanosecond horizon (`1e-9` tokens/s truncates to `999999999999999872` ns; `1e-10` and below return `Forever`) — every post-op token count and update stamp compared bit for bit, plus a native test pinning the sentinel-stamp corner against bits probed from dcrd's package directly (unreachable by the exporter's base-plus-offset clock) |
| `mixing`, `mixing/mixpool`, `mixing/utxoproof` | `dcroxide-mixing` | vectors | The mixing library primitives — the `Message` interface as a `MixMessage` trait over the eight wire messages, message signing and verification (the signed-data BLAKE-256 digest fed into the tagged, comma-joined `schnorrHash` with the pair request's zero session and run, then EC-Schnorr-DCRv0), session ID derivation and pair request session sorting (the lexicographic pre-sort, the epoch-tagged digest, and the sid-XOR re-sort into unmixed positions) with key exchange session validation and its two error identities, the DC-net math over F = 2**127-1 (`SRMixPads`/`SRMix`/`AddVectors`/`Coefficients`/`IsRoot`, `DCMixPads`/`DCMix`, the message vectors with XOR combining) carried as canonical residues in a Mersenne-folding u128 field — dcrd computes over unreduced `math/big` integers with a final `Mod(F)`, and every dcrd-observable output is that canonical residue — the per-run ChaCha20 PRNG, the UTXO ownership proofs (`utxoproof`), `MaxExpiry`, and the mixing limits/flags/script class constants.  Pinned by a scripted session generated inside dcrd's own mixing package: the identity hash and signed-data digest of all eight messages, deterministic RFC6979 signatures byte for byte with verification and a tampering rejection, session sorting and validation across its error cases, a full honest 4-peer exponential and XOR DC-net run (pads, mixes, power sums, coefficients, and root checks with recovery of every peer message), field membership, PRNG keystreams, ownership proofs both directions, and expiries across three networks.  Also ported: `mixpool` — the in-memory pool of mixing messages with the full acceptance gauntlet (signature verification, the pair request battery: change dust/version/script checks, missing UTXOs, message counts, mix dust, total amounts, the expiry window, script class, the fee band, and the UTXO checks over an optional fetcher trait incl. duplicate outpoints, unsupported P2SH, spent/unconfirmed/wrong-version outputs, outputs an unmined transaction already spends (dcrd's `mixpoolChain.FetchUtxoEntry` asks `mempool.IsSpent` before the chain and returns a nil entry, which `mixpool.go:1468` folds into the same rejection as a spent one — the port takes that answer as a predicate computed by the caller instead: dcrd reaches the fetcher from `checkAcceptPR`, which `AcceptMessage` runs before it takes the mixpool's mutex (`mixpool.go:1214-1226`), but the port's acceptance runs wholly under the mixpool guard, so asking from inside would take the transaction pool's lock while holding the mixpool's, closing a cycle against the acceptance gauntlet's mixpool probe, which takes them the other way round; the consequence is that the port asks the question before the pool's own rerun, already-accepted and pair-request checks (and before it consults the signature verdict it was handed), where dcrd asks after all of them, bounded by the wire's 512-outpoint cap on a pair request and pinned by a lock-order test that deadlocks on the other arrangement.  The message itself is handled in dcrd's lock scope: each received mix message is hashed once by the thread that read it (the connection's input thread, or its `peer-reader` thread where it has one; *Reading ahead of the block handler* under Deliberate divergences), as it is read and before any lock, and the signature is verified once, lazily, only after netsync's rejected-message filter and a brief already-accepted probe of the pool have passed the message, and with no lock held (`HashedMessage::verify`, `Pool::needs_signature_check`), as dcrd verifies inside `AcceptMessage` between its read-locked already-accepted check and its write lock (`mixpool.go:1205-1214`), so a replayed rejected or pooled message costs lookups and no Schnorr verify; the pair-request checks — the chain UTXO fetches and one ownership-proof verification per claimed UTXO — still run under the mixpool guard where dcrd runs them unlocked, so validating a large pair request briefly holds up the mixpool's other users (getdata serving, `MisbehavingBlock`, the mempool's `NonMixSpendsPR` and `MisbehavingTx` probes)), the opcode-dispatched ownership proof over regular and stake script kinds, and the summed value match; key exchange validation with session checks, position bounds, the early-epoch gate and, since `3ce5cc41` and `874f6772`, the late gate (an epoch more than twenty minutes past, one at or above 2**63 included) and the whole-minute rule (`mixpool.go:1685-1692` at `382ff4dd`), the two gates comparing the epoch with the wall clock in Go's internal seconds (the late gate then in nanoseconds) and the minute rule judging the epoch alone, own-PR-missing orphaning with `MissingOwnPRError`, identity and pairing compatibility; the per-type message limits with dcrd's exact messages incl. the CT limit message that prints the ciphertext count where the maximum belongs, and none for a key exchange, whose seen pair requests only the wire bounds, at 512, since `6b65e0d1` dropped `MaxPeers` from `checkAcceptKE` (`mixpool.go:1669` at `382ff4dd`); conflict detection, including a second key exchange by one identity in a session (`ffb42adf`, `mixpool.go:1703-1722` at `382ff4dd`), with dcrd's plain error for a session that records a message `RemoveMessage` dropped — dcrd's map order decides which of the two it reports when a session holds both, the port reports the one for the hash smaller in internal byte order (the reverse of the displayed hex); the per-identity message limit of `38811b0b` (896 messages over a pair request's life, counted over the identity's message index with removed messages included, refused on the direct path, for a key exchange after the same-session check and before its pair requests are looked up, so before it can be orphaned, and when orphans are reconsidered, `mixpool.go:46-51`, `:1333`, `:1641`, `:1724` at `382ff4dd`); and orphan handling with reconsideration cascades and source-based eviction), sessions with expiry (QK-0002, now retired: `release-v2.1.5` created every session with the maximum expiry because the slice it folded over was never appended to, so sessions only died through pair request expiry; dcrd fixed it in `d11ae7af`, which is in the current target, and `accept_ke` now folds `pr.Expires()` into the running minimum over the KE's known referenced pair requests exactly as master does, with the mixpool vectors regenerated against master so the session rows carry real expiries), the removal surface (rejected messages, sessions, confirmed sessions and mixes, spent and unresponsive pair requests) backed by the recently-removed LRU cache from dcroxide-containers, the pairing queries (`MixPRs`/`CompatiblePRs` with the expiry-ordered double-spend filter/`ReceiveKEsByPairing`), the receive collection semantics (QK-0003: dcrd's capacity-misuse error path leaks its read lock; the synchronous port returns the error), and the misbehavior observer (`CheckPrevEpoch` with the timeout blame analysis, strike sets with common-ownership merging, `MisbehavingTx`/`MisbehavingBlock`/`ExcludePRs`, and strike removal through completed mixes).  dcrd's mutexes, blocking `Receive` broadcast, background expiry task, and observer epoch ticker are daemon-phase concurrency: the port is synchronous with injectable wall and monotonic clocks (orphan age uses the monotonic one, as Go's `time.Since` over a `time.Now()` reading does; key exchange epochs use the wall clock, as dcrd's `time.Unix` comparisons do, with Go's internal-seconds arithmetic reproduced for out-of-range epochs, and so does the recently-removed cache's TTL, as `container/lru` stores and compares expiry in `UnixNano` (`map.go:175`, `:228`); the cache's lazy expiry-scan schedule is under Open), `receive` collects what is currently accepted (dcrd's pre-cancelled-context path, which its own observer uses), and the scheduled expiry latch is exposed for the daemon to drive.  Pinned by a 1107-row scripted session generated inside dcrd's own package at `382ff4dd` by `tools/mixpoolgen`, which builds and signs every message, over mock chains with and without UTXO fetching: 119 acceptances spanning every rule rejection and orphan cascade, the key exchange rules added since `6f6cf21b` (epochs too late, far past and off the minute, a second key exchange by an identity in its session, a session recording a removed message, 65 seen pair requests), expiry rounds, all removals, the pairing and receive queries, and two observer strike rounds against a timing-out peer in an 8-peer pairing — comparing the full pool state and strike table after every operation.  Every key exchange epoch in it but those of the four rows that pin the epoch gates is a whole minute inside dcrd's window, and the nanosecond edge of the late gate, which a capture at a whole second cannot show, is pinned by `key_exchanges_late_for_their_epoch_are_refused` (`crates/dcroxide-mixing/tests/mixpool_acceptance_rules.rs`). | `addrmgr` / `connmgr` / `peer` | `dcroxide-addrmgr` / `-connmgr` / `-peer` | vectors | Complete: `addrmgr` — network address types with dcrd's key and group key formatting (Go's `net.IP.String` semantics incl. the dotted rendering of IPv4-mapped forms and RFC5952 IPv6, `JoinHostPort` bracketing, the /16 IPv4 and /32 (/36 he.net) IPv6 groups with the tunnel extractions for 6to4/Teredo/RFC6052/RFC6145), the full RFC-range classification and routability rules, the reachability lattice and external-address candidacy, known-address viability (`chance` bit for bit and `isBad` across all five criteria), the manager itself (new/tried buckets over the double-BLAKE-256 bucket derivations, add with the 2N re-add likelihood, bad-then-oldest new-bucket expiry, attempt/connected/good transitions incl. the tried-bucket eviction that swaps the oldest tried address back into a new bucket, the address cache with its 23%-capped random subset, local address bookkeeping with priority scoring and best-local selection), and the `peers.json` serialization, written via serde with dcrd's exact field names — incl. QK-0004: dcrd never restores the serialized address timestamps on load, so loaded addresses are re-stamped with the load time (Go's zero time for the attempt/success fields round-trips exactly through its `Unix()` encoding, modeled as an optional).  The file is read with `json.NewDecoder(r).Decode` semantics. The first JSON value is framed as `Decoder.readValue` frames it. A file with nothing but whitespace is `EOF`. A syntax error is the scanner's first one inside the value, reported at the byte that breaks it. A value the file cuts short is `unexpected EOF`. A complete value is decoded without what follows it, and a number or literal ends at the first byte that cannot continue it. The value is then decoded through `dcroxide_dcrjson::gojson` into a Go type mirroring `serializedAddrManager`. Field names match case-insensitively and the last duplicate key wins. `null` and missing fields keep their zero values. `Version` and `Attempts` are 64-bit Go `int`s, so `KnownAddress.attempts` is `i64` and wraps on increment. `Key [32]byte` and the bucket arrays zero-fill or truncate. All error texts are Go 1.26's, the toolchain dcrd's release image uses. `load_peers` returns what dcrd's `loadPeers` logs: `Loaded %d addresses from file '%s'` from `numAddresses()`, or `Failed to parse file %s: %v` and `Failed to remove corrupt peers file %s: %v`. Open, read and remove failures carry Go's `*os.PathError` text (`open <path>: permission denied`, `read <path>: is a directory`, `remove <path>: directory not empty`), with the errno spelled as Go's `syscall.Errno` spells it on Unix. The removal ports `os.Remove`'s unlink-then-rmdir, so an empty `peers.json` directory is removed. A failed load keeps the counts it reached (QK-0023). Divergences: (1) a `null` entry in `Addresses` is a nil-pointer dereference that panics dcrd at every startup, since the file is never removed; the port rejects the file, removes it and starts empty. (2) With a duplicate `Addresses` key, Go's decoder reuses the structs the first occurrence allocated, so fields the second occurrence omits keep the first's values; gojson decodes each occurrence fresh. (3) A `LastAttempt`/`LastSuccess` outside the `i64`-nanosecond range (before 1678 or after 2262) saturates on load. `chance` and `isBad` stay exact, because `chance` compares with `saturating_sub` as `time.Since` saturates, but a later save writes the saturated seconds and not the file's value. (4) Off Unix, the file-error texts keep the system's message as Rust reports it, without the `(os error N)` suffix, and the removal does not reproduce `os.Remove`'s Windows attribute handling. (5) A read error partway through the file is reported as the read error even when the bytes read before it already hold a complete value or a syntax error, which Go's decoder would report first; this case is unreachable on a regular file.  dcrd's mutexes and the peers-file ticker goroutine are daemon-phase concurrency: the port is synchronous, and both the clock and the random source are injectable traits (dcrd's `crypto/rand` calls are not reproducible even in-package, so the dump pins every deterministic surface under a pinned bucket key and a lowered tried bucket size, and the randomized selection paths are covered natively with a scripted RNG).  The interior sharing is `Arc`/`Mutex` rather than `Rc`/`RefCell` — the clock is `Arc<dyn Fn + Send + Sync>`, the random source `Arc<Mutex<dyn AddrRng + Send>>`, and each shared known address `Arc<Mutex<KnownAddress>>` — so the manager is `Send` and can live in the daemon's shared server state across the peer threads (a compile-time assertion pins it); this is thread-safety plumbing with no behavioral change, confirmed by the unchanged vectors.  Pinned by a scripted session generated inside dcrd's own package: 27-address key/group/routability grids, the reachability and candidacy lattice, bucket derivations under the pinned key, manager state transitions through adds, attempts, connections, and good promotions incl. the eviction path with full per-address state comparison after every operation, viability values bit for bit through crafted serialized state, cross-loading dcrd's own `peers.json` output plus a save/load round trip, and the deserialization error cases.  The 2.2 campaign (upstream master 452c1a6c) ports the addrmgr v2 delta: Tor v3 onion addresses as a first-class type (`TorV3Address = 4` — value 3 stays retired for TorV2 — with the SHA3-256 `.onion checksum` derivation, 35-byte pubkey/checksum/version validation, the lowercase-base32 onion rendering in `Key`/`ipString`, the `torv3:<pubkey[0]&0xf>` group key checked before the IP-based groups, and `EncodeHost`'s onion branch that uppercases the 56 data characters for Go's standard base32 decode before falling back to IP parsing), per-bucket address-type statistics (`bucketStats` counters over every new/tried bucket, incremented and decremented at dcrd's exact add/expire/promote/evict/deserialize sites with Go's wrapping `uint16` arithmetic), and the filtered `GetAddress(filterFn)` selection (the all-false filter and no-matching-bucket early returns, bucket-index collection from the stats, the 50% tried/new split over matching buckets only, the nth-matching-entry walk, and the unchanged 1.2-factor chance-accept loop), plus the reachability-lattice delta (the new `Private` reach state for TorV3-to-TorV3 and the three TorV3 arms in `getRemoteReachabilityFromLocal`, checked before the RFC4380 case exactly as dcrd orders them; dcrd master's `NetAddress.Network()` net.Addr shim has no port equivalent yet — nothing consumes it before the piece-177/178 daemon surfaces); the daemon's `newAddressFunc` passes dcrd server.go's filter (IPv4/IPv6 always, TorV3 only when onion reachability is configured: `!noonion` and a proxy or onion proxy set).  dcrd 2.2 also relocated `seed.go` and `tor.go`→`tordns.go` from connmgr into addrmgr verbatim; the port moves them into `dcroxide-addrmgr` accordingly (the `ErrTor*` kinds join addrmgr's error kinds, and the reflected JSON node type in seeder decode errors becomes `addrmgr.node`), with the frozen tor/seed vector rows relocated alongside. QK-0006: Go dispatches `Exp` to platform assembly that differs from the portable code by one ulp on 276 of the 1801 possible decay ages, so dcrd's ban scores are already platform-dependent and the port follows the portable source; the connection manager itself, as the synchronous decision core of dcrd 2.2's `internal/connmgr` described under the 2.2 connmgr rewrite below (dcrd's `Connect`/`AddPersistent`/`Disconnect`/`Remove` gates, the exponential `backoffWithJitter` retries, and the 25-failure pause in the daemon's outbound driver), with the daemon executing the dials, closes and timers the core decides. The 2.2 campaign (upstream master 452c1a6c) ports the peer rework: the four-step handshake with the verack exchange inside it (inbound reads the version, writes its own, writes verack, then reads the remote verack; outbound writes first and reads the remote verack before writing its own), strict at the negotiated addrv2 version — any non-verack second message disconnects with `ErrNotVerAckMessage` — and tolerant below it (up to three non-verack messages are queued and replayed through the input handler after the handshake, dcrd's `delayedHandshakeMsgs`); the typed handshake error kinds from dcrd 2.2's new `peer/error.go`; the version callback fired from inside the version read after the peer state updates and before the too-old rejection, so the daemon's `OnVersion` rejections abort the handshake before any verack; the peer's network address as a `wire.NetAddressV2` (the local version message converts it to the legacy `addr_you` with Go's `To16` semantics — the mapped form for 4 encoded bytes, unchanged for 16, and all-zero bytes for any other length, notably a 32-byte Tor v3 key — and the proxy hiding compares the converted form); `MaxProtocolVersion` at the addrv2 version 12; the remote services zeroed until the version message arrives (pre-2.2 seeded the local services); and `PushAddrV2Msg` (copy, shuffle, and truncate past 1000, returning the list actually sent).  The daemon side: `serverPeer.OnVersion` reshaped to master (the services update still precedes every rejection; the mix-capable outbound preference now rejects — with dcrd's exact error text — where pre-2.2 disconnected and deliberately continued; the address advertisement, getaddr request, and good marking moved to the post-handshake add-peer admission the port's `on_connected` already modeled), wired for the first time into the live handshake through the serve hooks; the version-dispatched address push (`pushAddrMsg` choosing the legacy or v2 form by the negotiated version, both filtering peer-known addresses first); the `OnAddrV2` intake (no empty-list ban, a claimed type that does not match the canonical bytes bans with "sent invalid addrv2 message", the same ten-minute-future clamp); `natfSupported` dispatching the v2 type filter (with Tor v3) from the addrv2 version; the addrmgr↔wire-v2 address conversions; and the sendheaders request moved to dcrd master's post-handshake `Run` position — the port had never requested header announcements before, a real gap this closed.  `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is 12; the wire frame differential drives at 12 against the master-pinned oracle with a live `addrv2` row (the legacy `addr` row alone pins one below, the last version it encodes at), and the frozen getinfo/getnetworkinfo rows carry `protocolversion` 12. Go's nested `onClose` closures become explicit per-connection `ClosePlan`s; goroutines, contexts, and sockets are daemon concurrency (the node's outbound driver runs `targetOutboundHandler`'s fill loop with the 25-failure pause, per-entry `runPersistent` backoff ladders, and the accept-path admission shedding, all over one shared core, through whose methods alone the fill's reservations are taken and given back: `auto_outbound_acquire` takes both permits, `claim_outbound_candidate` registers the picked address's group, `auto_outbound_reserve` takes the host permit and the dial registration and unwinds any failure up to the registration, and the dial's `ClosePlan::auto_outbound` releases everything on the dial's failure or the connection's close; outside the core the semaphores and outbound groups can be read but not written directly; the handler's blocking `totalNormalConnsSem.Acquire` is modeled as a single parked waiter on `SemCount`, and a release hands it the permit directly and wakes the driver, as Go completes a blocked channel send inside the releasing receive, so a slot freed by an inbound close or a failed manual dial goes to outbound maintenance ahead of any `TryAcquire`; `pickOutboundAddr` draws each candidate from the address source outside the core's lock and claims it under the lock, as dcrd holds only the outbound groups' mutex there, so inbound admission never waits on the address manager). | `internal/rpcserver`, `rpc/jsonrpc/types`, `dcrjson` | `dcroxide-rpc`, `dcroxide-dcrjson`, `dcroxide-rpctypes` | vectors | Complete: `dcrjson/v4` — dcrd drives the whole package through Go reflection over registered command struct types, so the port makes the reflection data explicit as `GoType`/`GoValue` descriptor trees (struct fields with their `json`/`jsonrpcdefault`/`jsonrpcusage` tags, pointers-as-optionals, and Go's type display strings for the error messages) and reimplements Go `encoding/json` semantics over them: HTML-escaped strings (`<`/`>`/`&` to `<` etc. plus U+2028/9, and `\b`/`\f` by their names as `appendString` has written them since Go 1.22), Go's shortest-round-trip float formatting (an exact halfway tie between two shortest candidates keeps the even final digit, as Go 1.26.5's Dragonbox does, where Rust's own shortest digits round it away from zero; checked once against 3M Go-formatted float64/float32 values whose generator and corpus are not in the tree, with 90 Go-generated tie rows pinned in `crates/dcroxide-dcrjson/tests/review_go_text_parity.rs` and a kept generator with a committed corpus pending, ADR-0007's 2026-10-26 addendum) with the e-form cutoffs (`abs < 1e-6 \|\| >= 1e21` for JSON with the `e-0X` cleanup; decimal exponent `< -4 \|\| >= 6` for `%v` in usage/help text), bytewise-sorted map keys, declaration-order struct fields with `omitempty`, `[]byte` as base64, and a Go-faithful JSON validator/decoder producing `json.UnmarshalTypeError` values (`number 3.14`, leaf types from nested failures) and scanner syntax messages (`unexpected end of JSON input`, `invalid character 'x' ...` with the byte quoted as `quoteChar` quotes it, and the `invalid character ' ' ...` that input cut off inside a number, literal, string escape or `\u` escape reports through `scanner.eof`'s synthetic space) byte for byte, as Go 1.26's v1 `encoding/json` produces them (the Go release dcrd's release image builds with, 1.26.2, rather than the module's `go` line: Go 1.27's default `GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2` words the syntax errors differently -- a control character in a string is `in string` rather than `in string literal`, a token cut short is `unexpected end of JSON input` rather than the synthetic-space `invalid character ' '`, and a high byte is quoted `'\xff'` -- while the `UnmarshalTypeError` `Type`/`Value` pair the parameter type errors are built from is unchanged; the pin-bump procedure above says what a move to 1.27 entails); struct keys fold as `foldName` does (U+017F and U+212A match `s` and `k`).  On that base: the registry (`Register` with the full validation gauntlet and its error strings, usage-flag battery incl. the `String()` rendering with unknown-bit hex tails, `(dynamic type, string)` method keying so a plain-string lookup of a named-type method correctly misses, `RegisteredMethods` filtering), `MarshalCmd` (positional params cut at the first nil optional), `ParseParams` (defaults populated only for absent trailing params — an explicit `null` leaves the field nil with no default, pinned), `NewCmd` with the whole `assignField` conversion matrix (numeric widths with Go's overflow checks, `ParseInt` base-0 semantics incl. hex, `ParseBool`/`ParseFloat` acceptance, string-to-compound via JSON), `MethodUsageText` (incl. the English plural-to-singular squashing that turns `names` into `"nam"`), `GenerateHelp` over result-type descriptors with a port of Go's `text/tabwriter` column-block algorithm (per-block widths, trailing cells unpadded — the help output incl. the misaligned map-entry lines matches byte for byte), the JSON-RPC `Request`/`Response`/`RPCError` marshalling with all the error-code constants, and the concatenated-hashes helpers.
